### The install (Faber)

**Status:** in code, 2026-11-15 (Faber, 27b815a), not yet seen in play. The self-test and the blockout wait on the screen. Time: First Strike about 30 minutes, my estimate. The install about 1 hour 5 minutes, from the session's record (12:18 to 13:21 UTC).
- **Implemented: First Strike as data** (`docs/roster.md`: "her first hit on a new target always crits").
  - **A fourth kind of passive, `Opener`** (`Definitions.Passives`). Its Share is the critical chance on the first hit to land on a target. Kirra's is 1, so it always crits, past the critical's cap. Tuned live: `Passives.kirra.Share`.
  - **The rule:** `Rules.Passive.Critical` gives that floor. `Rules.Hit`'s effects take it as `Critical`, a floor under the hit's chance.
  - **In the fight** (`FightService`): each fighter remembers, by Id, the fighters whose hit has landed on it.
  - **My calls, tier 1:**
    - A dodged swing hasn't landed, so the next one is still the first.
    - A Shade that walks home at full health is fought anew, so the first hit crits again.
    - No cue of its own: the critical's own number is the cue.
- **Tested.**
  - The type check: 0 new. The logic: 5382 passing offline.
  - A new passive case covers the row, the rule's edges, a critical on the worst roll past the cap against a defender no hit crits on, a dodged swing dealing nothing, and a floor below one rolling as a chance.
  - The row check takes the new kind.
- **Implemented: the install**, 2026-11-15.
  - **The model:** the sixteen meshes and three maps are under the group, Approved (`docs/asset-log.md`). They're assembled from `tools/studio/Models/Kirra.json` into `src/storage/Kirra.rbxm`: no glow, and every piece casts its shadow.
  - **The clips:** all eight are saved in the place (`NL_Kirra<Clip>`).
    - Her tail layers play over the open set's Hit, Down and Up, one priority above (`Config.Animations.Layers`). Down's holds its last frame while she lies down.
    - Author now keeps a held part at its own weight when the Torso is at 0 only to carry keys, so the scarf moves in her layers.
  - **Her kit, on her row:** `KirraCombo` (Dash, Hit1, Hit2, Hit3, Sheathe) and `KirraScarfStep` (Blink, Land, Cut). Scarf Step's rows are taken as delivered (`Config.EffectRows`).
  - **Moves, new:** an action can carry its fighter on a beat (`Actions.Moves`), to the point `Rules.Fight.MoveEnd` gives.
    - A glide when the move has Seconds. The dash ends 3.5 studs short of the target, after 4 studs at most.
    - A blink at 0 Seconds. Scarf Step lands 3.8 studs past the target, facing back. It stops short of a wall and lands only on ground.
    - The replay starts each press of a move from the same spot.
  - **My calls, tier 1:**
    - One combo clip, played from 0 each round. The dash covers whatever gap is left, so a round started up close steps in place (section 3's question).
    - Scarf Step stays on take A (the Blink's `Turn` 0) until the blockout. Take B is `Turn` 44.
  - **For research, tier 2:**
    - The kit's coefficients are seeds: the combo at Rook's combo's rate per second (1.78), the signature on Haymaker's numbers.
    - Her reveal's strike lands on Land (`Config.Reveal.Strikes`).
- **Tested:**
  - Type check: 0 new.
  - Logic: 5814 passing offline. They cover the move rule, each layer over its rig's reaction, each hit reaching from where the moves leave her, and her reveal's strike.
  - Two self-test cases are written but not yet run:
    - the moves, through the replay, to MoveEnd's point, facing the target and in reach of the next hit;
    - First Strike, her first hit on a Shade.
  - Studio has the code (its `Source` searched).
- **Not done:**
  - the whole self-test;
  - Work's post: no Voidkin's work clip plays in a bay yet;
  - Scarf Step's blockout with Modulo, A beside B (section 5), with take A's check at 64 px;
  - TR14's verdict in motion;
  - research's verdict at phone size.
- **Visually reviewed:** no. **Director-accepted:** no.

### 5. Scarf Step's look: the parts and their techniques (Modulo, 2026-11-14)

`/ga-action` step 1, for research's technique check before any block-in. A signature, named as one: it gets the time it needs, and `ga-fresh-eyes` reads it at the blockout and the final (the checks above).
- **The beats it hangs on,** from the clip (60 fps): **Blink** 13 (0.22 s), **Land** 16, **Cut** 18 (0.3 s). The scarf holds straight 16 to 40, lets go from its tip at 44, and has settled by 70.
- **The hero shape, the line:** the scarf itself, which the clip lays straight from her knot back through the Shade, and a **Beam** of light along it. A Beam because the line moves along itself (AR8's rule: anything that scrolls or burns along a shape).
  - **Its direction:** from where she was to where she is. It's there as she lands (16), and it burns off toward her (38 to 46), its far end running up the line into her knot, slow, then fast (the acceleration). The light goes first and the cloth after, since the scarf lets go at 44.
  - **Its beat:** thin as she lands (0.3 stud), it swells on the Cut (0.75 in two frames), eases back by 26, and thins as it burns. The swell lands the cut on the line as well as on the Shade.
  - **In rows, as built:** one Beam, `Line`, on her `HumanoidRootPart`. Its `At` is her knot and its `Tilt` is the scarf's aim, both in her root's frame and both measured from the delivered clip, so it stays on the scarf wherever the game lands her. `Top` is the knot to the tip (9.95 studs), held, and then it runs into her knot from 38 to 46 (Quad In): that's the burn. `Taper` 0.45 narrows it toward where she was. The aim holds from 40, so the light stays straight as the cloth lets go off it.
  - **What changed from the check, tier 1:** there's no draw and no `Start`, `Head` or `Knot` rows. An end held on the effect's foot leaves the light off the scarf whenever she doesn't start 5 studs out, and a row can only hang on a Beam's end, so the burn rides `Top`. The 3 frames of draw were the blink anyway (the check).
- **The blink-out** (13): a small puff at her feet where she was, kicked along the line. Particles. It's kept quiet, so the line is what you see her go by.
- **The slide** (16 to 30): low dust at her feet as she brakes, sprayed along her slide. Particles, on her root set down to the ground.
- **The crescent on the way out** (Cut 18): the kit's `FxCrescent` on the Shade's chest, laid along the cut, sweeping head first as it grows and fading by 32 (0.23 s). A **mesh**, AR8's rule for a shape with a near and a far side. Its Glow peaks on the Cut and goes out with it.
- **The sparks** (18): a burst of `fx_spark` off the crescent, thrown along her line, away from where she was. Particles.
- **The light** (18 to 34): a PointLight on the cut, lit at once and eased out.
- **Not rows:** the hit at rung 7 (the hold, the flash, the tremble and the trauma come from AR8's ladder on the Cut's hit), and the caster's light (AR9's waits).
- **The skewer, measured before the block** (`tools/blender/kirra_scarf_step_fx.py screen`). The measure casts rays from cameras every 5 degrees round the fight. It tests them against the Shade's parts at rest, as boxes from its recipe. "Hidden" is the line going into the body and out of it, the skewer's read.
  - **The replay's camera stands on her left.** `Config.Combat` puts the lead beyond its target, turned 45 degrees, and the camera behind the avatar. That's heading 215 round the Shade. The clip's review camera stood on her right, at 28.
  - **A, her path through the Shade,** hides 4.9 of the line's 9.4 studs from the replay's camera. It hides some of the line from every heading.
  - **The B first delivered is withdrawn.** It was the light turned 24.9 degrees to her right, 1.48 studs off the Shade's middle. It passed on the far side and through the Shade's arm, hiding 7.2 studs: the Shade is 2.48 across each side with its arms, not the coat's 1.1. 9ba7d65 delivered it; nothing installed it.
  - **Turning the light alone** needs 40 degrees toward the camera to hide none from the replay. It also leaves the cloth through the Shade.
  - **B is now her path turned at her spot,** past the Shade on the camera's side, with the cloth and the light together. At 3.5 studs off the Shade's middle (44 degrees from a 5-stud spot, 1 stud clear of its arm), the replay's camera sees none of the line hidden and none drawn over the body. 56 of 72 headings hide none.
  - The other 16 headings look from beyond the Shade back at her spot, and her spot itself is behind the Shade from there. `Lead.Frame`'s 45 degrees keeps the play camera just outside them.
  - The line and the scarf ride her root, so B needs no clip re-export.
- **The block contains,** in grey (`Grey` true, `Action` `KirraScarfStep`, `Free` `Cut`), seven rows in one file for both takes, `build/handoff/Kirra/fx/KirraScarfStep.rows.json`: `Line`, the puff, the slide, `CutAt` (the Shade's chest), the crescent, the sparks and the light.
  - On A's path the line passes 0.04 studs off the Shade's middle at 3.12 up (its chest 3.0), 3.3 studs from her knot.
  - The crescent rolls its face 35 degrees toward the camera's side: her left in the replay. The keeper's ring flips it.
  - Written and checked by `tools/blender/kirra_scarf_step_fx.py rows` (plain `py`, from the clip). Its `preview` mode draws the line and the crescent from the replay's camera at 64 px in Blender. It waits for memory: Studio holds 3.6 GB.
- **Waits for the build:**
  - **Her blue** (`#47A6F5`), off red as the content check asks. The grey ramps get the blue by the rows' `Color` first, and a blue ramp is drawn only if the core needs its own hue (my call, tier 1).
  - AR9's drawn puff and spark, once Faber's kit rows take the sheets.
  - The sound (Faber's, one library cue). Anything new to upload.
- **For Faber, at the blockout** (his calls):
  1. **How the game moves her** at Blink. The clip has her land 3.8 studs past the Shade, facing back along her path. A is that path through the Shade.
     - B is her path turned 44 degrees at her spot, passing the Shade 3.5 studs off its middle (a seed, to tune in the replay).
     - The turn goes toward the camera's side of the line from her to the target. The server doesn't see the camera, but the avatar's side of that line is the camera's side, since the camera stands behind the avatar.
     - The rows don't depend on how she's moved.
  2. **The Shade's place.** The crescent, the sparks and the light are written 5 studs ahead of the effect's foot, the clip's distance. That holds if Scarf Step always starts 5 studs from its target and she faces it at the start.
     - On B, the effect faces where she faces at the start. If she turns to her path first, the crescent lies along her line, and the cut's rows need to ride the target, as `Body` rides the attacker: his code.
  3. **`Free` on the Cut,** so the hit's hold never stills the crescent. It also runs the line's tracks through the hold while her clip is frozen, so the burn runs about 8 frames ahead of the cloth. The light goes first either way.
  4. **The install order:** `rows_take` checks the action and its beats, so `KirraScarfStep` with Blink, Land and Cut goes in first. A and B are one effect. The take is her path, 0 or 3.5 studs off.
  5. **The crescent's roll** follows the camera's side: `Turn` Z +35 with the camera on her left (the replay), -35 on her right (the keeper's ring).
- **Content check, 2026-11-14: PASS for the grey block** (the dev build, nothing uploaded), on the numbers alone, with no render yet. Its conditions:
  - A reaches a public build only if the 64 px replay shows no read of a body run through, from any heading. The lean is to B.
  - B must clear the body on screen, not just in plan: 1.48 studs is 0.38 past the coat's edge. If it overlaps at 64 px, the offset grows or the clip's aim moves to the flank.
  - The skewer verdict counts once the cut's rows ride the target.
  - The crescent stays light and short-lived.
  - A review sheet of the chosen take, the line alone and then with the crescent, from the player's camera at full size and at 64 px on dark, before the finish or any upload. A new drawn sheet or ramp is checked again.
  - B's condition is answered by the measure above: B is now her path, and the replay's camera sees none of the line on the body.
- **Time:** the rows took about 25 minutes after the check, against 45 forecast. The skewer's measure, B's correction and A's roll took about 50 more, before anything was installed. The blockout with Faber in the replay is still about an hour, once Studio is back.
- **Four facts:**
  - **Made:** the rows are made, one file for both takes.
  - **Self-checked:** the file's own checks, the line measured from the clip, and the skewer measured from cameras round the fight.
  - **Not drawn:** the Blender preview waits for memory.
  - **Delivered:** to the handoff.
  - **Not accepted:** that's the blockout's verdict.
- **Research's technique check, 2026-11-14, night: passed, build the grey rows.**
  - **Each part has the right technique.** The line is a Beam, which is right because it runs along itself and has no depth. The crescent is a mesh, for its near and far side. The puff, the dust and the sparks are particles, and the cut gets a light. No shape with depth sits on a card.
  - **The hero is right: one line, with a direction and an acceleration.** The burn-off carries the acceleration, slow then fast into her knot. The draw is 3 frames, so it reads as the blink itself, not as a draw, and that's fine.
  - **Motion before layers, in the block:** judge the line alone first in the replay at full speed, then add the crescent, then the rest. A layer that doesn't add to the line's read at 64 px comes out.
  - **The line stays the brightest thing.** The crescent's Glow peaks below the line's swell on the Cut, so the eye goes to the line and the crescent marks where it cut.
  - **The skewer, tried in the first block, not after it:** two takes in the replay, A on the axis and B with `Start` off the axis past the Shade's flank. Both are judged at 64 px from the player's camera. If either reads as a skewer, take the flank, and the clip's aim follows (your re-export).
  - **For Faber's question 2, research's view:** auto-combat doesn't hold 5 studs, so the crescent, the sparks and the light should ride the target, as `Body` rides the attacker. How is Faber's code.
  - **Research, on the measured B: accepted.** Turning her path, not just the light, is the better lever. It keeps the cloth and the light together, and it beats my B by measure: none of the line hidden from the replay's camera. A stays in the block as the take it's judged against.

## AR11. Watt (Modulo, then Faber)

Research's request, 2026-11-14, night. **Designed again, 2026-11-15, night,** on the director's words (`docs/decisions.md`: the mechanic was "sooooo corny ... it need to look like real anime"; then "A" for the body).
- Ground 1's next Voidkin. His row in `docs/roster.md`: Common, Endurance, Tank, Storm, Zone 1. The look rules are in the roster's "The look".
- He matters more than his rarity says. He's the guaranteed second Voidkin and the first worker, dropped by the first enemy as a Named shard (D01).
- So he's the first reveal a player hasn't chosen, the first worker in a bay, and the first Tank anyone leads.

**Feel line:** the first Voidkin you didn't choose, and you're glad of him. A young man with presence, known at 64 px by his hair and the weapon he plants, who walks into a pack unhurried while the street locks around him.

**Signature feel line, Ground Wire** (its function unchanged): he drives his weapon into the street, and a ring of Storm's light snaps out along the ground. The Shades around him lock mid-step while he stands behind it, shielded and humming.

**What stays:**
- his row's numbers, Ground Wire's function, Insulated, and his bay aptitude;
- Storm's colours, light pink to white, with the effect conditions in section 2.

**What goes:** the whole look. Nothing of the first model is kept; sections 1 and 2 are its record.

**Delivers,** each shown before the next:
1. **Three takes,** for research's pick.
   - **How they're drawn:**
     - in grey on the R6 body, with Storm's accent only;
     - front and three-quarter from the game's camera, and at 64 px over noon and night;
     - each face drawn close up;
     - quick blockouts: hair, clothes and the weapon as rough meshes, never finished.
   - Each is a different character from an anime's cast, inside a Common's limit (three or four elements, one accent):
     - **A, the quiet wall:** tall and calm. A long dark coat with a standing collar up to his nose, its sleeves pushed to the elbows. Heavy dark hair swept back, its ends lifting in the static, pale pink at the tips. A black iron spear-staff with three white ceramic discs under a short blade, slung across his back. Half-lidded, steady eyes.
     - **B, the hothead:** a cropped jacket with its sleeves tied around his waist, a sleeveless high-necked top, fingerless gloves. Hair spiked up and back over one shaved side, with one pale pink lock. A slab of dark steel on his forearm, with a spike at its foot that he drives into the street. A grin.
     - **C, the sleepy one:** an oversized hoodie with the hood down, and wide trousers. A soft mop of hair that floats up with static. A long iron stake taller than him, carried over his shoulder, wrapped in cloth tape with one white ceramic ring. He plants it and leans on it. Eyes half asleep.
   - At 64 px, his outline parts from every lead before him.
   - **The pick:** research picks one at the verdict, with `ga-fresh-eyes`, judged against the bar of a real anime's cast. The pick goes to the director as a notice, not a gate, and the model starts on it unless the director says otherwise.
2. **The model** on R6, from the picked take, at the player's height: the hair, the clothes, the face, the weapon, and the places an effect starts (the weapon's head, the hair's tips).
3. **The clips,** as Pip's set:
   - Idle;
   - Combo: heavy and short, the Tank's way (`docs/design.md`);
   - Ground Wire;
   - Hit, Down and Up;
   - Reveal: the first Voidkin a player didn't choose, so it lands his character in one beat;
   - Skip, only if his fight needs it;
   - Work: the bay loop players see most early. It shows his own way of making power, never a trade's.
4. **Ground Wire's look,** built in 3D from the kit (AR8, "The method") with the builder in the same session (`/ga-action`).
   - One hero shape: the ring of light along the ground, with a direction (out from the weapon) and an acceleration.
   - The shield behind it.
   - The locked Shades read as stopped, never hurt.

**Waits:**
- his install;
- Insulated, and Ground Wire's stop and shield, as data (Faber's);
- his sounds;
- any colour beyond Storm's, until the palette's pick (the director to Modulo, 19:09).

**Checks:**
- the content check on the takes and before every upload;
- research's verdict on the takes, the clips and the final in motion;
- `ga-fresh-eyes` on the takes, and on Ground Wire at its blockout and final.

**Time it:** each delivery records what it took. The takes: 1 to 2 hours, forecast.

**Status:** the model is delivered, 2026-11-15, 20:30 (Modulo): take A with research's three fixes, baked on R6, in `build/handoff/Watt/` (delivery 3, below). For Faber's install, and research's verdict in the game.
- The director rejected the first design (sections 1 and 2), and nothing of it is kept. Its files have left `build/handoff/Watt/` (the visor, the wrench and its sheet removed, the rest replaced; they stay in git, 1c9dda6).
- **Next:** Faber's install; research's verdict in the game from the player's camera at phone size; then his clips and Ground Wire's look (Modulo's, after AR14's level 1).
- **Delivery 1, the three takes** (Modulo, 2026-11-15, night): `build/handoff/Watt/watt_takes.png`, for research's pick. In grey on R6, Storm's pale pink the only colour. Each take: three-quarter front, the game's camera, its face close, its outline; then all three at 64 px over noon, night and in black, beside Rook, Kirra, Pip and Lyric.
  - **A, the quiet wall,** as briefed. The pink is on the hair's lifted tips, where an effect starts. The spear's blade and discs stand over his right shoulder, the side the game's camera is on.
  - **B, the hothead,** as briefed. Mine: the slab is on his left forearm, since Rook's ram is on the right, and the pink lock falls over his right brow.
  - **C, the sleepy one,** as briefed. Mine: the pink is the hood's lining, seen from the game's camera, and its strings, seen from the front. The stake is planted at his right side with his hand on the tape. R6's arm doesn't bend, so the lean is a held arm, a tilted head and 4 degrees of his weight.
  - **Measured at 64 px** (how much two outlines cover the same pixels, 1 the same; from the game's camera, then the front):
    - A against Rook 0.75 and 0.67, Kirra 0.33 and 0.64, Pip 0.65 and 0.68, Lyric 0.70 and 0.67;
    - B against Rook 0.72 and 0.65, Kirra 0.34 and 0.63, Pip 0.61 and 0.67, Lyric 0.69 and 0.59;
    - C against Rook 0.76 and 0.65, Kirra 0.34 and 0.57, Pip 0.64 and 0.67, Lyric 0.69 and 0.66.
    - For scale, leads already made, from the game's camera: Pip against Rook 0.71, Lyric 0.63, Kirra 0.37. So each take parts from the leads about as far as they part from each other, and no further: on R6 the body is most of an outline, and hair and a weapon move it by a few hundredths.
    - The takes against each other, from the game's camera: A and B 0.80, A and C 0.73, B and C 0.72.
  - **Seen at 64 px, the maker's eye and not a verdict:** A is read by the spear's head and the pink tips; his coat is drawn dark, so over night he holds by the tips, the discs and his forearms, and his real colours will decide that. B is read by his hair; the slab is an edge at his far side from the game's camera, with its spike under it. C's stake stands apart from him and his pale mop is the lightest thing on him; the stake's iron is lost over night but for its ring.
  - **How it's made:** `tools/blender/watt_takes.py`, Workbench, no GPU turn; the leads by their own sheets' builders. One round of fixes on the maker's own read: A's coat longer, B's lock slimmer and his feet apart, C's stake clear of his face and his mop in soft lobes.
  - **Checks:** the content check (`ga-content-check`, on the sheet at full view and its 64 px rows): **PASS for all three, as a review sheet**, likeness judged from memory. Rules 2 and 4 and the names pass. Rule 3: no take would make a viewer name one published character; each is a genre type, and these conditions keep the model on its own side:
    - **A:** the hair stays dark, short and swept back with hard tips, never silver, white or pale blond, never long and straight. Both eyes show and match: no covered eye, headband, scar or marks by the mouth. The collar stays the coat's own, never a cloth mask, bandage or scarf. The coat stays long and open, with no cloud or emblem pattern and no school jacket's buttons. The weapon stays a spear with its three ceramic discs, never a broad cleaver. Ground Wire stays a planted weapon with a ring on the ground, in pale pink: never lightning held in the hand, never a thrust, never blue-white.
    - **B:** the hair stays dark and asymmetric, one side shaved only, never ash-blond or red, never a symmetric flame with a widow's peak. The slab stays one flat rectangular plate on one arm, never paired, rounded or like a grenade. No X or V mark and no orange on the top, no shark teeth, no scar. No explosions. The pink lock stays pale and hard-edged and ends above the brow, or lifts with the other spikes, so it never reads as a trickle down the forehead at 64 px over night (rule 4).
    - **C:** the hair gets a colour of its own, not white or silver-blue, and stays soft and lobed: it never turns to sharp white spikes when the signature fires. Static shows as thin pink wisps and the ground ring, never a blue-white aura around his body. The stake stays in every pose, with its tape and ceramic ring. The pink lining and strings stay at Storm's pale end: if a palette pick darkens or saturates the accent, it moves off the neck (rule 4).
    - **Whichever is picked:** the blade, spike or stake goes into the street only, never into or through a body, and locked Shades read as stopped, never hurt. The weapon never points at or passes his own head, neck or torso: A's spear is slung so its head clears his head and neck with a gap seen at 64 px from every heading (on the sheet its discs sit against the back of his head from the game's camera), and B's spike stays clear of his own leg and foot. The clips are stepped through from the side and three-quarter, with no held straight raised arm. The model, the clips and Ground Wire's look each get their own check in colour before any upload.
  - **Time:** by the clock, 19:19 to 19:30: the script, two renders, one round of fixes and the content check; the clock reads short against the work. Forecast: 1 to 2 hours.
  - **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes: the sheet whole and the 64 px block at three times its size. Delivered, yes, for research's pick. Accepted, no.
  - **Research's verdict** (2026-11-15, 19:40; the sheet whole and its 64 px rows. Fresh eyes read it blind beside and picked the same take.)
    - **Felt:**
      - **A** is a believable side character, but his face is hidden and he's black on black. Over night he's his pink tips and nothing else, and his slung spear says carried, not planted.
      - **B** is a grin and a gauntlet, the closest of the three to the Roblox avatar the director rejected. His slab is lost in his arm's outline.
      - **C** is someone: a half-asleep boy leaning on an iron stake taller than he is, unhurried before he moves.
    - **Tier:** C competent, near exceptional. A competent. B works.
    - **The one bold thing:** C's stake, planted and taller than him. It's the one upright line in the row beside Rook, Kirra, Pip and Lyric, it reads at 64 px over noon, over night and in black, and it is already Ground Wire: the weapon he plants, as the feel line says.
    - **The pick: C, the sleepy one.** He wears clothes, not a costume. His face has a mood, he carries one weapon, and his element is in his body, the hair the static lifts, not in a prop. A Common's weight: four elements and one accent.
    - **Fixes for the model** (the content check's conditions for C stand):
      1. **The hair, his identity, becomes one deliberate cut.** Today it's a cloud with spikes stuck on top, read as a cauliflower or a wig: the one thing that tips him toward corny. Make it soft clumps that taper, falling forward over the half-lidded eyes in one sweep, the crown lifted by the static in a few strands, with no spikes set on top. Its colour is his own, not white or silver-blue, at a middle value, so it reads over noon's cream as well as at night. Today's pale grey sinks into the noon ground.
      2. **Storm's pink, seen from the game's camera.** Today it's a thin ring at the collar. Make the hood larger and lay it down on his back, its lining open to the camera.
      3. **The stake over night.** Today only its ring survives. Pale tape keeps its line at night too.
      4. **The hoodie, an anime character's, not an avatar's.** An oversized hoodie is the commonest avatar there is. The sleeves fall past his hands and the hem hangs below the torso: two meshes, no new proportion.
    - **Then:** the model in colour, at the bar. The clips come next: the lean is his idle, and Work is his own way of making power. Then Ground Wire's look. The pick goes to the director as a notice, not a gate.
  - **The director's pick: A** (to Modulo, 2026-11-15, 19:31, the takes' sheet on screen): "def A, but looks at vanguard units then compare". Two A's were open, the takes and the palette, so Modulo asked which: "Watt's take A". Then, on the comparison below: "yess see the difference".
    - **Covers:** Watt is take A, the quiet wall, which replaces research's pick of C above; A is compared with Anime Vanguards' units; the difference shown is seen. It names no change of design, and it doesn't pick the palette.
  - **The comparison** (Modulo, 19:32 to 19:38, `ga-reference-study`). The question: how are Anime Vanguards' low-rarity units built on Roblox's blocky body, and where does take A fall short of them?
    - **Looked at:** 18 Rare and Epic unit pictures and 6 of the top rarities, from a public fan wiki, in `build/reference/anime-vanguards/` (gitignored, study only): `sheet_low.png`, and A beside five of them at one height in `compare_bodies.png` and `compare_heads.png`. Ratios are by eye on those pictures, not measured.
    - **OBSERVED:**
      1. The body is the plain blocky body at its own size. Nothing built on it thickens the torso or a limb.
      2. The clothes are painted on the blocks, cel-shaded in two or three tones, with drawn folds, seams, fastenings and trims. A built piece appears only where the outline needs one: a hat, a band, a weapon.
      3. The hair is the one sculpted thing: a mass of curved, tapering locks about one and a half to nearly two heads wide, rising over half a head above the crown, with a fringe to the brows, shaded from root to tip.
      4. The face is painted on a rounded head: eyes with coloured irises under a heavy upper line, thin brows, a nose's shadow, a short mouth.
      5. Each has two or three large clear colour areas, light against dark, so it reads from far.
      6. The rarities above add built layers: hats, coats, armour, light, particles.
    - **Take A against them:** hair of straight cones on a cap, about one head wide; a face of two slabs, half of it under the collar; a coat built thick, so the body is a wider black box with nothing drawn on it; one dark value from collar to boots. What A has that they don't: a weapon in his outline, and a coat with a cut. Research's read of A says the same of the face and the black.
    - **DESIGN, for A's model** (the way of building is taken; no unit's design, face, outfit or picture is):
      1. **The body stays R6's own size.** The coat is painted on the torso and arms in our own cel shading and ink line; only its collar, cuffs and tails are built, thin.
      2. **The hair is sculpted:** curved tapering locks swept back, half again as wide as his head, with a fringe, the tips lifting in Storm's pink. It stays dark, short and hard-tipped (the content check).
      3. **The face is painted:** half-lidded eyes with irises, drawn lids and brows, large enough to read from the game's camera. The collar stops under his nose and stays the coat's own.
      4. **Three values at 64 px:** the coat a dark colour of its own, never black; an inner layer and his forearms light; the trousers between.
      5. **The spear** is thin and slung clear of his head and neck (the content check's condition).
    - **Colour:** the brief's wait, "any colour beyond Storm's, until the palette's pick", ends with the pick in AR12 (A, Eclipse: a neutral UI, so a Voidkin carries its own colour).
    - **Four facts:** the comparison made and shown to the director, who saw the difference. The model: not started.
  - **Delivery 2, take A in colour** (Modulo, 2026-11-15, 19:38 to 20:00 by the clock): `build/handoff/Watt/watt_colour.png`, for research's verdict before the bake. The director, mid-build: "it need colors too." It covers this build: in colour, not grey.
    - **On the sheet:** the front, the three-quarter front, his right side and the game's camera; his face close; the grey take's face and body beside; his outline; his colours; then 64 px over noon and night from four headings, beside the grey take and Rook, Kirra, Pip and Lyric.
    - **Built as the comparison's five points say:**
      1. **The body** is R6's six blocks at their own size. The coat with its shade and light, the shirt, the belt, three ceramic toggles, the strap and the trousers are flat shapes painted a layer off the blocks' faces. Built: the collar, the rolled cuffs, the tape on his left forearm, an iron band on his right, the boots' soles.
      2. **The hair** is 25 flat curved locks on a cap. Seven sweep up off his brow and back over the crown, and the middle four flick up behind it in Storm's pink. Five lie down the back of his head, the sides turn out, and the fringe is parted over his right brow, one long lock down the bridge of his nose.
      3. **The face** is painted on the head: half-lidded eyes with Storm's irises, lids, brows, the fringe's shadow, a nose and a short mouth. The collar stops at his jaw, notched in front, so his whole face shows.
      4. **Three values:** the coat a deep petrol; the shirt, the tape and the discs pale; the trousers between. The boots are near ink.
      5. **The spear** is thin and taller than he is: 7.7 studs to his 6.04. It runs from behind his left calf to over his right shoulder and leans back, so its discs and blade stand above his hair and outside it.
    - **His colours** (all in the script's `PALETTE`): Coat `#1F4954`, Storm `#FFB3E8`, Shirt `#EFE9DE`, Trouser `#B4AEA2`, Leather `#A9683A`, Hair `#232738` with its light `#4A5478`, Skin `#F3CFB1`. One accent, Storm's pale pink: the hair's lifted ends, his eyes, and the coat's lining where it turns out (cuffs, edges, vent, collar).
    - **Measured:** 4.8 x 2.27 x 7.71 studs with the spear, 4091 triangles. At 64 px from the game's camera, his outline against Rook 0.74, Kirra 0.33, Pip 0.65, Lyric 0.68 (the grey take: 0.75, 0.33, 0.65, 0.70).
    - **Seen, the maker's eye and not a verdict:**
      - From the front and the three-quarter he reads as a character: a face with a mood, a mass of hair, a coat with a cut. Over noon and over night he holds by the petrol coat, the pink cuffs and his pale forearms.
      - From the game's camera the hair is a dark mass with a rim of pink points: less of a shape than from the front.
      - From the front the lifted ends stand in a row, which may read as set on.
      - Over night the iron shaft is lost and the discs and blade float.
      - In the render the pink reads a little deeper than its chip, under Workbench's shading.
    - **How it's made:** `tools/blender/watt_sheet.py`, written again for this design, Workbench, no GPU turn. It runs in stages (`new`, `old`, a lead a run, `sheet`): with Studio open the PC's memory was short, and the guard stopped four runs before the split. Four rounds on the maker's own read and the check: the hair from fat straight locks to curved ones; the head smoothed, the eyes larger, the collar lowered from a mask's height to his jaw; the pink ends flicked up for the game's camera; the spear moved. The first design's `tools/blender/watt_model.py` is removed, since it built on the first sheet's shapes. The baker is written again at the bake.
    - **Checks:** the content check (`ga-content-check`, the sheet whole, its parts and the 64 px rows at twice their size; likeness from memory).
      - **First look: BLOCK, on one condition.** The spear's head sat against the back of his head from the side, and its discs touched the collar from the game's camera. The other five conditions for A, rule 3 in colour, rule 4's pinks, rule 2 and the names passed.
      - **Second look, the spear moved and four 64 px headings added: PASS, as a review sheet.** The discs and blade keep ground from his head, hair and collar from all four headings, noon and night. Only the blunt shaft lies on his back. The hair's row of pink ends changes neither read.
      - **Its conditions, added to the ones above:**
        - The accent stays at `#FFB3E8` or paler in game, in hard-edged bands and wedges, never a drip or fleck below the collar or the vent. The tape stays clean.
        - The pink ends stay small pale tips on a rounded dark mass: never a magenta rim on large star-shaped points, and no blond or yellow in the fringe.
        - The toggles stay pale, never gold or brass, never a centre row. The coat never closes to the collar and never goes navy or black.
        - The fringe's shadow stays soft and symmetric. The collar never rises over his mouth or nose in a clip.
        - In every clip, the draw and the return included, the blade and discs never pass his head or neck, even for a frame. The butt stays a blunt cap.
        - This PASS covers the sheet, not an upload.
    - **Not yet:** the bake on R6 (the atlas, the meshes, the recipe) under the Blender lock; his clips; Ground Wire's look.
    - **Time:** by the clock, 19:38 to 20:00: the script written again, four rounds, the leads' runs and two looks from the content check. The clock reads short against the work. Forecast for the model: 2 to 3 hours.
    - **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes: the sheet whole, the face close, the head from the game's camera and the side enlarged, the 64 px rows at twice their size. Delivered, yes, for research's verdict. Accepted, no.
    - **Research's verdict** (2026-11-15, 20:10; the sheet whole, the game's camera at its size and the 64 px rows at three times. Fresh eyes read it blind beside.)
      - **Felt:** from the front, a quiet anime side character: the stoic face, pink-eyed, over a collar lined in pink. The jump from the grey take is real, and the comparison worked. From the game's camera, where players see him, he's a petrol slab with a dark mop and a thin line across it: nothing on him there is his own yet. At 64 px the eye lands on the spear's white discs, not his hair.
      - **Tier:** competent. At the bar from the front, not yet from the game's camera.
      - **The one bold thing:** the face. Keep it exactly as it is.
      - **Fixes, into the bake** (the content check's conditions hold):
        1. **The hair, his identity from behind.** From the game's camera it's a dark mass the size of his head, and from the front its pink ends stand on top like cones set on. Make the swept locks fan back and out past the head's box, their upper planes in the hair's light, so the mass has a direction from behind. The pink is the last part of each lock, curving back with it, the ends varied in length: small pale tips on a dark mass, never a row of points.
        2. **Something between dark hair and dark coat.** The hair and the coat are both dark, so from behind his top half is one mass, and over night it sinks. Show the collar's pink lining from behind, so a pale band parts the head from the body. Open the back's vent on the lining from the waist to the hem, so the coat's back, the most-seen area on him, isn't a flat rectangle. The cuffs narrow to a turned edge, so the pink still adds up to one accent.
        3. **The sling and the spear.** By colour he blurs with Kirra beside him: dark hair, a dark blue body and a brown strap across it. The sling goes pale, the shirt's colour, so a light line crosses his back from the game's camera, and it leaves Kirra's brown. The shaft takes a pale grip wrap and a pale butt cap, so it reads as a line at night and the discs stop floating. Planted in his idle, as Modulo plans.
      - **No second sheet.** The baked model's verdict, in the game from the player's camera at phone size, covers these fixes, in one light, the game's own (the director's words of the same night). Show the game's camera and 64 px row before the atlas only if the hair's shape leaves you in doubt.
  - **Delivery 3, the model on R6** (Modulo, 2026-11-15, 20:10 to 20:30 by the clock): `build/handoff/Watt/`, for Faber's install and research's verdict in the game.
    - **The files:**
      - seven meshes, `Watt_<Piece>.json`: Torso, Head, RightArm, LeftArm, RightLeg, LeftLeg and Spear;
      - four maps at 1024 px: `Watt_Color.png`, `Watt_Roughness.png`, `Watt_Metalness.png`, `Watt_Emissive.png`;
      - `Watt.recipe.json`: the pieces, the spear's joint, the glow and the points;
      - `watt_model_sheet.png`, the baked model in Lantern Row's dusk, and `watt_atlas_check.png`, the maps as uploads.
    - **Research's three fixes, as built:**
      1. **The hair.** Seven swept locks fan back and out past the head's box, their upper planes in the hair's light, uneven in length, each ending in a small pale tip that curves back with it. Six shorter locks stand on the crown behind them, and the nape's hairline sits inside the collar.
      2. **Between hair and coat.** The collar's top is turned out and down behind and at his sides, so its pink lining is a pale band between head and body from the game's camera; nothing of it is in front. The back's vent is open on the lining from waist to hem. The cuffs are the coat's light with a narrow turned edge in pink.
      3. **The sling and the spear.** The sling is the shirt's pale, and no brown is left on him (the belt is the boots' dark). The shaft has a pale grip wrap, a pale band under the discs and a pale butt cap. He stands with the spear planted.
      - The face is as research saw it.
    - **Measured:** 4,738 triangles in 7 pieces (the Head 2,948, its hair and face). 5.55 studs to his hair's top, 4.10 wide; the spear 7.79 long. One atlas at 92 px a stud. The glow mask holds the hair's pale ends and the three discs, each on its swatch.
    - **How it's made:** `tools/blender/watt_model.py`, from `watt_sheet.py`'s shapes (`build(model=True)`). What the sheet paints a layer off a block is cut into the block's face (`flatten`, and `flatten_head` for his face): one flat surface a face, so nothing flickers at a distance. The bake is Cycles on the CPU (`cpu`), under the lock: with Studio open the GPU's start was stopped by the memory guard. Blender's peak 855 MB.
    - **For Faber, the install:**
      - As Pip and Kirra: every piece carries the body's own surface, the rig's six parts hidden, the Head's decal removed. His face is on the Head piece.
      - **The spear is one piece on a joint of its own on the Torso** (`motors.Spear`), its pivot at the grip's middle. With no clip it lies slung on his back. **He stands with it planted,** which his Idle will key. Until the clips come, `motors.Spear.stood` gives a held pose: the grip at his right fist, the shaft upright with its butt on y = 0, his Right Arm turned to point along `right_arm`.
      - **The glow:** one mask for two pieces, each with its own tint and strength (`emissive.pieces`): cold 0 as he stands, hot in Ground Wire's hold. The strengths are the review's, untried in Studio.
      - **The points:** SpearHead, Blade, Butt, Grip, HairEnds and Chest.
      - **Check in Studio:** each piece's size against the recipe; the face's edges up close and from far (it's cut into the head's wall); the collar's band and the vent from the player's camera; the spear's joint in both poses.
    - **Seen, the maker's eye and not a verdict:**
      - From the game's camera he's no longer one dark mass: the fan of hair with its pale ends, the pink band at the collar, the pale sling across the petrol coat, the vent's wedge, and the spear upright beside him.
      - Planted, the grip's wrap is mostly inside his fist and forearm from the game's camera: it shows below the fist from the front, and whole when the spear is slung. The shaft's line holds by the discs, the band under them and the butt's cap.
      - In the dusk the pink reads as a pale lilac.
    - **Checks:** the content check (`ga-content-check`; the model's sheet, the atlas check and the four maps at full size, likeness from memory): **PASS, for delivery and for the upload of these exact files.** Take A's conditions hold on the bake. Three things it carries forward:
      1. Slung, the discs clear the fanned hair by little from the game's camera: the clips' check shows the slung pose from the side and the back at 64 px, and the hair grows no further toward the spear.
      2. The baked contact shade leaves plum bands on the skin's islands, under the cuffs and the collar, covered on the model. A later bake that makes them redder or ragged goes back for a check.
      3. The vent's wedge stays Storm's pale with its trouser-shade inner, never a skin tone.
    - **Not yet:** his clips, Ground Wire's look; the glow and the face seen in Studio.
    - **Time:** by the clock, 20:10 to 20:30: the three fixes in four light looks, the cut of what's painted into the blocks, three bakes (the face's colour, the legs' bands and stray vertices in the collar found and fixed between them) and the content check. Forecast was 2 to 3 hours.
    - **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes: the sheet whole, the row of bodies, the face close, the glow and the 64 px row enlarged, the atlas. Delivered, yes. Accepted, no: research's verdict is in the game.

**Research's read of the clips' blocking (Quaestor, 2026-11-16, 23:03, from `watt_blocking.png`; poses, before timing).** The verdict comes in motion, at timing.
- **What carries him:** the planted spear is his language. He leans on it, plants it to reveal and to cast, and works with a palm on it. Down, it stands alone while he sits beside it, and he hauls himself up it. That's the feel line's "the weapon he plants", and the bold thing.
- **Ground Wire, blade-down: try it.** Driving the blade into the street is the stronger anime gesture, where a butt-down plant reads as a staff's tap. The flip in the wind-up is anticipation of its own, and the discs low at the ring's centre put Storm's light where it snaps out.
  - Block both and judge them in motion at timing. It's Modulo's tier 1 call.
  - Read it as a blade driven home, never as an electrician's ground rod (the register, 2026-11-15, night).
- **For timing:**
  - Hit1, "the wall," must read as the shaft shoved toward the Shade. In the strip the shaft runs out to his right, blade-end first, and could read as a side jab.
  - Idle's look along the street must carry at 64 px. In the three frames it barely shows, so the head turns further and the shoulders follow a little.

**The animation comparison** (Modulo, 2026-11-16, written by 23:27 by the clock, `ga-reference-study`). The director, mid-timing: "look at some anime vanguard units animations". The question: how do Anime Vanguards' units move (idle, attack, a weapon in two hands) on the blocky body, and where do Watt's clips fall short?
- **Looked at:** the public fan wiki's attack recordings of ten units, eight of them used: two spear users, a scythe, a two-handed sword, a staff, two swords and an Epic. They're in `build/reference/anime-vanguards/animations/` (gitignored, study only): the `.gif` files, a contact sheet of each, `Rummie_crop.png`, `Saber_crop.png` and `Genitsu_crop.png` enlarged; Watt's Combo shrunk to the same size in `build/scratch/modulo/watt/clips/combo_small.png`. The recordings show the units about 30 to 50 px tall, so this is by eye. Nothing is measured but the recordings' lengths. The Rare units have no recordings.
- **OBSERVED:**
  1. Each recording loops in 2.6 to 4.1 s: the unit stands at its spot, attacks, and stands again.
  2. **The attack travels.** The scythe, the spears, the two-handed sword and the Epic dash into the enemies, strike and come back to their spot. The Epic's dash is three afterimages in about 0.3 s, after a crouch of about 0.1 s.
  3. **The effect carries the hit.** From the Mythic up, bursts in the unit's colour cover the screen for about a second and hide the body. The recording's camera pushes in and shakes. Whether the game's own camera does is unknown.
  4. **The idle is almost still** at that size. It reads by its outline (the scythe stood at the side, its blade above the head and turned out; the sword held before the chest; the Epic's crouch) and by an aura that never stops.
  5. **Both hands on one weapon:** the two-handed sword is held with both fists together before the middle of the chest, at rest and between strikes. So the blocky arms cross to the centre.
  6. **The swing is big.** The scythe sweeps over and round the body, about the unit's own height, and the body leans 30 to 45° into it.
- **Watt's clips against them,** at the same size (`combo_small.png`): his hits read in three of nine key poses, the bar across (15, 21) and the rising diagonal (36). In the other six the spear stands upright beside him, one outline, and his torso stays near upright through the whole Combo. What he has that they don't: the planted spear as a language, the clips' own beat.
- **DESIGN, for Watt's timing** (the way of moving is taken; no unit's move, pose or effect is):
  1. **He stays planted:** no dash. The quiet wall doesn't travel, and that's the difference from them that we keep.
  2. **Each hit breaks his outline:** the spear leaves the upright and his body leans into it, 15 to 30°. Hit2 sweeps from low behind him to high before him, and Hit3 is lifted higher before the drive, driven in at a slant ahead of him, his body over it.
  3. **The light carries each hit,** small at a Combo hit and big at Ground Wire (the effect's look, with Faber). The pose only starts the read.
  4. **The idle keeps its lean,** the outline that reads. An aura that never stops would be a new visible asset: a tier 2 proposal for research, not built.
  5. **The two-handed holds stay,** as the sword's are (5 above). R6's shoulder pivot sits on the arm's inner edge, so a fist brought to the chest's middle always sinks the arm's inner edge into the chest. The export's audit counts that as "Arm through body" (0.38 to 0.69 studs, against 0.05), in Combo 54, Ground Wire's hold (both takes) and Work 176.
  - **Research's call** (23:27): keep the two-handed holds at Combo 54 and in Ground Wire's hold, both takes, listed as R6's own look. The player's camera sits behind him for both, the wind-up is brief, and his body covers the crossing.
  - **The exception, Work 176:** Work is seen from before him at the bay, so the crossing would show. That lift goes one-handed: his right hand on the shaft, his left palm staying on the cap.
  - **The outline changes** (2 above): yes, tier 1.

**Timing** (Modulo, 2026-11-16, to 23:41 by the clock):
- **Changed:**
  - **Hit2** winds down and round to his right, then the whole spear wheels as long as it is: the butt up into the Shade, the blade down past his right side.
  - **Hit1** holds a beat (21 to 24) before the left lets go.
  - **Hit3** is lifted higher, his body rocked back, and driven in at a slant ahead of him, the body over it.
  - **Work's post** stands before his left shoulder (`POST`, 1.7 left and 1.75 ahead of his root, the palm 2.95 up), so his left arm reaches straight.
- **Fixed:**
  - The pops: Combo 11, the blade take's 129, its limb flip at 28.
  - Their cause was the hand on the shaft. A straight R6 arm meets the shaft at two points, and the solver jumped between them, so it now keeps the one it held unless the jump is small (`on_shaft`, `SWITCH`, `JUMP`).
- **The audit** over all nine clips passes except the two-handed holds research accepted (Combo 54, Ground Wire 47, the blade take's 127).
- **In motion:** `film_<Clip>.mp4` in `build/scratch/modulo/watt/clips/`, each from its first view at 30 a second (the `film` mode). The two Ground Wire takes are side by side in `film_GroundWire_both.mp4`.
  - From the player's camera, both drives travel about a stud: the light carries Ground Wire, the pose only starts it (the study's 3).
- **Next:** research's pick between the two Ground Wire takes. Then the export with its sheets, the content check, the delivery to `build/handoff/Watt/clips/`, and Ground Wire's look with Faber (`/ga-action`).

**Research's verdict on the timing, and the pick (Quaestor, 2026-11-16, 23:44).** Read frame by frame at 30 a second, from `film_GroundWire_both.mp4` (every frame to 0.7 s, then every fifth), `film_Combo.mp4` and `film_Work.mp4`. Research can't play a film, so the frames stand in for full speed.
- **Felt:** a quiet wall that answers with his spear. Each Combo hit leaves a new outline (the bar across, the rising diagonal, the drive), and Ground Wire lifts the spear high in both hands and drives it home in a fifth of a second.
- **Tier:** competent, no obvious issue.
- **The one bold thing:** the planted spear, blade and discs above his head through Ground Wire's 1.7 s hold.
- **The pick: butt-down.** Blade-down's flip spends 0.20 to 0.27 s pointed at the camera, nearly gone, and reads only at 0.30 to 0.37 s. From 0.40 s its blade and discs sit low behind him, so the plant's point and the light's source are hidden, and the hold shows a bare shaft. The hold is what the player watches, so the blocking read's case for blade-down doesn't survive the player's camera. Butt-down's lift is its own anticipation.
- **No fixes.** The Combo carries the blocking read's two asks, and Work's post brings the palm before his shoulder. The Signature words stay "plants his weapon" (25c8c6f). Faber's markers are the butt-down ones, Plant 25 and Release 124.

**Delivery 4, Watt's clips** (Modulo, 2026-11-16, 23:47 by the clock):

  1. The light starts at the spear's foot in the street.
  2. It never bursts where the shaft overlaps a Shade from the player's camera, or it could read as coming off the body.
- **For Faber at install:**

  - The bay's post cap goes where Work's palm rests: 1.7 left, 1.75 ahead of his root, 2.95 up.
- **Four facts:**

**Ground Wire's look: the parts and their techniques** (Modulo, 2026-11-17, committed 00:33 in e92061c). `/ga-action` step 1, for research's technique check before any block-in. A signature: `ga-fresh-eyes` reads it at the blockout and the final.
- **The beats,** from the delivered clip (60 fps):
  - the lift, 0 to 16, both hands up;
  - the drive, 16 to 25, the butt falling its last 1.1 studs in two frames;
  - **Plant** 25 (0.42 s), then the hold to **Release** 124 (2.07 s);
  - the spear out of the street by 136, at rest by 150.
  - Rung 7 holds both bodies 0.13 s (8 frames) on the Plant.
- **Measured from the clip** (`tools/blender/watt_ground_wire_fx.py measure`, the delivered Transforms on the recipe's joint): the butt stands in the street from 25 to 136, 0.15 right of and 0.80 ahead of his root. The discs are 6.6 studs up the shaft from it.
- **The hero, the ring:** a thin ring of light along the street, out from the butt's foot to the stop's reach (the hit's `Round`, 9, round his root).
  - **Direction:** out from the weapon.
  - **Acceleration:** it snaps out and brakes (Quart Out, 10 frames, three quarters of the way in its first 3), then holds at the edge and fades by 28 (0.47 s).
  - Its centre drifts from the butt to his root as it grows, so at full size its edge is the stop's: what's inside it is stopped.
  - **Technique, a mesh:** a ring on the ground has a near and a far side. It's the kit's `FxColumn` flattened to a low wall, 0.7 studs tall at the foot and 0.24 at the edge, which keeps a thin line on screen at any radius. The column has no caps, so there's no disc. The kit's hoop has a floor band 30% of its radius, 2.7 studs wide at the edge: a filled band where the content check wants a thin ring.
- **Before it, the charge** (16 to 25): a short **Beam** of light runs down the shaft with the drive, from the discs into the street, slow then fast (Quad In), and goes in on the Plant.
  - It rides the spear's `Butt` point, along the shaft. A Beam, because it runs along a shape.
  - The drive travels about a stud from the player's camera (Timing, above). So the charge is the drive's read and the ring's start in one line: the discs, the shaft, the street, the ring.
- **The plant** (25): a **light** at the butt's foot, lit at once and eased out by 24, and a low spray of **sparks** (particles) all round along the street, never up.
- **The ring's front** (25 to 33): **particles** let go along the ring's rim as it travels (an emitter riding the ring, its disc the ring's radius), so its sweep leaves glints in the street. Layer 3 at most, and out if it doesn't add at 64 px.
- **The hold's hum:** the discs' and the hair's own **glow** (the recipe's emissive: cold 0, hot 2.5 and 3.0). The discs gather on the lift, flare on the Plant, pulse twice a second through the hold, and go out as he pulls the spear free.
- **The stop,** on every Shade inside the ring, from the Plant to the end of its stun:
  - a small ring of the same light at its feet (`FxColumn` again, a mesh, 1.4 studs round), closing in on it in 4 frames as the hero passes, pulsing with the discs;
  - its pose held (Faber's stop);
  - no arcs, sparks or flinch on its body: stopped, never hurt.
  - It's its own effect, riding the stopped body, played by Faber's stop for as long as that body's stun lasts (halved on the tough). It goes at once when the stun ends: the lock lets go.
- **The shield,** 5 s from the Plant, past the action's end: faint light standing up from the street round him.
  - A **mesh,** the kit's `FxShell`, 4 studs across, its top 5.8 up and its centre 1.8 up. Its lower part is under the street, so only a rim 3.6 across meets the ground. The spear's blade stands out of it in the hold.
  - Bright at its foot and clear by its top (the `rise` ramp), lit on the Plant, then held quiet with the same hum.
  - Its own effect too, riding him, played from his `ShieldUntil` (FightService sets it "for its look"), and ended with the shield.
- **The content check's note 2, measured** (`... screen`, cameras round the fight, the target Shade at rest 4.5 ahead):
  - From the replay's camera (heading 215, as for Kirra), the target covers 5 to 10 of 16 points on a 1-stud ring round the butt's foot, and 8 to 14 of 20 points of the shaft. So on the play camera the plant sits partly behind the target.
  - By construction, nothing at the plant rises: the light and the sparks lie along the street, the ring is a low wall, and nothing bursts at the shaft's height.
  - The block's take at 64 px is the check.
- **The block contains,** in grey (`Grey` true, `Action` `WattGroundWire`), three rows files, written by `tools/blender/watt_ground_wire_fx.py rows` from the clip (plain `py`). They're in `build/handoff/Watt/fx/`, byte for byte the checked drafts (delivered below):
  - `WattGroundWire`: the charge, the plant's light, the sparks, the ring, its front, the discs and the hair. `Free` is on the Plant, so the ring runs through rung 7's 8-frame hold while the bodies are held, and the stop lands with it. The glows ride the free clock from there, so their release is written 8 frames late to stay on the clip.
  - `WattGroundWireStop`: its ring.
  - `WattGroundWireShield`: the shell.
- **Waits for the finish:**
  - Storm's colours: a Storm ramp the kit lacks, white core to the `#FF6FD8` edge;
  - a drawn street mark, if the block wants one: lines of light in the stone, never a stain;
  - static off the hair;
  - his sounds (Faber's);
  - anything new to upload;
  - **the ring's front comes out of the rows** (Modulo, 2026-11-17, from Faber's take in the game): its glints don't show apart from the sparks at any size, nor at 64 px ("The blockout in the game", condition 5), so by its own line above it goes. The block stays as taken for the verdict, since it reads the same without them.
- **For Faber, at the blockout** (his calls):
  1. `Effect = "WattGroundWire"` on the action, then `rows_take` for the three files.
  2. The stop's look: `WattGroundWireStop` played on each body his stop holds, riding it, and ended with its stun.
  3. The shield's look: `WattGroundWireShield` on Watt from `ShieldUntil`, ended at it, or when the shield is spent if it can be.
  4. The sparks' flat fan is `SpreadAngle` (6, 180) from `Front`. If Roblox turns it into a wall, swap the two (a Tune).
- **Section 2's effect conditions still standing:** Storm light pink to white, as lines and a thin ring with speed; no filled dark disc on the ground; no drops or stain. The lens's plum and the jaw's arcs were the first model's, and went with it.
- **Time:** the rows, about 45 minutes after the check. The blockout with Faber in the replay, about an hour.
- **Content check, 2026-11-17, by 00:35: PASS for the grey block** (the dev build, nothing uploaded), on the spec and the three files, with no render yet. Its conditions for the block:
  1. The sparks lie flat. If `SpreadAngle` (6, 180) draws a wall or a standing fan, swap the two before anyone judges the take.
  2. The charge, from the replay's camera, never reads as light through the target's torso, at full size or at 64 px. If it does, it's shortened to the shaft above the Shade, or cut.
  3. A take for the next check: the Plant and a mid-hold frame, full size and at 64 px over dark, from the replay's camera and two more headings. `ga-fresh-eyes` reads the same take.
  4. The stop's ring stays a thin line with the street showing inside it. If it reads as a band or a plate, its wall thins.
  5. The front's glints ride the rim. If they fill the disc, they're fixed or dropped.
  - **For the finish:** Storm's ramp runs from a white core to `#FF6FD8`, never toward red or dark; a street mark, if any, is lines of light in the stone; static off the hair is lines and arcs; the finish gets its own check on the exact files before any upload.

  - Implemented: the rows.

  - Director-accepted: no.

Then the shield's last 3 s show nowhere in the world, and on a phone the player watches the fight, not the HUD. The blockout plays the shell and no shell side by side (tier 1). **The retake** (Faber, 2026-11-17, 10:12 to 10:40 by the clock), research's must-fix above, three parts.
- **1. On a Shade.**

- **2. The shield, as an outline** (`Config.Effects.Outlines`):
  - It's the outline of the body's one Highlight, which it shares with the hit flash so neither hides the other. The fill is clear, and it's Occluded.
  - In grey-white (236, 238, 245) at 0.15 until Storm's colour pass. It's read each frame, so a Tune shows at once. `Effects.Lasting` still switches it off.
  - The shell's rows are out of the game (`Config/EffectRows/WattGroundWireShield.json`). Git keeps them, and Modulo's handoff copy stays.
- **3. In motion,** in `build/captures/ar11/`. Five takes of 14 s, each holding one whole press, from the charge to past the shield's end. They run at 57.1 to 58.8 fps with Studio in front: the game's own rate with the effects up.

  | Take | Camera, light | The Plant at |
  |---|---|---|
  | `retake_play_outline.mkv` | the player's, dusk | 6.000 s |
  | `retake_play_outline_day.mkv` | the player's, day | 5.233 s |
  | `retake_play_none.mkv` | the player's, day, no shield look (the side by side) | 4.233 s |
  | `retake_plus70_outline.mkv` | +70° round the fight's middle, day | 4.067 s |
  | `retake_minus70_outline.mkv` | −70°, day | 7.683 s |

  - The turned cameras keep the player's camera's distance and height, held on every frame. So the Plant's shake doesn't show in them.
  - **Sheets:** `sheet_<take>.jpg`, from 0.4 s before the Plant to 5.4 s after (the shield ends at 5.0). `fight_play_outline_day.jpg` and `fight_play_none.jpg` are the fight up close, the side by side.

  - **The brawler:** held mid-swing, its arm out at Watt, with the stop's thin ring at its feet. It reads as caught, not knocked.
  - **From the player's camera,** the brawler stands in front of Watt for the whole hold and covers most of him. That's how the replay frames a fight, the lead beyond its target. The outline shows only on what's left of him in view.
  - **The outline itself:** a Highlight's line is about one pixel wide at any distance, and Roblox gives it no thickness. At the player's distance it's faint beside none. No 64 px read is made yet, and a one-pixel line is the first thing that size loses.
  - **From ±70°** it traces him and his spear, and it's gone after the shield (`close_plus70_outline.jpg`, `close_minus70_outline.jpg`). Where the brawler covers him, the line also runs along the brawler's edge. An Occluded outline traces only what of him is in view.

- **Four facts:**
  - Implemented: yes, in grey.

    - logic: 25,042 passed, 0 failed, offline and in Studio. The looks case now takes a look as rows or an outline, never both. The shell's rows checks went with its file;
