**Research's verdict on the retake, in motion (Quaestor, 2026-11-17, 10:44),** from the takes at 10 frames a second (the player's camera by day, +70°), the side by side and the close sheets, with `ga-fresh-eyes`' blind read beside it.
- **Felt (research):** the Plant snaps hard and reads: a flash, and the ring out along the street. Then the brawler just stands in front of Watt, and the shield isn't there. **Fresh eyes:** "the Shade never visibly stops mid-step (it just stands, then shuffles on)", and the outline and no-outline captures look the same.
- **Tier:** works (both reads). **The one bold thing:** the ring snapping out along the street, on both reads again. It stays as it is.
- **What the retake proved:** a Shade caught mid-swing can't carry the lock from the player's camera, because its swing points away from the camera. And a one-pixel outline can't carry the shield at game distance. From that camera, the Shade covers Watt, and only his spear's head stands clear above it.
- **Must-fix, one class: a lasting look reads on what the player's camera sees, as a fill or a light, never as a line** (round 2 on the hold):
  1. **The lock on the stopped Shade's whole body:** a fill (the body's `Highlight` with its fill set, grey-white until Storm's pass), on for the stop and gone with it. It pulses slowly, which is the hum. The ring at its feet stays.
  2. **The shield on the spear's head:** a small self-lit glint on the blade that pulses for the shield's 5 s, planted or in his hand. It's the one part of him that's always in view. The outline may stay, since it costs nothing and reads close, but it doesn't carry the shield.
  - A tier 1 trial on a blockout: Faber's technique, Modulo's look at the colour pass.
- **The check:** the same takes, the player's camera and +70°, plus one at 64 px. The lock and the shield must each read at a glance beside the side by side.
- **Not this piece's, asked of Faber:** at the play camera, the lead stands beyond its target, so every signature's body is hidden at its own moment. Does a real fight frame it this way, or only the replay? The answer goes in this entry. It isn't held here.
- **Rounds:** this is round 2 on the hold. If the next one fails, the hold's options go to the director with this evidence.
  - Director-accepted: no.

**The first design, 2026-11-14 to 15, rejected by the director: its record.** Sections 1 and 2 are kept as Modulo wrote them.

### 1. The sheet (Modulo, 2026-11-14)

**Where:** `build/handoff/Watt/watt_sheet.png`. `tools/blender/watt_sheet.py` rebuilds it with `-- <out> small`. Its `check` mode builds every pose without rendering, and `draft` renders the takes at half the pixels a stud. The plan was written offline while memory kept headless Blender shut. The draft found two misses, both fixed as tier 1 before the sheet: the wrench's size and its jaw (below). Nothing here is settled before research's verdict.

- **The idea:** a man and his tool, wired in one line. The current runs one way through him: from the generator on his back, out of its two coils, into the wrench, and down into the street. Ground Wire is that path made visible, so the props explain the signature before it plays.
- **The wrench, the hero prop:**
  - An open-end wrench whose head is wider than his chest: 3.6 studs across (R6's torso is 2) and 2.4 tall, rounded. The jaw's gap is 1.5 wide and cuts 1.45 below the shorter jaw. It leans 15° off the handle, as a spanner's does, so one jaw stands longer.
  - Why these: in the draft, at 2.6 by 1.6, it read as a big wrench, never a shield, and his outline stayed 0.71 to 0.74 of Rook's. With a square, narrow gap, the head had two even lobes and read as a double axe three-quarter on. The leaning gap reads as a spanner in black and at 64 px.
  - Its handle ends in a short copper spike, a ground rod, so "plants the wrench" (his row) is literal. He drives it in spike first, and the head stands before him as the shield.
  - 5.39 studs end to end. Steel, with copper at the spike and one rubber band at the grip.
- **The pack:**
  - A squat generator housing on a frame. Steel, so it's the back's one light mass from the player's camera: Kirra's fix 1, applied before it's asked.
  - Two coils on short posts rise from its top corners: copper windings a stud tall, each with a cap, the caps over his crown at 5.68 studs. They're the two places an effect starts, and they make his top outline: a head between two posts, where Rook's is one collar wall. The draft's coils, 0.56 tall, barely changed the outline.
  - A small round gauge on the housing, on the camera's side.
- **No cable, tier 1:** a cord from the pack to the wrench would dangle through every clip and need joints of its own. Instead the coils arc to the wrench in Ground Wire's effect (delivery 4), so the path still reads.
- **The visor:**
  - A welding visor on a cream headband, flipped up on his brow at rest.
  - In a fight it comes down, its lens slot lit Storm.
  - One hinge on the head: up and down are one joint's two keys, and "flipped up after a win" is a clip's last beat.
- **The body, stocky on R6's blocks:**
  - A Kiln coverall with its top half pulled down and its sleeves knotted at his left hip, their ends hanging, over a Graphite work shirt. The back panel hangs over his seat, an orange mass under the steel pack.
  - A cream canvas harness: straps over his shoulders and down his chest to the belt, the front's light lines.
  - Graphite rubber gloves to mid-forearm, and boots with thick soles, so Insulated is in the clothes.
  - Clothes are thin shells on the blocks, like Rook's, Pip's and Kirra's.
- **The face:** R6's round head, deep brown skin, a close crop under a cream headband, a shadow of stubble on a strong jaw, heavy level brows, a broad nose, and a mouth set in a small crooked line: pleased with himself before anything's done.
- **Colours,** three chips:
  - **Kiln `#C46A2A`,** the coverall. It's Endurance's orange (the type's `#F4902A`) taken deep, so it holds at noon. It's 28° of hue off Rook's Oxblood and nearly twice as light (luma 0.47 against 0.25).
  - **Steel `#A8ACB2`,** the wrench and the housing: the machine's colour, and the light mass the camera sees.
  - **Storm `#FF6FD8`** (`Config.UIKit`'s Elements), only on what's lit: the coil caps, the visor's lens, the gauge's needle, and the jaw's edge in Ground Wire. It's magenta, 109° of hue from Kirra's Sky `#47A6F5`, and the sheet's chip row sets the two side by side.
  - **Neutrals:** Graphite `#2E3035` (rubber and shirt), Copper `#B5713C` (windings and spike), Cream `#E8DCC4` (headband and stitching), Skin `#6A4330`.
- **How he stands,** two takes for the 64 px measure:
  - **A, stood:** the wrench planted on its spike ahead of his right side, broadside, its head at his chest, his right hand on its grip, his left hand by the knot.
  - **B, leant:** the wrench's head on the street ahead of his right foot, its handle leaning back into his right hand, the spike past his wrist.
  - I recommend A. From the player's camera, it parts more from Rook (0.66 against B's 0.70), and its head stands as the shield the feel line names. From that camera, B's head lies flat, and the wrench reads as a long stick. Both are on the sheet, and research picks.
- **Ground Wire's key pose,** the hold after the slam:
  - The wrench stands spike-down in the street before him, its head at his chest.
  - He's hunched behind it, one foot braced back, both hands on the head's edges, chin down. The lit lens shows through the jaw's gap: a slot of Storm in a steel wall.
  - R6's legs don't bend, and spreading them lifts the body: its soles' edges go lowest. So the head comes down by a lean of 22° over the wrench with the chin down. The script's geometry check puts the lens at 4.26 studs up, inside the gap's 3.49 to 4.89. Across, the wrench is set so the leaning gap's middle passes through the lens, and the rod is 0.3 into the street.
  - Both coil caps are lit.
  - On the sheet, the ring is a grey disc round the spike at its key radius, with one grey Shade mid-step inside it.
  - Three views: from the enemy's side (the wall and the slot); side on; and from the player's camera (the lit pack, with the head's edges standing out past his shoulders on both sides).
- **The measures,** printed and on the sheet:
  - At 64 px over noon, Lantern Row's dusk and night, and in black, beside Rook, front and from the player's camera.
  - The outlines' overlap is the sheets' own measure (1 is the same outline). Take A, stood: 0.66 from the player's camera, 0.65 front. Take B, leant: 0.70 and 0.59.
  - For scale, by the same measure: Pip against Rook is 0.71 and 0.65, and Lyric 0.63 and 0.57. Two broad R6 bodies share most of their pixels whatever they carry, so this measure can't part them as it parted Kirra from Pip (0.35). The number misses what parts him at 64 px: the two coil posts over his head and the wrench's crescent at his side. Both are clear in the black row.
  - Storm is 109° of hue from Kirra's Sky, and the chip row sets them side by side.
  - Sizes: 5 studs to the crown, 5.68 to the visor's top at rest, 8.49 across with the wrench beside him.
- **The script:** it uses Kirra's render and measures. Rook, Pip and Lyric are drawn by their own builders. It's a Workbench blockout, with no GPU turn. `check` peaks at 213 MB, the draft at about 0.8 GB, and the full sheet at 1.5 GB.
- **The blockout's size:** 6,764 triangles standing, against Rook's 5,092.
- **Content check:** PASS, 2026-11-14, for the sheet only. Two notes for the clips:
  - In Ground Wire's hold, the jaw frames his face from the Shades' side. In the clip, the gap stays clear of his head and neck from every heading.
  - The workshop comedy stays at his competence, never at his expense. Watch for this in Reveal and Work.
  - The mesh, the textures and each clip get their own check before any upload.
- **Time:** about 1 hour 40 minutes in all. The plan and the script took about 55 minutes. The draft, the two fixes and the sheet took about 45 more (22:04 to 22:50 local), with the renders waiting on memory in between. On Opus 5.5. Kirra's sheet took about 45 minutes in its session, plus a plan written in the session before.

**Research's verdict on the sheet,** 2026-11-14, night, read whole at full size:
- **Felt:** a stocky worker with a generator on his back and a spanner as big as a door, pleased with himself; at 64 px he's the coil posts and the crescent before he's anything else.
- **Tier:** competent, no obvious issue.
- **The one bold thing:** the crescent wrench. One tool is his weapon, his shield and his ground rod, and the sheet's black row parts him from Rook by it and the two posts, where the overlap number can't. That measure is accepted as blind for two broad R6 bodies; the black row is the read.
- **Take A,** stood: the head stands as the shield the feel line names. B's head lies flat from the player's camera and reads as a long stick, as Modulo found.
- **Storm** reads away from Kirra's Sky at a glance, in every row.
- **Fix, at the model (delivery 2):** in the dark row, the shirt, gloves and sleeves fuse into one Graphite mass, so the arms that swing the wrench vanish on a night street. Lift the shirt one value step, or give it a cream rolled cuff, so the arms part from dusk; the pack and the legs already do. Serves the Combo's read.
- **Note for delivery 4:** from the player's camera, the hold is the lit pack, with the wall mostly hidden, so Ground Wire reads by the ring and the coils' arc. The effect carries the shield's read from behind, never the pose alone.
- The content check's two notes stand for the clips.

### 2. The model on R6 (Modulo, 2026-11-15)

- **Where:** `build/scratch/modulo/watt/model/` until research's yes, then `build/handoff/Watt/`. The files:
  - `Watt_<Piece>.json`, 8 pieces;
  - `Watt_Color.png`, `Watt_Roughness.png`, `Watt_Metalness.png` and `Watt_Emissive.png`;
  - `Watt.recipe.json`;
  - `watt_model_sheet.png`, with a half-size copy;
  - `watt_atlas_check.png`, and `watt_model.blend` for the clips' rig.

  `tools/blender/watt_model.py` makes them in under a minute, with a Cycles bake under the lock: `blender -b --factory-startup --python tools/blender/watt_model.py -- <out dir>`. His body comes from `watt_sheet.build(model=True)`, so the sheet and the model share one source.
- **How he sits on R6:** as Pip and Kirra do. Each of the six parts is a rigid mesh that carries the body's surface; the rig's blocks are hidden and the face decal goes. The generator and its two coils are the Torso piece's. Two pieces have joints of their own:
  - **The visor, one hinge on the Head** (`Watt_Visor`), its pivot at his temples. Up on his brow is its mesh, so he's himself with no clip playing. A turn of -100° about the hinge brings it down over his eyes: down for a fight, up again after a win, as a clip's own keys.
  - **The wrench, one piece on the Torso** (`Watt_Wrench`), its pivot at the grip's middle, as Kirra's blades are. With no turn it stands planted ahead of his right side as take A has it, its rod 0.3 studs into the street. The clips key it into his hands, and into the street for Ground Wire. On the Torso and not the arm, so a clip can leave it standing while his hands move to the head's edges.
- **The numbers:**
  - 8,532 triangles in 8 pieces: the torso 3,280 (with the generator and the coils), the wrench 1,192, the head 1,064, each leg 760, each arm 656, the visor 164. Kirra is 10,398, Rook 5,092.
  - 5.68 studs to the coils' caps, 4.12 wide, 5.88 across with the wrench at rest.
  - One 1024 px atlas, at 74 px per stud.
  - Maps: colour with the contact shading baked in, roughness, metalness (the steel and the copper, at 0.55), and a glow mask. No normal map.
- **Research's fix, the arms at dusk: a cream rolled cuff.** Each short sleeve ends in a cream band 0.2 studs tall, a touch wider than the sleeve. I took the cuff over a value step: one step up from Graphite still sinks on a dusk street, as Kirra's navy did, and cream is already his harness's colour, so it adds no colour. The sheet shows him lit as Lantern Row's dusk, large and at 64 px: the cuffs are two light bars that mark where each arm is. The forearm under it (skin, then the glove) still sinks; the cuff carries the arm.
- **What lights, and how.** Roblox draws `(mask x strength x tint + light) x colour map`, so the colour under a glow carries its hue. One mask, and each piece takes its own tint and strength (the recipe's `emissive`):
  - **The Torso:** the two coil caps and the gauge's needle. Their colour is Storm, lit or not. Strength 0.5 as he stands, 2.0 in Ground Wire's hold, tint white.
  - **The Visor:** the lens. With its light out it's a dark Storm glass (`#752E63`), where the sheet drew it ink: under ink no strength could light it. Strength 0 at rest, 7.0 lit, tint white.
  - **The Wrench:** the jaw's two inner walls. Steel with their light out, as the sheet has them. Strength 0 at rest, 3.0 lit, tint Storm.
  - Each lit surface sits on its swatch, one flat block on every map, so no small island reads as a drop on the colour map (the content check on Pip's flame).
  - The strengths are the review sheet's own, drawn with the glow multiplied through the colour map as the engine does. DESIGN, untried in Studio.
- **For the builder:**
  - Eight points: `CoilR` and `CoilL`, the caps, where an arc starts; `Jaw`, the middle of the gap, where the arcs land; `Rod`, where the rod meets the street, where the ring starts; `Lens`; `Gauge`; `Chest`; `Grip`.
  - The recipe gives the wrench's `along` (the handle's line) and `across` (the head's width), and the visor's hinge `axis` and `down` turn.
  - The three lit pieces each need their own SurfaceAppearance, for their own strength; the other five can share one.
- **Mine, tier 1:** the lens's dark glass; the caps lit low at rest, so the two posts carry Storm in every clip; the wrench's joint on the Torso.
- **What I see wrong:**
  - With no clip playing his arms hang, and the wrench's grip grazes the right glove. Every clip keys the hand to it.
  - In take A the head's inner horn passes about 0.1 studs ahead of the harness. A clip that turns the torso keeps the wrench clear.
  - From the front the head covers his right arm and chest, as take A does on the sheet. The face stays clear, three-quarter on and from the front.
  - The visor up is a steel plate standing over his brow; side on it reads as a raised visor, from the front as a thin peak.
  - The shirt, the gloves and the pack's rails are still one Graphite family. The cuffs, the harness and the steel part them.
- **Self-review:** made and looked at whole, three runs: the sheet at full size, the atlas check, the recipe read back. The first run's figures were small on the sheet, and the lens's point was given with the visor down where its mesh has it up; both changed.
- **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes. Delivered, no: for research's verdict from `build/scratch/modulo/watt/model/watt_model_sheet.png`. Accepted, no.

**Research's verdict on the model,** 2026-11-15, night (`watt_model_sheet.png` whole, its 64-pixel rows and the dusk panel):
- **Felt: yes.** A stocky mechanic you know at 64 px by the two coil posts with their Storm caps and the crescent wrench. From the player's camera he's the steel pack with its gauge and the two posts, the back's one light mass, as the sheet planned.
- **Tier:** competent, no obvious issue.
- **The one bold thing:** still the wrench. On the model it holds as a shield from the front and as a ground rod by its spike.
- **The sheet's fix lands:** the cream rolled cuff parts the arms from the night street in the dusk panel, and the shirt keeps its value. Modulo's call stands over a lifted shirt: a cuff is one clear edge, where a lighter shirt would grey the whole black-and-orange read.
- **The dark lens stands:** dark Storm glass with its light from the emissive. Off, it reads as glass. Lit, it reads as a light. A pale colour would look lit even when it's off.
- **Modulo's list of what's wrong** is all for the clips, not the model: the grip against the glove at rest, the horn ahead of the harness, and the head over the arm from the front. Each clip keys the hand and checks the audit.
- **For delivery:** to `build/handoff/Watt/` once the content check's line is in. Then the clips.

**Delivered (Modulo, 2026-11-15, 18:41):**
- **Content check:** PASS for delivery to the builder, 2026-11-15, on the sheet, the atlas check, the four maps and the recipe. One note taken before delivery: on the colour map the wrench head's two islands lay back to back, a mirrored pair of crescents at 64 px. `watt_model.py` now turns the right one half round in its own box (`turn_a_head`), so both open the same way. The model was baked again, a fourth run, and the check re-opened every file: PASS, the pair's read gone, nothing new. The model's look is unchanged, so research's verdict stands on it.
- **Its conditions, for later:**
  - The clips: the sheet's two notes; the wrench never moves into his own torso, head or neck; the rod goes into the street only, never into or at a Shade; a Combo hit shows light, sparks or dust; each clip is stepped on the bare rig from the side and three-quarter (Ground Wire's hold there is a hunch with both hands up).
  - Ground Wire's look: Storm stays light pink to white, as lines, arcs and a thin ring with speed; no filled dark disc on the ground, no drops or stain; the lens's plum is never an effect colour; the arcs land at the jaw and are read from every heading at 64 px; locked Shades read as stopped.
  - The upload: its own check on the exact maps and meshes, with one more look at the two small copper islands, and the glow seen in Studio from the player's camera first.
- **Where:** `build/handoff/Watt/`, the eight meshes, the four maps, the recipe and `watt_model_sheet.png`, copied byte for byte from the checked run. `watt_model.blend` stays in `build/scratch/modulo/watt/model/` for the clips' rig.
- **Time:** about 15 minutes by the clock, 18:27 to 18:41, both checks included: four guarded runs under a minute each (Blender's peak 1,005 MB). On Fable 5.2. Kirra's model took about 25 minutes.
- **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes. Delivered, yes, for the builder's install. Accepted, no: research's verdict is in, the director hasn't seen it.

**Held (Modulo, 2026-11-15, 18:49):** research's message, minutes after the delivery: hold Watt, don't deliver, don't start his clips. It relays that the director rejects the design as it stands and wants him rethought; the director's words are research's to record (`docs/decisions.md`), and a new brief comes from research once the director answers on the overall style. The delivery above had already been copied and committed (1c9dda6). So: **nothing in `build/handoff/Watt/` is for install**, the files stay where they are until the new brief says what's kept, and no clip is keyed. Nothing new starts on any Voidkin's look until then.

## AR24. The chest opening's look: three directions (Modulo, for research's verdict, then Faber's block)

Research's word with 22c0d60 (02:21): "AR24, the chest opening's look, can start from TR34 … the band's look and the best's moment are ours to design." The feel line, the beats and the numbers are TR34's (`docs/test-requests.md`); the form is the director's (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-11-17, 02:16 and 02:20): a translucent black band across the whole screen, a title on top, the items centred, OK below. Content check on the plan, before drawing (2026-11-17, before the first drawing at 02:30): PASS on rules 2, 3 and 4, with eight conditions the sheets keep (one line for the rest of the haul, no reel and no casino words, nothing to buy, the best's light in hard lines with nothing pooling, our own crest, our own backdrop, the stand-in face in scratch only, a content check on the finished pictures).

- **The block contains:** three directions, each on one sheet: settled (37 Shade Chests of Lantern Row, ten merged tiles from Common up, the best a Rare), three beats (the tap, the pour, the best landing) and one chest alone. All three share one layout, so they compare as looks: a band 150 points high centred at 205 of 375, tiles at 48, the best at 64 after a wider gap, the haul as one line of coloured words, OK under the row.
- **Waits:** two rows and "+N more"; the Epic-or-better reveal before the band (TR34, beat 4); the game's text face (Blender's stands in); the forge stones' own colour and mark; the motion, judged in Faber's block from a phone capture; the sound (AR13's cues, Faber's).
- **The sheets** (`build/scratch/modulo/ar24/chest_band_A.png`, `_B`, `_C`; `chest_band_glance.png` shows the three settled at phone size, 1 px a point; drawn by `tools/blender/chest_band_directions.py` from AR6's gear pictures, AR21's ChestShade and the game's rarity colours, over Faber's TR24 phone capture of the bag, dimmed. The capture is the slate bag, before the lacquer rebuild of AR18's A):
  - **A, the seam: the void's own.**
    - The band opens as one hairline of pale light that parts into its two edges: the reveal's crack turned on its side. Inside, violet-black.
    - The best: its colour runs out along both edges from it, and an upright shaft of its light stands behind it, cut hard at the band.
    - The price: the calmest of the three. Its moment is the edges' colour, which reads at a glance but doesn't hit.
  - **B, the banner: anime poster energy.**
    - The band tilts up to the right and runs in from the left on its slant, black with fine diagonal lines, a bold stripe along its top. The title is big and slanted, the haul under it, both on a black tag hung from the stripe.
    - The best: straight focus lines in its colour burst from behind it inside the band, the stripe takes its colour, its rarity's word on a slanted tag under it. OK is a white slanted plate.
    - The price: the tilt sits against the bag's straight grid, and focus lines on every open could tire (below).
  - **C, the tray: the bag's lacquer.**
    - Warm black, brass rules along both edges, the chest's picture in a brass ring hung over the top rule. The tiles are the bag's own cells (AR18's A), so what lands looks as it will in the bag.
    - The best stands on a short brass bar in a halo of its light, rimmed in its colour. OK hangs from the bottom rule.
    - The price: it's warm brown more than black, and the best's moment is the quietest at phone size.
- **In all three:** the best's light is lines and shafts with hard edges, never a pool under a tile. "×N" sits on the tile's corner. Nothing on the band, the single chest or "+N more" offers a purchase.
- **Proposed, for every direction:** the best's moment scales with its rarity. A Common best gets no light, an Uncommon its colour on the rim, Rare and up the full moment. A run of ordinary opens then stays quick, and a good one stands out.
- **My lean: B.** It's the director's "black" most literally, and its best moment reads at a glance at the phone's size, which A's and C's don't. A is the right mood for the void and costs the least to build. C fits the bag best but loses the moment.
- **Self-review, up close and at phone size** (2026-11-17, 02:32). Fixed before handing in:
  - B's haul ran into the focus lines, so it moved under the title on a black tag.
  - A's and C's bands let the bag's cells read through behind the title, so A's band went from 0.86 to 0.92 cover and C's from 0.9 to 0.94.
  - C's halo didn't read, so it went from 0.42 to 0.6 and from 46 to 54 points wide.
  - The sting's white flash ended in a visible square, so its window grew.
- **Four facts:**
  - Implemented: the three sheets and the glance.
  - Tested: drawn without error, every tile's picture found.
  - Visually reviewed: my own pass only, up close and at phone size. Research's verdict waits.
  - Director-accepted: no.


**Research's verdict on the three directions** (Quaestor, 2026-11-17, 02:35). It's read from the three sheets and the glance at phone size, and it picks the direction. The look is judged in motion in Faber's block, from a phone capture.
- **Felt, B:** the haul lands like a poster. The best one bursts out of the band in its own colour, and it's the only one of the three whose moment carries at the phone's size. **Tier:** competent, as a direction. **The one bold thing:** the focus lines in the best's colour.
- **A** is the calmest: right for the void, but its moment is a shaft that doesn't hit. **C** is warm brown, against the director's "black", and its moment is the quietest. Neither carries the chance being felt.
- **The pick: B,** with three fixes so it holds the director's form as said ("central horizontal black Ui transparent that span the whole screen … text on top and the items in middle centered with a ok button below"):
  1. **The band's body is level and centred.** The slant lives in the cuts: the stripe's ends, the tags and the OK plate. The energy stays and the band is horizontal.
  2. **The title and the haul are centred over the row,** still on their black tag.
  3. **The band's body is translucent:** the bag shows faintly through it, and the cover rises only behind the words.
- **Modulo's proposal is taken** for every open: the best's moment scales with its rarity. A Common best gets the size-up only, with no lines. An Uncommon gets its colour on the rim and the stripe. From Rare up, it gets the full burst. How many lines, how long and the hold are numbers in `Config.Chests.Opening`.
- **The director's pick (2026-11-17, 02:40): A, the seam** ("i like A \"the seam more"), in place of research's B. B's three fixes are void, since A already holds the director's form. The rarity-scaled moment stays. A's price is its calm moment, so at the block the edges' run of colour and the shaft are judged in motion. If the moment doesn't hit, the levers are their speed and brightness, never a new shape.
- **Next, superseded by the pick:** Modulo redraws B's settled band and one chest with the three fixes. Then Faber's block (TR34), and research's verdict in motion from a phone capture: up close, every state, beside a shipped anime game's reward screen.

### A for Faber's block (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 02:48)

On the director's pick, `tools/blender/chest_band.py` draws A as it stands, in place of B fixed (44b4a67 keeps that, void). Its sheet (`build/scratch/modulo/ar24/chest_band_A_seam.png`; `chest_band_A_seam_phone.png` at a pixel a point) shows the tap, the sting, settled with a Rare best, and one chest with an Uncommon best. Its notes give every number in points, as seeds for `Config.Chests.Opening`: the band, the inside's ramp, the edges' light and how it burns as the seam opens, the run's reach, the shaft, the tiles, the type and OK.
- **The rarity scale in A:** a Common best gets its size-up only. An Uncommon gets its colour on its rim and running out along both edges. Rare and up add the shaft and the glow.
- **Changed from 02ee5aa's A:** only that scale. The shaft, the glow and the sting's white flash used to show for every best.
- **Four facts:**
  - Implemented: the sheet and its phone strip.
  - Tested: drawn without error.
  - Visually reviewed: my own pass. Research's verdict comes in motion, after Faber's block.
  - Director-accepted: A's direction is the director's pick (02:40). This sheet hasn't been seen.

## AR25. Sable (Modulo, then Faber)

**Waits for the big update after launch** (the director, 2026-11-17, 02:40: five Voidkin at launch). The takes can be drawn when Modulo's launch work is done.

Research's request, 2026-11-17, 02:36, at Modulo's ask ("Sable is next in the roster's order").
- **His row** in `docs/roster.md`: Uncommon, Technique, Assassin, no element, Zone 1. The look rules are in the roster's "The look".
- **Why he matters:** he's Lantern Row's only Uncommon, so for most players he's the first shard that isn't Common. He's also the gap the shards roll today (TR24, Sable's guard).
- **The cap goes** (research's call, on Modulo's flag): the row's feathered cap reads as a period costume, the corny the director rejected in Watt. The duelist lives in the silhouette, the stance and the rapier.

**Feel line:** the first Uncommon you pull, and he looks like he knows it. A duelist with poise, known at 64 px by his stance and the thin line of his rapier, who waits for the blow and makes the Shade sorry it threw one.

**Signature feel line, Riposte** (its function unchanged): he sets his guard, the next blow glances off his blade in one bright spark, and he's through the attacker in a single lunge before it lands.

**What stays:** his row's numbers, Riposte's and Footwork's functions, and his bay aptitude.

**Delivers,** each shown before the next, as Watt's (AR11):
1. **Three takes,** for research's pick.
   - **How they're drawn:** as Watt's. In grey on the R6 body with one accent; front and three-quarter from the game's camera; at 64 px over noon and night; each face close up; quick blockouts.
   - **The limit:** each take is a different anime cast member inside an Uncommon's limit: three or four elements and one accent of its own, apart from every lead's and every rarity's colour.
     - **A, the heir:** composed and cold.
       - A fitted, high-collared short jacket, with a short half-cape pinned on one shoulder only.
       - Pale hair in sharp clumps, with a long fringe over one eye.
       - A slim rapier with a swept guard at the hip.
       - Narrow eyes and a faint smile.
     - **B, the show-off:** cocky.
       - A cropped open jacket with its sleeves pushed up, over a high-necked sleeveless top, and one long glove.
       - Hair cut short at the sides, its long top falling forward in sharp points.
       - A dark-bladed rapier worn across the small of his back.
       - A grin.
     - **C, the quiet prodigy:** soft-spoken.
       - A long open coat over a loose shirt with a ribbon tie, its collar turned up on one side.
       - Wavy, shoulder-length hair tied low.
       - The rapier in a plain pale scabbard, carried in his hand, never worn.
       - Gentle eyes you can't read.
   - At 64 px his outline parts from every lead before him, Kirra's blades and Watt's spear included. The rapier reads as a thin line, never a stick.
   - **The pick:** research's, with `ga-fresh-eyes`, judged against a real anime's cast. It goes to the director as a notice, not a gate.
2. **The model** on R6, from the picked take: the hair, the clothes, the face, the rapier, and the places an effect starts (the blade's point, the guard).
3. **The clips,** as Pip's set:
   - Idle, in his stance;
   - Combo: quick and light, the Assassin's way (`docs/design.md`);
   - Riposte;
   - Hit, Down and Up;
   - Reveal: the first Uncommon, so it lands his poise in one beat;
   - Work: his own way of making power, never a trade's.
4. **Riposte's look,** with the builder in the same session (`/ga-action`):
   - **One hero shape:** the lunge's straight line, from his guard through the target, with a sharp acceleration.
   - **The parry:** one spark on the blade, in his accent.
   - **No element,** so its light is his accent and white.

**Waits:**
- his install;
- Riposte's guard and Footwork as data (Faber's);
- his sounds.

**Checks:**
- the content check on the takes and before every upload;
- research's verdict on the takes, the clips and the final in motion;
- `ga-fresh-eyes` on the takes, and on Riposte at its blockout and final.

**Time it:** each delivery records what it took. The takes: 1 to 2 hours, forecast.

## AR26. The launch five's basic attacks (Modulo and Faber, through `/ga-action`)

Research's request, 2026-11-17, 02:41, on the director's word of the same night (`docs/decisions.md`): "their m1 need to be original need Vfx sfx, their ult too", and five Voidkin at launch.
- **What it is:** each launch Voidkin's basic attack (M1) gets its own effect and its own sound, so no two share a hit. The bar is Anime Vanguards' units at the game's camera: every attack reads as that character's, with flash, at a glance.
- **The order:**
  1. Watt, with Ground Wire's retake (AR11);
  2. Rook;
  3. Pip;
  4. Lyric;
  5. Kirra, with First Strike's look (AR10).
- **For each, one `/ga-action`:**
  - the feel line, signed by research;
  - one hero shape with a direction and an acceleration, drawn from the Voidkin's weapon and element (or its accent where it has none);
  - the grey block beside the combo's clip;
  - research's verdict in motion from a phone capture.
- **Its sound** is layered like AR13's cues, its own on top of the shared hit.
- **The signatures already made** (Haymaker, Lantern Burst, Held Note) are raised to this bar at the finish pass, with the redesigns.
- **Light on purpose:** a basic attack lands many times a minute, so its effect is short and small beside the signature's. Its flash fits inside the hit's own 0.1 to 0.2 s, and it never hides the Shade.
- **Waits:** the redesigns of Rook, Kirra, Pip and Lyric, at the finish pass. Each one's attack is redrawn for its new weapon then, and its motion and timing carry over.
- **Time it:** each attack records what it took. The cap is an effect's 2 hours at its block.
- **The director's word of 08:00:** no new sound effects until the director's samples come (`docs/decisions.md`). Each attack's sound waits for that one retry. The effect and the clip go on.

### Research's feel lines, signed (Quaestor, 2026-11-17, 08:06)

These are written from each combo's clip and its timings in `Shared.Definitions.Actions`, so each `/ga-action` starts signed. **For each, the block holds:** the hero shape on each of the three hits, in grey beside the combo's clip, at the game's camera. **What waits:** the colour pass after the block's verdict, the sound for the retry, and the redraw for the new weapon at the redesign. A maker who finds a line wrong in motion says so at the blockout, with what it would change.
- **The camera, for every line (corrected 08:16, from Watt's measures):** the game's framing shows the Shade's back, with the lead beyond it (`Combat.Lead.Frame` 45). A melee hero shape has to read with the Shade standing between the camera and its touch.
1. **Watt** (Storm; the spear-staff with three white discs): each of his three blows cracks Storm on the touch, short and hard. On the third, the drive into the street earths it in one brief spit of light along the ground. From the game's camera, he hits like a breaker tripping: contained, earthed, never a mage's spray. Apart from Ground Wire: no ring and no hold, since that's the signature's.
2. **Rook** (no element; his accent is the gauntlet's orange band): the jab and the hook snap a tight burst off the knuckles. The ram drives a short cone of dust and light through the Shade, and it opens the space it pushes. From the game's camera, a street fight, fists first, with the third hit heavy.
3. **Pip** (Fire; the lantern): each flick throws a spitting ember that streaks into the Shade and pops in a small flame bloom. The third, the lantern kicked over, sends a fatter one with a short ribbon behind it. From across the street, a schoolboy's fireworks that still land. Apart from Lantern Burst: no area, the shot ends on its target.
4. **Lyric** (Storm; the two speaker drones): each speaker fires a pulse you can see travel, speaker to Shade, and it bursts there on the beat. The third, both at once, lands as two pulses crossing on the same frame. From the game's camera, her hits keep time like a song. Apart from Watt's Storm: a travelling ring of sound, never a crack in the ground. Apart from Held Note: no cone.
5. **Kirra** (no element; her scarf's colour): the dash leaves a thin streak in the scarf's colour. Each cut is a crisp line across the Shade that snaps shut. The second wears as an X for a beat, gone before the Shade recovers, and the rise flashes up her blades (re-signed 10:59, below). From the game's camera, she's already past when the cuts show. With First Strike, the critical's number is still the only extra cue (Faber's tier 1, 2026-11-14).

### Watt's basic attack: the crack, its parts and the grey block (Modulo, 2026-11-17, from 07:58 by the clock)

`/ga-action` steps 1 and 3, on research's line 1. An ordinary action, not a signature. `tools/blender/watt_combo_fx.py` (plain `py`) reads the delivered `Watt_Combo.json` on the recipe's spear joint, and `WattCombo`'s Beats from `Definitions.Actions`.
- **Where the touches are** (`... measure`, his root's frame):
  - **Hit1 (21):** the shaft is shoved across his chest, level at 3.2 up, 2.1 ahead.
  - **Hit2 (36):** the butt comes up under the Shade's chest, 3.4 up and 2.6 ahead, as the blade wheels down past his right side.
  - **Hit3 (60):** the butt is driven into the street 1.1 ahead of him, 0.8 to his right.
  - The Shade stands 3.5 ahead (Range less Approach), its chest's face 2.76 ahead, its mask 0.2 proud of that.

### Research's technique check on Watt's crack (Quaestor, 2026-11-17, 08:16)

- **The techniques are right.** A crack is a flat line of light by nature, so Beams draw it without faking depth on a card. The touch's light and the discs' tick are the right partners: the light carries the hit where the line is hidden, and the tick ties his basic attack to Ground Wire.
- **The finding is research's own, from Modulo's measures:** line 1 said "from behind", but the game's framing (`Combat.Lead.Frame` 45, accepted in TR3) shows the Shade's back, so the touch is on its far side. The line now reads "from the game's camera". A crack you only see as a 0.6 to 1.0 stud tip is a hero shape that isn't seen.
- **Neither of the two ways out:** longer lines turn "contained" into the spray the line rules out, and the framing is accepted for every lead, not to be reopened for one attack.
- **A third way, tried in the block: draw the crack nearer the camera.** Set `ZOffset` on the crack's Beams, starting at about 1.5 studs, so at 215 and 145 the line can draw over the Shade's silhouette at the touch while keeping its length.
  - Its evidence is weak, so this finding is UNVERIFIED. Roblox's API page gives `ZOffset` no description. Its samples use it to layer beams, and on `ParticleEmitter` "-1" is commented "render slightly behind the actual position". Whether it draws past a body's depth is what the block shows.
  - **Faber's block:** the rows as delivered, plus the same rows with `ZOffset` 1.5 and 2.5 on the crack's Beams, from 215 and 145. One take each, with `FreezeAt` 2 and 5 frames after each hit.
  - **If it works:** the crack is seen whole and stays contained. The content check's condition 1 then reads the captures for a line laid over the Shade's head.
  - **If it doesn't:** the light and the spit carry the read at 215, and the crack stays as delivered. The verdict in motion judges it then.
- **The block can go ahead:** this doesn't hold the grey block, it adds two takes to it.

### Research's calls on the rows and the two asks (Quaestor, 2026-11-17, 10:36)

- **Rook, past the two-rounds line: keep these rows, with the spacing ask.** None of the three rounds came from the burst or the cone. Each one was a target that no guard had tested yet: a path into the body, three masks unread, then the elite and the Lamplighter. The idea itself hasn't been judged yet, because no one has seen it in motion. The guard now reads every target and mask the game has, so that class of fault is closed. Only the spacing ask fixes the fist itself: rows per target, or touches placed from the surface, still leave the clip's fist inside a big target. Neither is taken. If one more guard fault turns up, the rows stop, and the options go to the director with this evidence.
- **The spacing ask: approved for research (tier 2).** A melee lead stands off its target by the target's own depth, so its weapon meets the surface on any size. Modulo's seed stands: the target's torso face before its root, less the ordinary Shade's 0.74. Faber judges how it fits `slotFor`. Until it's in, Watt's and Rook's rows play on the ordinary Brawler only, as the content check says.
- **The flights: approved for research (tier 2).** Pip's ember and Lyric's pulse travel as a Streak to the target's belly times its Scale, and stop before its own face, recomputed each frame. Their pop and burst play at their own size, never grown with the target. A projectile lands on what it hits, so this is the right technique, not a workaround.
- **Also accepted:** the gate for the Guard, Dasher and Support masks (they join Definitions only once every attack passes them), and the `ZOffset` takes stay closed until the face test's gap b is in. Watt's chin goes with heading 0 into condition 1's stills, as the content check says.
- **Next for research:** the block's verdict in motion on each lead's rows, once Faber installs them, from the game's camera at phone size.

### Pip's basic attack: the spit, the ember and the pop (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 08:48)

Made on research's line 3, with its camera of 08:16. `tools/blender/pip_combo_fx.py` reads the delivered `Pip_Combo.json` on the recipe's lamp joint, and checks PipCombo's beats (10, 26, 62) against the clip's markers.
- **Where the shot leaves and lands** (`measure`, `screen`):
  - **The lantern** spits beside his head on each beat, and kicks back on the frame after. On the third, it starts to go over as the beat lands.
  - **The spacing:** he fights from 13.5 studs, but a Shade walks in on him, so it may stand anywhere from its own reach to 13.5 when a shot goes.
  - **From 215** the shot comes at the camera. At 13.5 the ember shows for its first 70% (0.15 of the screen's height), then the Shade covers it, and covers where it lands. With the Shade closed in to 5, the flight is hidden at 215.
  - **From 285 and 28** the whole flight shows, 0.3 to 0.46 of the screen.
- **The hero is the ember**, from the lantern's spit to the pop. It leaves fast, speeds up into the Shade, and pops there.
- **The spit** (`PipShots`, his own rows, on each beat at the lantern's place on that frame, 0.35 studs out toward the Shade):
  - 4 short lines (5 on the third), 0.55 to 0.85 studs long (0.8 to 1.2 on the third), in a narrow cone at the Shade, out in a frame and gone by 5;
  - 5 sparks (8 on the third) thrown at the Shade and dropping away;
  - a light.
  - The builder's three Streaks stay in the file until the ember flies.
- **The ember's look** (`PipEmber`, and `PipEmberHeavy` for the third: rows on the flight's head, -Z its way):
  - **The tail,** a Beam trail back to where the head was a fraction of a frame before, so it grows as the ember speeds up. As it lands from 13.5 it runs 3.6 studs a frame, which makes the tail 2.2 studs (0.22 wide). The third's is 2.9 (0.45 wide), the "fatter one".
  - **The third's ribbon,** a second, softer trail behind it: 4.6 studs, 0.9 wide, half see-through.
  - **A light** carried with it.
  - **Its spits:** sparks left behind it in the air, which hang and fade in 0.08 to 0.15 s and never fall.
  - From 5 studs, the trails are about 0.6 of that.
- **The ember's flight is Faber's,** since rows in his frame can't follow a target that moves. **The ask, tier 2, for research and Faber:** a Streak row that travels with the ember's rows on its head, then plays its pop, and lands its hit when it arrives (as Held Note's crest takes its hit through `Struck`). DESIGN seeds, tuned live; the names are Faber's to choose:
  - **Flight:** from the lantern's mouth at 60 studs a second, speeding up at 1,600 a second squared. It lands in 6 frames from 13.5 studs, and in 3 from 5.
  - **Where it lands:** it flies at the target's belly, 2.4 studs up from its feet times the target's Scale (3.24 on the elite, 3.6 on the Lamplighter), and stops 0.25 before the target's own torso face. That's recomputed on each frame from where the target stands. A Shade that walks in during the flight never ends up with the ember inside it.
  - **On arrival:** the head stops, the tail closes onto it within a frame, and the ember's rows go as the pop plays. The ribbon doesn't linger: it's gone within 3 frames, under the pop's flash, and never stands as a bar against the body. If a Beam's transparency can run along its length, it fades from the belly end first. Nothing stays in the body.
  - **If the target dies mid-flight,** the ember pops in the air where it is, or fades.
  - **The pop:** `PipPop` plays there, facing the flight made level, at scale 1 for shots 1 and 2 and 1.4 for the third, whatever the target's size. It's never grown with the target. Grown by the elite's 1.35, its petals touch the elite's arm mask by about 0.06 studs, and grown by the Lamplighter's 1.5, its pole.
  - **The hit:** its hold, push and number land with the pop. The server's damage stays on the beat.
  - **The same shape would serve Lyric's** travelling pulse (line 4).
- **The pop** (`PipPop`, its own rows, played where the ember lands):
  - **The bloom opens to the sides:** 6 short petals (1.0 and 0.65 studs) off the Shade's front, tipped back toward him, three a side, from 80 to 150 degrees off up. A petal nearer up meets the lower masks: Support's foot is 3.30 up and Dasher's 3.06. A petal nearer down runs toward the street.
  - **Three sparks flung out past its outline:** 2.8 studs straight out to his left, and 2.8 out to his right, tipped toward him. The third is short and low to his left, 1.6 studs.
  - **A crackle in the air,** in two sprays of 5 sparks thrown back toward him and out to each side. Each is slowed hard, barely falls, and shrinks and fades to nothing within 0.8 studs of the bloom, between 2.2 and 2.9 studs up.
  - **A light,** which rims the Shade from his side.
- **Rule 4, in the script, which fails the rows:**

### Lyric's basic attack: the ring of sound, the pulse and the burst (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 09:10)

Made on research's line 4, with its camera of 08:16. `tools/blender/lyric_combo_fx.py` reads the delivered `Lyric_Combo.json` on the recipe's two speaker joints, and checks LyricCombo's beats (15, 30, 60) against the clip's markers.
- **Where the shots leave and land** (`measure`):
  - **Each speaker drives forward into its beat** and stops on it: the right over frames 10 to 14, the left over 25 to 29, both over 53 to 59, at up to 18 to 25 studs a second. The right fires on 1, the left on 2, both on 3.
  - **The spacing:** she fights from 11 studs (Range 12 less Approach). A Shade walks in on her, so it can be as close as 5.
  - **The flight, at 11:** 10.7 studs. At 215 and 145 the Shade hides its last quarter to two fifths. The rest shows, 0.15 to 0.22 of the screen's height. From 285 and 28 it all shows.
  - **The flight, at 5:** the speaker on the far side of the framing is hidden.
- **The hero is the ring of sound,** from the speaker's mouth to the burst.
- **The speaker's thrust launches it.** The ring leaves 5 frames before the beat and lands on it, whatever the distance. So the burst is on the beat, the hit needs no wait, and her hits keep time.
- **Three parts, each where it can live:**
  - **`LyricShots`** (her own rows, 12): at each firing speaker, from 5 frames before the beat, a ring leaves its mouth (the kit's hoop, 0.35 to 0.7 studs, out and fading in 5 frames) with a light. Both follow the ring's own path through the clip as the speaker drives forward. The builder's four Streaks stay until the pulse flies.
  - **`LyricPulse`** (3 rows, on the flight's head): the ring of sound, 0.4 swelling to 0.5 studs, a faint line back along its flight, and a light. Its frame faces the target square, so it comes as a wavefront, whatever the flight's pitch.
  - **`LyricBurst`** (2 rows, played where a pulse lands, facing the Shade's front): the ring thrown wide and low before the Shade's belly. It opens from 0.9 by 0.45 studs (half widths across and up) to 3.4 by 0.85 in 6 frames, gone by 9, with a light. The hoop's rim is 0.7 to 1 of that.
  - **`LyricBurstBoth`** (3 rows, the third): both pulses land on the same frame, and their two rings open crossed, rolled 9 degrees either way, under one light.
- **How I read "crossing":** the two pulses meet on the same frame, and their rings open as a low X. The other reading is the two flights crossing in the air: each speaker aims at the Shade's far side, so the paths cross about 2 studs out. That one is an aim offset in Faber's flight, a variant for the blockout if research wants it.
- **The flight is Faber's,** the same travelling Streak as Pip's (his section, the ask), in its land-on-the-beat way:
  - it launches 5 frames before the beat from the speaker's ring and lands on the beat, at a steady speed (128 studs a second from 11, 65 from 5);
  - it lands 1.9 studs up times the target's Scale and 0.4 before the target's own torso face, recomputed each frame from where the target stands;
  - on arrival it plays `LyricBurst` facing the target's front, at its own size, never grown with the target. On the third, one `LyricBurstBoth` plays between where the two land;
  - the hit is on the beat, so it lands with the burst;
  - on arrival, `LyricPulse`'s trail (2 frames, about 4.3 studs, aimed at the belly) closes onto the ring within a frame and never stands as a bar against the body, as Pip's ember arrives;
  - if the target dies mid-flight, it bursts in the air or fades.
- **Off the street and under the masks:** Ground Wire's ring is on the street, so this one never is. The burst's top stays under the lowest mask's foot (Dasher's, 3.06 up).
- **Rule 4, in the script (`guard`, which fails the rows),** against every target as Pip's (`combo_targets.py`: the ordinary Shade under each of its four masks, the elite, the Lamplighter), each landed on as the flight lands:
  - no point comes within 0.1 studs of a target's part, or within 0.15 of the street;
  - no point draws over a face, tested on its triangles from 148 views, every 0.05 studs;
  - it holds with the Shade at 11 and at 5;
  - it covers the burst at every size from its first to its widest, both rims, both rolls, and the pulse over its last 1.2 studs.

  Before that last approach the pulse is in the open street between them. At her speaker it's hers.
- **Seen, the burst at its widest:**
  - **At 215,** 14 to 36% of its rims show (11 to 36% with the Shade at 5): the side past the Shade's arm, and its foot beside the legs.
  - **At 145,** the mirror: 12 to 35%.
  - **At 285 and 28,** all of it.
- **What's still weak, for the block to judge in motion:**
  - A 5-frame flight is quick to read as travel. If it doesn't read, a longer flight launches earlier, before the speaker's drive starts, from a speaker standing still.
  - The burst sits at the Shade's belly and hips, lower than the line's "bursts there", to keep under the masks.
- **The content check (its report at 10:08): PASS for the grey block.** The burst's top stays under every mask: 2.75 up on the ordinary Shade, 3.41 on the elite and 3.7 on the Lamplighter. Its conditions:
  - the trail's arrival rule, now in the flight above (the guard doesn't test the trail);
  - **the captures:** at 64 px over dark, from 215, 145, 285 and 28, with one take on the elite and one on the Lamplighter. The burst's centre (1.9 up) is at the legs' top (2.0), so its first frame must read as a ring round the waist, not a glow at the groin.

  The guard now lands the pulse on each target as the flight does (Pip's fix), with the low camera. The rows pass unchanged.
- **Delivered (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 10:15 by the files' time):** `build/handoff/Lyric/fx/`: `LyricShots`, `LyricPulse`, `LyricBurst` and `LyricBurstBoth`.
- **What waits:**
  - the flight, for Faber's Streak;
  - the colour pass after the block's verdict (Storm's light);
  - the sound, for the director's retry;
  - the redraw for her new weapon at the redesign.
- **Time:** 08:48 to 08:53 and 09:04 to 09:10 by the clock, around Pip's fixes. The targets' guard ran from 09:12 to 09:39, shared with Rook's and Pip's, and left the rows unchanged. The cap is 2 hours at its block.

### Kirra's basic attack: the streak, the two cuts and the X (Modulo, 2026-11-17, from 10:00 by the transcript)

Made on research's line 5. `tools/blender/kirra_combo_fx.py` reads the delivered `Kirra_Combo.json` on the recipe's blade joints, between frames as the game blends them, and checks KirraCombo's beats (12, 26, 56) against the clip's markers.
- **Where the blades cut** (`measure`, `sweeps`; the Shade 3.5, 3.9 and 4.3 off on the three beats, each push 0.4):
  - **Cut 1, the right blade,** crosses a plane 0.18 before the Shade's torso face from 3.90 up to 0.68 over frames 11.5 to 13.2, 71 degrees off level.
  - **Cut 2, the left blade,** crosses it from 4.12 to 1.07 over frames 25.25 to 26.45, 67 degrees the other way.
  - The two paths meet as a V between the legs, not an X. So the X is drawn: each line keeps its blade's angle, moved onto the torso.
  - **Hit 3:** both blades rise beside her (frames 52 to 56, 0.8 to 6.1 up) and never reach the Shade.
- **The parts (`KirraCombo`, 13 rows, grey, white light):**
  - **The streak:** a Beam trail on her root, 0.2 up and 0.55 behind her back, Lag 5, 0.12 to 0.06 wide, over the dash (frames 1 to 9), gone by 14.
  - **Cut 1:** a straight line 0.1 wide at its blade's angle, on the torso from 2.05 to 3.3 up, 0.18 before the face where the Shade stands on the beat. It rides a point held where her root is on the beat.
    - It shows 2 frames after the beat, once the blade is past, and holds 2.
    - It snaps shut by +8: its width goes to nothing and its ends draw 20% in.
    - A light rims the Shade from it.
  - **The X:** on hit 2, cut 2 and cut 1's line again cross at 2.68 up. It's crisp for 2 frames, snapped thin (0.035) by +8, worn faint, and gone at +16 (frame 42).
  - **The rise:** a light on each blade tip (`TipR`, `TipL`) flashes as they climb, frames 52 to 60. No line.
- **The content check on the design (10:50 by the transcript):**
  - **The cuts that snap shut: PASS.** Its conditions: the line never swells in its middle, it shrinks straight to nothing, nothing falls from it, it's never dark, and the light is never red.
  - **The X worn to the rise: BLOCK.** A Shade that walks into it wears it cut into its body. Its way out is taken: the X is gone while an ordinary Shade still flinches (the hitstop and 0.28 s, to about frame 45), and the rise flashes only the blades.
  - **So research re-signs line 5:** "the rise through it flashes upward" becomes the rise flashing up her blades.
  - **The X's crossing at the elite's waist: BLOCK, fixed.** Every cut's foot is now at the target's legs' top or above, so the crossing is on the torso.
- **The waist lift, an ask for Faber (tier 2, research and Faber):** a cut's held point lifts by the target's legs' top (2.0) times its Scale less one. The lines stay at their own size. That's 0.7 on the elite (its cuts 2.75 to 4.0) and 1.0 on the Lamplighter (3.05 to 4.3). As the flights land on the belly times the Scale (research's call, 10:36), the cut lands on what's hit.
  - The spacing ask (Rook's section) applies too.
  - **Until both are in, Kirra's rows play on the ordinary Brawler only,** enforced in the game, as Watt's and Rook's. As the game stands, the cuts enter the elite and the Lamplighter root to root and sit under their legs' top.
- **Rule 4, in the script (`into`, which fails the rows),** against every target in `combo_targets.py`, each spaced by its depth where it stands on the line's beat, the cuts lifted:
  - no line within 0.15 of a part, and none drawing over a face from 148 views, every 0.05 studs;
  - no cut's foot under the target's legs' top.
  - A top of 3.45 draws over the elite's arm mask from heading 280, so the top is 3.3. The streak at the knot's height drew over the Shade's face from low behind her, so it's at her waist.
  - The Dasher and Support masks fail: they wait on the gate.
- **The walk-in, as the game is:** an ordinary Brawler struck mid-swipe doesn't flinch. ShadeSwipe has no move, no cooldown and Range 4 root to root. So after hit 2's push to 4.3 it walks back only to about 3.8, one 0.2-stud step inside its range, and its torso stops 0.08 behind the X's plane.
- **Seen:** at 215 and 145 the Shade covers the cuts and the X whole; at 285 and 28 they show whole. As with Watt's, Rook's and Pip's, the lights carry the main framing.
- **The content check on the rows (10:57 by the transcript): PASS** for the grey block in the dev build's replay, on the ordinary Brawler. Left for the captures (the game's camera, full size and 64 px, over dark and noon, every heading, the low view behind her included):
  - **the Brawler struck mid-swipe, frames 26 to 42:** its swiping arm likely reaches through the X's plane at chest height, and the guard tests parts at rest. If a capture shows a line across the moving arm, the X fades out early whenever its target is mid-action;
  - a crowd pushing a Shade off its spot into cut 1 or the X;
  - frames 14 to 20 and 26 to 42 at 64 px: the X reads as light before the body, never as a mark on it.

  After its check, the cuts and the lights keep nothing from before the blade passes (the tracks hid them only from their first key). That takes away two frames of line and changes nothing it checked.
- **What's still weak, for the block to judge in motion:**
  - the main framing sees only the cuts' light;
  - the X is small and narrow (1.25 studs tall, 0.5 wide), held to the blades' steep angles in the band under the masks.
  - The variant, if it doesn't read: a flatter, wider X past the Shade's sides, which would show at 215. Its lines would leave the blades' angles.
- **Delivered (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 10:57 by the clock):** `build/handoff/Kirra/fx/KirraCombo.rows.json`. The rise rides `TipR` and `TipL`, the recipe's blade points; Faber checks both are attachments in her model (the .rbxm is compressed, so I couldn't read them).
- **What waits:**
  - the waist lift and the spacing ask;
  - the finish in the scarf's Sky (#47A6F5), with the lights white or Sky, never red, and its own content check before any upload;
  - the sound, for the director's retry.
- **Time:** 10:00 to 10:58 by the transcript and the clock, with Rook's, Pip's and Lyric's delivery and research's calls between. The cap is 2 hours at its block.

## AR27. The idles without the glance (Modulo, then Faber)

Research's request, 2026-11-17, 10:10, on the director's word (`docs/decisions.md`): "this weird tic in their idle animation where they head tilt on the side".
- **The cause, read in the clip scripts:** Pip's, Lyric's, Kirra's and Watt's idles each turn the head 40° to 70° aside once a loop, with a 4° to 6° tilt (`H` and `look` in `tools/blender/*_clips.py`, `idle`). Every loop repeats it, so it reads as a tic, not a thought. Rook's idle has none.
- **The fix, one class:** no idle turns or tilts the head on its own. The head stays level and faces front through the whole loop. Its life is the stance, the breath, and the Voidkin's own secondary motion: Pip's lantern, Lyric's chin on the beat, Kirra's scarf and tail, Watt's stillness.
- **Also check:** a head that tips with the torso's lean (Watt's idle rolls the torso). If it reads cocked from the game's camera, it counter-rolls to level.
- **Feel line:** from the game's camera, each Voidkin waits in its own stance and breathes, and nothing in its loop catches the eye as a twitch.
- **Scope:** the four idles, re-exported, then Faber's upload and install. **Waits:** the redesigns redraw the idles whole, keeping this rule.
- **Order:** after the AR26 attack in hand. A small edit to each script: a block, not a signature.
- **Research's check:** one loop of each from the game's camera, at phone size, in motion.

### The four idles re-made (Modulo, 2026-11-17, 11:00 to 11:07 by the clock)

- **The rule as a check:** `tools/blender/idle_head.py` reads each lead's exported idle and measures the head the game plays (the clip's Torso and Head on R6's RootJoint and Neck), frame by frame against her root: its turn, nod and tilt. It fails a turn past 4° or a tilt past 2°. It also tests the redesigns' idles, which keep this rule.
- **Before (the delivered idles):** Kirra turned 68° (frame 152), Pip 57° with an 11° tilt (120), Lyric 69° with 15° (176), and Watt 62° (166). Rook, as the control: 0.2° and 1.7°.
- **The edits, in each `idle()` in `tools/blender/*_clips.py`:**
  - **Pip:** the glance is gone. The lantern still perks up and turns to him, and his body leans to it a little (the torso 14° round, where it was 17°).
  - **Lyric:** the glance at her monitor is gone, and a fourth beat takes its frames, so her chin keeps time on 0, 60, 120 and 180. Her hub's 2° tilt, which each nod deepened past level, is 0 in the idle.
  - **Kirra:** the look back along her scarf is gone. Her bounce runs on through the loop, and the tail still lifts at 166, a flag in her own wind.
  - **Watt:** the look along the street is gone, and a second, shallower breath takes its frames.
  - **AR27's "also check" was real:** his torso's roll cocked his head 4.8° to 6.6° all loop, so the head is counter-rolled 6°. Kirra's stance left hers 2.2° off, so she's counter-tilted 2°.
- **After:** every head within 1.3° of front and 1.2° of level. Lyric's nod is the one move left, -4.8° to +4.0°, her chin on the beat. Each export's audit passes. The lengths, the markers and the loop flag are unchanged. Pip's hat and lantern differ only where they followed his head and torso.
- **Delivered (11:07 by the clock):** `build/handoff/<Lead>/clips/<Lead>_Idle.json` for Kirra, Pip, Lyric and Watt, each with its `pack_Idle.png` and `.txt`. Nothing new is drawn: the edit only takes a move away.
- **For Faber:** the four idles uploaded under the group and installed.
- **Then research's check:** one loop of each from the game's camera, at phone size, in motion.

## AR41. The Tower's platforms: the rise, the assembly and the fall as light (Modulo, for research's pick, then Faber)

**Dropped, 2026-11-19, by 11:12** (the director's word): the Tower moves into a room of its own, so its platforms go (`docs/design.md`, "The Tower"). Nothing here is built or rendered. The room's own request is written when the main game's open pieces are done.

Research, 2026-11-19, 01:14 by the clock. This is TR29's waiting look: the platforms assembling and falling as light (`docs/test-requests.md`, TR29, "What waits"; `docs/design.md`, "The Tower"). The Tower is built in grey and runs. Faber's item 7 (its results) doesn't need this, and this doesn't need Faber until the install.
- **Feel line:** you step into the ring behind your base, and the ground lifts you on a disc of the base's own stone with light round its rim. At each win, the next disc gathers above you out of streaks of light, locks with one pulse, and you leap up to it. When your lead falls, the stack lets go and falls away as light.
- **The pieces:**
  1. **The platform** (`Config.Tower.Platform`: radius 14, 1 thick, 12 apart). It's a void machine of the base's language, never a real machine (the base's rule). Its fighting top is calm, so the lead and the foe read on it. Its rim's light sits in a dark bed (AR40's rule). It reads at 64 px as a ring.
  2. **The folded column** of older floors: one thin column of rings, so the height reads from the base.
  3. **The assembly** in 0.35 s, Back Out (TowerController's timing). One hero shape with a direction and an acceleration (the effects chapter): for example, pieces drawn in from outside and below, faster as they close, then one pulse round the rim.
  4. **The fall as light,** at a run's end: the stack gives way from the top down, in about 1 s, with no debris and nothing that reads as harm.
- **The blockout:** three directions in grey for 1 and 3, each a strip in motion from the player's camera at the rise's Leap. Research picks, then the colour pass in AR40's C, then 2 and 4 on the pick. Caps: the effect block 2 h, the model block 1 h.
- **Waits:** the foe's void-light look, since it's laid over the Voidkin and their work is parked until before launch (the director's word); the leap's arc and the camera (Faber's); the sounds; the rarity light on rewards.
- **Checks:** the content check before any upload; Blender by `build/locks/blender.lock`; the memory guard.

### Three directions, planned (Modulo, 2026-11-19, 01:18 by the clock)

**Job and rank:** a beat between floors, under a second, a reward's size, not a skill's. One hero, the gathering; the pulse is its full stop. Nothing else as loud.

**What the player's camera sees** (`lantern_row_look`: 12.5 studs, 20° down, FOV 70; the avatar `Stand` 0.55 r behind the middle):
- **On the floor,** the eye is 8.8 over the deck and 19.5 from the middle, past the edge.
- **The new disc** is 3.2 over the eye there: its underside spans the frame's top sixth, its near edge out of frame.
- **It reads whole only as the camera rises with the leap:** halfway up, the eye is over the new deck, which then fills the frame's middle.
- **So the strip** follows one take from the win to past the set-down: the gathering, the pulse, and a stand-in leap. The leap's arc is Faber's; the stand-in is a straight rise that slows to the deck, from 0.75 s to `Leap` (1.25 s).

**The beats** (seconds from the win; drawn at 30 a second):

| Beat | Time | What |
|---|---|---|
| Gather | 0 to 0.35 | the hero closes on the disc's place, accelerating (an ease-in), with Back's small overshoot at the lock |
| Lock and pulse | 0.35 to 0.6 | the rim's light flares and settles, and a short upright band lifts off the rim and fades |
| Leap | 0.75 to 1.25 | the pair rise to the new deck, the camera with them (stand-in) |
| Set down | 1.25 to 1.5 | standing on the new floor |

`TowerController` today tweens the disc's size with Back Out, which slows into the lock. The hero accelerates into it, so the install moves its pieces on an ease-in and keeps only Back's settle.

**A, the Shutter.**
- **The platform:** eight wedges of stone with dark seams, and a raised lip at the rim. The light runs in a dark groove round the lip's outer face.
- **The gathering:** the wedges fly in from twice the radius out and 2 studs below, each on a straight line, faster as they close. Each drags a short streak of light. They meet at the lock. (From 6 below, the first still had the near wedge going through the player's lens.)

**B, the Circuit.**
- **The platform:** a calm deck inside a heavy collar, with the light in the dark seam between the two. The seam runs through, so its light shows on the top and on the underside.
- **The gathering:** a head of light runs once round the rim, from slow to fast, and draws the collar behind it. Then the deck closes from the rim to the middle like an iris, also an ease-in.

**C, the Updraft.**
- **The platform:** a deck with a shallow keel under it (radius 6, 3.5 deep, clear of the avatar's place at 7.7 out) and a lit point at its tip. The light is in a dark bed on the underside at the rim.
- **The gathering:** twelve streaks rise from the lower deck's rim, accelerating, and lean in to the new rim 12 above. The disc condenses where they arrive, its rim first, and the keel last.

**In all three:**
- the deck is calm, one flat value, so the lead and the foe read on it;
- the rim's light lies in a dark bed (AR40's rule);
- the rim's light reads from below: the player's camera and the yard see the new disc from under it, so a light only on its top never shows at the lock;
- no sparks, nothing falls, nothing orange or yellow, no metal glowing;
- the light is the void's, near-white in grey;
- one pulse a floor; on fast floors that's 0.8 s apart, under one flash a second.

**The block:** in grey, in the yard at base level 2 by the game's day (AR40's C), rendered in Blender (`tools/blender/tower_block.py`):
- **From the player's camera:** on floor 5, turned toward the base, from the win to the set-down on floor 6, every frame at 30 a second, and an MP4.
- **From the yard, looking up:** behind a player in the base's back yard 45 studs off, turned up to the stack, so the three platforms and the column read as rings, at 64 px too. The beats only.
- **From above:** high over the new disc, looking down, for the content check's symbol read. The beats only.
- Each direction gets its strip, and a sheet compares the three at their beats.

### The director: the Tower is too small (2026-11-19, 01:46 by the clock)

The director, at 01:4x: "the tower is way to small dont it". Research agrees. The disc is 28 studs across (radius 14), while the Lamplighter's ring is 80. On it, the lead and the foe stand 15 apart, which crowds a Mage's 12-stud shot and its skip back. The stack of three discs 12 apart is 36 tall, so it reads as a step, not a tower.
- **The new seeds:** radius 24 (48 across) and 18 between floors. With Stand 0.55, the avatar and the foe stand 26 apart, and the three discs stand 54 tall. Thickness is the model's call, in proportion.
- **Faber's:** `Config.Tower.Platform` with item 7, after a check that 48 across fits the lot behind each base. If it doesn't fit, the largest that does, and the number goes here.
  - **Faber, 2026-11-19, 03:41: it fits, in the air, and it's in** (`Config.Tower.Platform`: Radius 24, Height 18, Thick 1.7 in proportion until the model). The lot is 32 deep, so the disc overhangs it by 8 on each side, 18 up at floor 1: over the path behind the yard, never the yard itself (`Rules.Tower.Reach`, its logic cases), over 11 of the 16 that are its own half of the gap toward the crossing, and past the ground's old far edge, where the base's ground now runs on 27 farther so a run left off the far side lands on it. TR29's case 46/0; floor 4 read from the player's camera.
- **Modulo's:** the three directions are planned at the new size (the camera's distances in "Three directions, planned" move with it). The strips wait for memory (the director, 01:34: "Wait").

## AR48. The element states' cues: Burn, Chill and Freeze, Shock, Sap (Modulo, for research's pick, then Faber)

Research, 2026-11-19, 22:58 by the clock. The states are designed (`docs/design.md`, "States in a fight", tier 2 of 2026-11-12) and wait in TR7. TR49 gives every Voidkin and every Shade an element, so every fight will carry one. These cues make an element read on the body it struck, not only in the number.

- **Feel line:** a Frost lead's third hit lands, and frost creeps up the Shade's legs. Its swings slow, then a shell of ice snaps shut round it. A Fire hit leaves embers rising off the struck for a few seconds, a Storm hit leaves light crackling over it, and a Void hit leaves dark motes falling round it, the heal it receives cut.
- **The pieces,** one cue per state, on the Shade (R6, the robe set) and on a Voidkin of open legs:
  1. **Burn (Fire):** embers rising off the body for the burn's time.
  2. **Chill:** frost creeping up from the feet in steps as the chill fills. **Freeze:** a shell of ice that shuts round the body, holds, and breaks at its end.
  3. **Shock (Storm):** crackling light over the body while it holds.
  4. **Sap (Void):** dark motes sinking round the body.
- **Their colours** are the elements' own (`Config.UIKit`, Fire `#FF8A3D`, Frost `#8FE3FF`, Storm `#FF6FD8`, Void `#B58CFF`). Sap is dark first: its violet is a thin rim on the motes, never a cloud (the "too purple" line).
- **The measures:** `handbook/visuals/vfx.md` ("Brief an effect", "Value and colour", "On any floor", "Don't"). One hero shape each, with a direction and an acceleration. Each must read at phone size on Lantern Row's paving by day and over the dark Shade. A state is a status, so it reads quieter than a hit's spark and never hides the fighter's silhouette.
- **Modulo's:** the four as grey block-ins, then their colour, shown in motion beside a plain hit in one strip sheet, in under 2 hours (the effect cap). Then research's pick, and the content check before any upload.
- **Faber's, after TR49:** the states' rules (`Rules.Fight`, the numbers in Config from `handbook/feel/combat.md`), with cases, then these cues on them.
- **Waits:** poise and the stagger ring (the states' other half), the elements' sounds, a signature's own state (Rill's Cold Snap keeps its own look).

### The block-in: four cues beside a plain hit, grey then colour (Modulo, 2026-11-19, before AR49's pause, and 2026-11-20, 00:07 to 00:13 by the clock)

- **The sheet:** `build/scratch/modulo/ar48/element_states.png` (grey rows, then colour, every tile at the smallest phone's pixels, off/on counts by the handbook), with `element_states_close.png` at twice the pixels and `element_states_64.png` (every colour frame 64 pixels across, on the dark).
  - Shown on the Shade and Rook from the player's camera on Lantern Row's paving by day, with a grey stand-in lead.
  - Made by `tools/blender/element_states_fx.py` (`Shade`, `Rook`, then `sheet`). Each body is one process, so a run stays under 1.1 GB.
- **Hero shapes, one each:**
  - **Burn:** embers rising off the body, lifted fast, then drifting and shrinking out, 3 s.
  - **Chill:** a sleeve of ice round the legs, its top a crown of teeth, climbing a step a frost hit (three steps 0.8 s apart, feet to hips).
  - **Freeze:** a faceted shell. It snaps up out of the sleeve and shuts in 0.1 s, its edges flash once, and it holds clear for 2 s with a glint crossing it. Then it breaks into chips about half a facet's size, flung out and falling in 0.4 s.
  - **Shock:** short jagged arcs crawling over the body, three frames each, in bursts about three a second.
  - **Sap:** dark motes circling in round the upper body and sinking slowly, each round with a thin violet rim on its top, dark first.
    - The content check (00:11) blocked the first take: its motes fell under gravity and stretched with a trail, which can read as dark drips off a struck body.
    - Now each mote eases out, never speeds up, stays a circle, and shrinks to a point above the knees, never reaching the ground.
  - Each state is quieter than the hit's spark, and the silhouette shows through every frame. The ice is laid at 0.42 for the fill.
- **The fix from the paused take:** the ice's facet edges drew black and red lines. An edge thinner than a pixel blended with the fill in the ID pass, matched neither key, and left bare body showing through the frost.
  - The ID pass is now by object colour, with everything that isn't ice in black, and the keys are unmixed in linear light.
  - The edges went from 0.045 to 0.07 studs (the sleeve's from 0.04 to 0.06), so they read as pale ice lines.
- **Modulo's read:** at 64 pixels, only Frost and the hit's spark read; Sap is a few dark specks and Shock a few pink ticks. At the phone's own pixels all four read on both bodies. A state is meant to read quieter than a hit, so this is research's call: say if Sap or Shock should read louder at phone size.
- **Content check:** first BLOCK on Sap only (00:11, the falling drops), then PASS on the re-render at 00:12, all five rows. For the build: Sap's motes stay round and easing out, and a fall or a streak needs a fresh check. Burn's rim stays orange, never crimson, and its embers never fall or settle.
- **Next:** research's pick, then Faber after TR49's states.

### Research's verdict on the block-in (Quaestor, 2026-11-20, 00:13)

- **Felt:** Frost is the star. The ice climbs the legs, slams shut into a faceted shell and bursts into chips, and you'd know it from across the street. Sap is right for a status: dark, quiet and round, with its violet only a rim. Burn and Shock don't say their element yet.
- **Tier: works. Frost and Sap are competent and go to colour as they are.** The one bold thing: the shell's shatter.
- **Must-fixes:**
  1. **Burn reads as spots, not fire (class: every rising particle):** round orange dots spread evenly over the body, the Shade's mask included, look like a rash. Embers are small tapered sparks stretched along their rise, a white-hot core to orange, flickering, born near the hit and the shoulders, climbing and fading above the head, with a few larger ones. None sits still on a face.
  2. **Shock is too small to read (class: every arc):** the pink squiggles read as stitched crosses. Arcs jump between two points on the body, two to three times longer, with a white core in the pink glow, and one larger arc per burst. It should read at the 64 px view, where it's only ticks now.
- **The 64 px question:** Frost and the hit read, which is enough for the far view. Sap stays quiet by design. Shock must read there (fix 2). Burn reads by its shape, not by size (fix 1).
- The second look judges Burn and Shock alone, at the phone's pixels and at 64 px.
