  - Every beat lands on its frame: the caster's ball at -14, the beam at 0, the mint star at the brawler at 3, the dome at 8, its fade by 16, gone by 58.
  - Short of the target: the star at 3 is smaller, with less white mass round it, and the motes on the brawler at 30 don't show.
  - **Status:** implemented and taken in play. Visually reviewed by me only. Research's look waits. Not director-accepted.
- **Research's half checkpoint (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 18:53; the same sheet, in both orders): passes.**
  - **Felt:** the Support's ball, the beam to the Brawler, a mint star and its shell, gone by 58, each on its frame.
  - **Gates (MEASURED: pixels 40 or more above the frame at 58, the same cells):** the mend's peak at 3 is 82% of the target's area (18,366 against 22,364), its mint 90%. At 8 the shell is 115%. The link and the caster's light, the main read, carry.
  - **For the final, one fix, a bug rather than polish:** the motes at 30 don't draw (mint 173 px against the target's 1,788), so a layer that's built but unseen gets checked. The star's size at 3 isn't a must-fix.
  - **Next:** the final, from QA's pack once QA joins.
- **The motes' fix (Scintilla, 2026-11-26, 19:02, tested in play 19:05 to 19:17; for Faber's review):** the half take showed them drawn but unseen. In play on the deck (`ward/takes/ar58_motes*.mkv`, scratch): they rose only 1 to 1.7 studs, small, and at LightEmission 1 a mint added onto the light deck washes out to white, so they showed only while in front of the dark body. `SupportHeal.json`'s Motes now: 14 in four bursts from 4 to 13, Speed 11 to 17 at Drag 3.5, Size 1.0 to 0.85, a light mint (150, 245, 180) at LightEmission 0.15, laid over the scene. Seen in four mends: soft mint motes from the shoulder to beside the mask at 16, 24 and 30, fainter over the floor. Measured at 30 as research did (40 above frame 58, mint): 740 and 14 px with 10 motes, 401 and 118 with 14, against the target's 1,646; which motes fall behind the body varies by mend. A light mint over a light floor rises about 25 levels, under the 40. They show, but softer than the target's bright points: for research at the final.
- **Found for the final (Scintilla, 2026-11-26, 22:59; from the code, not seen in play):** `SupportShield.json`'s Veil, the point its ribbons ride, is written to turn slowly (Spin 0 to 1200 over 3600 frames), but it's a `Ground` row, and Ground takes the effect's facing in place of the row's rotation. So the ribbons have never turned, and every checkpoint judged them still. Research's call at the final: keep them still and drop the Spin, or turn them by a still Ground point with a turning point riding it.

## AR59. Rook, redesigned to the anime bar (Modulo, for research's pick and the director's look, then its finish; Faber's install after)

Research, 2026-11-23, 22:53, on the director's pick by 22:52 (`docs/decisions.md`). Tier 2: a look players see, in Modulo's area and then Faber's. It goes into the game only on the director's acceptance. The first of the launch five's redesigns. The order: Rook, Kirra, Pip, Lyric, then Watt's finish (AR11). Each is its own request, written when the one before closes.
- **Why Rook first:** he's the lead every new player holds, in every fight of the first session and on the lead's card. He was drawn before the bar of 2026-11-15 (`docs/roster.md`, "The look"; AR1 has his history).
- **Feel line:** in the first fight, the player looks at their lead and sees a character from an anime's cast. The hair's silhouette reads first, then a sharp face, then a jacket with one asymmetry, then one weapon with style. It's a Common: simple, and never corny.
- **What holds:**
  - his row in `docs/roster.md`: Common, Red, Warrior, no element, Haymaker's function, and Second Wind (its look line is a seed);
  - the roster's look rules: a character, not a job; hair as the identity; a face of his own; the R6 body;
  - his rig and today's clips;
  - violet only on what's alive (AR40).
- **The block contains:** three directions in the game's own light, each shown:
  - full body from the player's camera in a fight on the deck, at the smallest phone's size;
  - the bust at the size of the lead card's portrait;
  - the silhouette at 64 pixels;
  - beside today's Rook and a Shade.
- **Checks:**
  1. The content check before building.
  2. Research's pick, with fresh eyes. It's a signature piece, judged blind against the bar, never against its own sheet.
  3. The director's look at the pick.
  4. The finish.
  5. The director's acceptance before Faber installs it.
- **What waits:** the basic attack's clip, effect and sound (AR26, not covered); the portrait's render, after the finish; Faber's install, in his order.
- **Time:** a signature piece gets the time it needs. The blockout is about 3 hours, as a guide.
- **Order:** Modulo's next, after AR58's must-fix.

### Modulo's blockout: three directions (2026-11-23, 23:48; started 23:19)

Sheet: `build/handoff/Rook/takes/rook_takes.png` (1900 x 2882), and its five parts beside it: `_full`, `_bust`, `_fight`, `_row`, `_small`. Script: `tools/blender/rook_takes.py` (modes `takes`, `rook`, `shade`, `sheet`). Rough meshes on the R6 body, flat colours, never finished.
- **A, the stray:** wild black hair swept up and over to one side, one clump of it red. A bone cropped jacket lined red over a black top, its right sleeve cut off at the shoulder. A dark iron gauntlet with a red ram, 1.15 of today's. A taped left hand, the tape's end swinging. Amber eyes, a grin.
- **B, the captain:** a long teal-grey tail tied high with a red cord, two long bangs. A long navy coat worn open, lined red, over a white shirt, its right sleeve rolled. A slim steel gauntlet, 0.84 of today's. A white ribbon on the left cuff. Narrow grey eyes, a flat mouth.
- **C, the sleeper:** a heavy sand-olive mop over his left eye. A big rolled bone hood-collar over a sleeveless charcoal top, very wide oxblood trousers. A bone-white gauntlet to the elbow with a red ram, 1.4 of today's. Tape on the left forearm. One half-shut gold eye.
- **What the sheet shows, as the block asked:** each from the three-quarter front and from the game's camera; the bust in the lead card's pose, then at the card's size on the smallest phone; in a fight beside a Brawler from the player's camera at 450 pixels high; a row at the lead's distance with today's Rook; at 64 pixels over night, over the deck, and as an outline.
- **The rig holds in all three:** the gauntlet is on the right arm with a ram to slide, and something light swings from the left arm. Only the idle pose was drawn: no take has been run through the clips.
- **Content check:** the written directions first (A PASS; B and C changed before building: B's hair from crimson to teal-grey and its wrist cord to a white ribbon, C's hair from ash to sand-olive and its eye from red to gold). The drawn sheet: PASS for A, B, C and the words, on these bytes (sha1 `d71c20ff` for the whole). Its hold points for the finish:
  - all three: the ram stays a flat, matte, hard-edged plate in one colour;
  - A: the red stays hair, never a band; the jacket's back stays plain; the torso covered; one sleeve cut; plain tape on one hand; the collar's lining reads as one band with the front trim;
  - B: one teal-grey tail, always tied; the collar's red reads as a lining, never a hood; a thin cord; no lone red patch between the legs or at the vent at 64 pixels;
  - C: a smooth rolled collar joined to the top, no fur and no hanging tail; the hair stays muted and falling forward; the top covers the whole torso; Second Wind never stages him asleep.
- **What I see, before research's read:**
  - A follows the feel line's order most plainly: hair, face, the cut sleeve, the gauntlet. Light jacket against dark hair and legs gives three values at 64 pixels. Its outline is also the genre's commonest.
  - B has the outline most its own from behind, where the player's camera is. The coat's tails are a built layer, more than AR11's comparison gives a Common, and from behind he's one dark mass. His gauntlet is the smallest, so the Warrior reads least.
  - C's collar hides his mouth and chin, so his face reads least at the card's size. His gauntlet reads most.
  - My lean is A. Research picks.
- **Stand-ins:** the deck in the fight pictures is drawn (pale plates, a day sky); the camera, the light and the sizes are the game's. The hair is faceted blades, the faces flat slabs.
- **Not in the repo:** the director's example batches (2026-11-20, `docs/decisions.md`). The bar used is AR11's comparison.
- **The four facts:** made (a blockout sheet); self-reviewed at full size and at the phone's sizes; delivered for research's pick; not in the game, nothing uploaded.

### The director's word: the make is rejected, Rook is redone (Quaestor, 2026-11-23, 23:53)

- **The director, about 23:45 to 23:52** (`docs/decisions.md`, the top row): "DONT MAKE THOSE MODEL WITH THOSE STYLE", "LOOK AT ANIME VANGUARD ANIME ADVENTURE THEN REDO". The rejection is of the make, and all three takes share it. Nothing of A, B or C carries over.
- **Research's miss:** AR11's comparison (2026-11-15, "The comparison") had already found how Anime Vanguards makes a unit. AR59 never named that make, and research picked A at competent within it, with fresh eyes given no benchmark.
- **OBSERVED,** from `build/reference/anime-vanguards/` (`sheet_low.png`, `compare_bodies.png`, `compare_heads.png`, `kokashi-*.webp` and `todays-strongest-*.webp` at full size; Modulo's own read agrees). Anime Adventures: no pictures found yet, so UNVERIFIED.
  1. **The body:** plain R6 blocks at their own size. The clothes are drawn on the blocks' faces, in two or three tones with soft shade, ink lines, folds, seams, trims and fastenings.
  2. **The head:** Roblox's round head, smooth. The face is drawn: almond eyes with a coloured iris and a highlight, a heavy upper lash line, thin brows, a tick for the nose, a short mouth, and the fringe's shadow on the brow.
  3. **The hair:** the one sculpted piece. Dozens of thin tapering locks, smooth-shaded from root to tip, about 1.3 to 1.6 heads wide, rising above the crown.
  4. **Built pieces:** few and thin (a blade, a headband, a mask, a hat). Nothing is chunkier than the limb it sits on. Weapons are slim and long.
  5. **Colour:** two or three large clear areas, light against dark.
- **AR59's takes against them:** garments built as thick volumes, a cube head, slab eyes, faceted blades for hair, a gauntlet up to 1.4 times today's, flat facets in the render.
- **The redo, DESIGN** (the make is taken; no unit's design, face, outfit or picture is):
  1. **The make:** the five points above. It's the make for every redesign, from Rook to Watt's finish.
  2. **The design:** a side-cast archetype of the genre's famous shows, made our own (the director's "basics or our own"). Rook stays Common, Red, a Warrior, and keeps Haymaker's function and Second Wind.
  3. **The weapon:** the oversized gauntlet goes. His fists are a glove or wraps drawn on the arm, with at most one thin built piece, no thicker than his forearm. Whether `Rook_Piston` stays in the rig, so the clips keep their keys, or is dropped is Modulo's technical call. Haymaker's weight is carried by the clip and the effect, never by size.
  4. **The takes:** two or three, each with its face, hair and clothes drawn in the make. A rough mesh is read as the style, so none is rough.
  5. **The render:** smooth shading, in a light close to the game's. No flat facets.
- **Checks:**
  1. Each take beside Anime Vanguards units at the same height, internally (never uploaded or published), and at the card's portrait and from the player's camera at the smallest phone's size.
  2. Fresh eyes given that same Anime Vanguards sheet as its example.
  3. The content check, with the Fighters test (`docs/decisions.md`, "Fighters").
  4. Research's pick, then the director's look, then the finish.
- **Time:** the make kit gets the time it needs, as the make for the whole cast. Rook's takes follow, as a guide 2 to 3 hours.

### Modulo's redo: the kit, and three takes in the make (2026-11-24, 01:19; started 2026-11-23, 23:53)

- **The kit:** `tools/blender/anime_make.py`, the make for every Voidkin from here. `Block` is one of R6's blocks with a picture drawn on each face (`Paint`: shapes, lines and soft shade laid in studs); `Head` is Roblox's round head with its picture wrapped round it, and `draw_face` draws the anime face from a few measures (the eyes' size, tilt and lid, the iris, the brows, the mouth); `Hair` is one mesh. A plain stand-in with no design shows the make (`stand_in`), and `turnaround` draws a head from six sides, to judge a hair by.
- **The hair, twice sent back by the director** (to Modulo, 2026-11-24: "the hair need a rework tbh" on the first stand-in, then "do research on how to make good hair" on the second). The first was a cap of flat clumps; the second, 60 to 74 thin spikes standing off the head in even rings, read as spines.
- **What the research found** (OBSERVED on the reference's units in `build/reference/anime-vanguards/`, and READ in the sources below; none of it measured on players):
  - **Few clumps, and broad.** A reference head shows 20 to 30 from one side. Each is a third to nearly half a head wide and half to most of a head long. The drawing guides start from "3–6 bigger chunks" and name our fault: stiff hair "usually happens when you draw too many short clumps sticking straight out".
  - **Three sizes, never even.** Big, middling and small in turn; a big clump parts into one or two points toward its end.
  - **Laid like roof tiles.** Each clump "should clearly sit over another", and volume comes from "gaps between clumps and how they overlap". A clump is "a curved shape that hugs the skull": it lies on the head and only its tip turns out.
  - **One origin.** Clumps "start at the crown and fan outward", all turned one way round it.
  - **A leaf, not a thorn.** Wide near the root, a long convex taper to a sharp point, one ridge, a dark underside. The shadow goes "under overlaps and near roots".
  - **The fringe:** 3 to 6 clumps, the middle one "a bit longer", over no more than half the face; a lock before each ear.
  - **The size:** the reference's hair is 1.3 to 1.55 heads wide and stands 0.45 to 0.6 of a head over the crown.
  - **Sources:** skyryedesign.com, "How to draw anime guy hair"; clipstudio.net, "Easy guide to drawing anime hair"; design.tutsplus.com, "Create big gravity-defying anime-styled hair"; superhivemarket.com, Anime Hair Maker's documentation (a strand is a curve with a profile and a taper); studio.blender.org, the stylized character workflow ("layer the clumps more on top of each other"). Two Roblox DevForum pages and CG Cookie's article could not be read.
- **The hair now:** `Hair.clump` roots a leaf in the egg over the scalp, walks it over the head the way the hair is combed (`heading`, `bend`, or `comb`), then lets it leave the head and turn out by its point (`hold`, `flick`); `split` fans one or two smaller leaves from the same root. `Hair.row` lays a row of them, big, middling and small in turn. Rows go on from the nape up, each further off the head than the one below. A leaf's three faces are smooth along it and sharp across it, and are wound to face out (the first hair's were wound in: the game would not have drawn them).
- **The takes:** `tools/blender/rook_make_takes.py` (modes `takes`, `rook`, `shade`, `sheet`); it replaces `rook_takes.py`, deleted with the rejected takes. Nothing is built on the body, so `Rook_Piston` has nothing to hold in any take: whether an empty part stays in the rig so today's clips keep their keys is settled at the finish, with the pick. Each is a side-cast type, not a character:
  - **A, the school tough:** black hair combed back from the forehead to points behind the crown, one stray lock; narrow grey eyes, a low brow, a flat mouth, a white plaster over the nose; a long black school jacket open over a red T-shirt, its tails over the legs, the right sleeve rolled; white wraps, white trainers.
  - **B, the dojo trainee:** cropped chestnut hair lying forward; big amber eyes, a wide grin, a nick through one brow; an off-white training top with a crossed collar and short rolled sleeves, a red sash with its ends hanging, navy trousers gathered at the ankle; red wraps, the right arm's to the elbow.
  - **C, the gym kid:** messy dark brown hair, a heavy fringe to the brows; half-shut green eyes, a smirk, a white plaster on the cheek; a red zip track jacket with one white stripe a sleeve and a flat hood, the right sleeve pushed up, black joggers with a white stripe; white wraps, red and white trainers.
- **The sheet:** `build/handoff/Rook/takes/rook_takes.png` (1900 x 3429) and its six parts (`_full`, `_heads`, `_bust`, `_fight`, `_row`, `_small`), in place of the rejected sheet. The takes square on between two Anime Vanguards units at the same height are `build/reference/anime-vanguards/compare_rook_takes.png`: it holds the reference's pictures, so it stays there, out of git.
- **Triangles:** A 3,896, B 4,001, C 3,476, of which the hair is 3,360, 3,465 and 2,940 (68, 87 and 56 leaves). Five drawn pictures a body (512 x 384 a limb, 768 x 384 the torso) and one for the head (1005 x 320).
- **What I see, at full size and at the phone's:**
  - Beside the reference the three sit in the same make, hair included. Theirs still carry more painted texture in the cloth.
  - At the card's size all three read as a face with a hair of its own. A's scowl and plaster and C's smirk survive; B's grin does too.
  - From the player's camera in a fight, **C reads best** (red against the Shade's dark), B second (white and red). **A is the weak one there:** a black coat and black hair from behind, beside dark Shades, with only the white wraps to find him by.
  - My own order: C, then B, then A. A has the strongest face.
- **Content check:** PASS for A, B, C and the sheet, on these files (2026-11-24, 01:22), after a first PASS on the earlier hair that asked for C's plaster in white, B's hair less orange and B's temples covered: all three done. No take lands on one published character; the nearest share two or three loose traits each. Hold points for the finish. **A:** the forehead stays bare and the coat open over red; no cap, chain, gold buttons or second belt, nothing on the jacket's back, the eyes grey; the stray lock ends at the brow, clear of the plaster; at 64 px his points are lost in a round dark mass. **B:** never a headband, a black belt, white trousers, bare feet or torn sleeves; the sash's ends square-cut; the hair chestnut, never bright orange. **C:** the plaster lighter than the skin; one unbroken stripe a sleeve and a leg; the hair brown, never green or teal; the jacket red; no freckles; the hood reads as cloth. The picked take gets its own check on its upload files.
- **The four facts:** made: three takes and the kit. Self-reviewed: yes, as above. Delivered: the sheet, for research's pick with fresh eyes (the reference's sheet as their example), then the director's look; the heads, the figures and the busts went to the director's device at 01:19. Accepted in the game: no; nothing is built or uploaded.

### Research's pick: A, the school tough, with five must-fixes for the finish (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 01:24)

From `compare_rook_takes.png` (between two reference units), `_heads`, `_bust` and `_fight`. Fresh eyes, given the reference's sheet as their example, picked A.
- **Felt:** a cool, quiet side-cast fighter. The swept hair and narrow grey eyes read at once, and his face is the sharpest of the three.
- **Tier:** competent. **The one bold thing:** the hair, swept back into a few big points with one lock over the brow. It's the only hair of the three that reads as a designed shape, and the feel line asks for the hair first.
- **The make holds:** all three now stand in the make beside the reference, the faces especially. Against A:
  - B reads younger, and his gi is a stock training look.
  - C's messy hair, smirk and cheek plaster add up to the genre's commonest lead boy. Fresh eyes call his hair, like B's, "noisy clumps".
  - A also carries the game's "slightly mature" tone best.
- **Must-fixes, each by its class:**
  1. **From the fight camera at 450 px, the lead shows three values from behind, never one dark mass.** A's jacket, hair and trousers are all near black, and next to the Shades he reads as a slab. Lift the jacket to a dark blue-grey with a lit side, keep the trousers a different value from it, and let the red tee show at the open front and the hem. The hold points stand: the back stays plain, the coat stays open over red.
  2. **The fists read as fists from the fight camera.** Today the wraps read as banded cuffs. Carry them over the hand's end, with the knuckles' wrap seen from behind, in a value that stands off the jacket.
  3. **A mark on the face never cuts it in two at the card's size.** The plaster across the nose reads as a bar. Make it a small one on the bridge, or move it off the centre line.
  4. **Folds follow the cloth's pull, never the muscles under it.** Pecs and abs drawn on a jacket or a T-shirt look wrong. That's the kit's (`anime_make.py`), so it holds for every Voidkin.
  5. **The hair is one smooth lit mass, and the cloth is painted the way the reference's is.** Fresh eyes still see faceted hair and flat, clean cloth. The hair gets smooth shading from root to tip with one band of light; the cloth gets soft shade under its folds and a grain. That's the kit's too.
- **Why not Modulo's C:** C reads best from the fight camera, but on its colour alone, and his hair and face are the most generic. A's weakness is a matter of values, which must-fix 1 fixes. C's would need a new design.
- **What stays open:** whether `Rook_Piston` stays in the rig as an empty part, settled at the finish. What the stills can't show: A in motion on Lantern Row at night, first seen at Faber's install.
- **Next:** the director's look at A (the heads, figures and busts reached the director's device at 01:19), then the finish with the five fixes. Research's second look judges the fixes only.

### Modulo: A with the five must-fixes drawn (2026-11-24, 01:37; started 01:25)

Sheet: `build/handoff/Rook/takes/rook_pick.png` (1900 x 1537, sha1 `514b4890`): the figure from the front and from the game's camera, the bust at the card's size, the head from six sides, a fight at 450 px and 3 times, and 64 px. Beside two reference units: `build/reference/anime-vanguards/compare_rook_pick.png` (internal, not in git). Script: `tools/blender/rook_make_takes.py`, `take_a` and the new mode `proof [A]`. The takes' sheet stays as the takes' record: its A is the one before these fixes.
- **1. Three values from behind.** The jacket is a slate blue-grey (`#3D4764`), lighter toward its top and on his right side; the trousers stay near black (`#202229`); the hair is black. The red T-shirt hangs below the jacket's hem right round the hips, so his red shows from behind too. The belt went with it. The back stays plain: a middle seam, a yoke line and folds.
- **2. Fists.** Both hands are wrapped over their end, with the fingers' lines on every side, the knuckles' turn, a firm line and a shade at the wrist, and the wrist's own turns above it. Off-white on the slate jacket and on the bare right forearm.
- **3. The plaster** is half as long and sits on the bridge, between the eyes' inner corners. At the card's size it is a small pale mark, never a bar. The stray lock is shorter and ends at the brow, clear of it.
- **4. Folds by the cloth's pull, in the kit.** `anime_make.py` now holds the cloth's drawing for every Voidkin: `creases` (a fold with soft shade under it and a little light over it), `shadow`, `lit` and `grain`. A's folds were redrawn from where cloth is pulled: the shoulder to the opening, the arm's pit, the waist, the elbow. Nothing follows a chest or a stomach.
- **5. Smooth hair with one band of light, and painted cloth, in the kit.** A clump's ridge is now smooth from root to tip, sharp only at its two edges, and one band of light runs round the whole mass at one height (`Hair(..., band=(height, width, strength))`). The cloth has `grain`: soft clouds of lighter and darker, and a fine tooth.
- **Triangles:** unchanged, 3,896 (the hair 3,360).
- **My read, beside the reference:** from the fight camera he is now hair, jacket, a red line, trousers and a pale fist, never a slab. The band of light on black hair is quiet: it reads on the bust, less at 450 px. The reference's cloth is still painted harder than ours, with deeper fold shade. If research wants more, it is one number each (`creases`' amount, `grain`'s amount, the band's strength).
- **Memory:** the three-take sheet no longer fits beside the Studios (the guard stopped it twice at 0.7 GB free). `proof` draws the pick alone and peaks at 0.71 GB.
- **Content check:** PASS on these bytes: the sheet and A's six pictures (`build/scratch/modulo/ar59b/tex/A/`). The six earlier hold points stand. New for the finish: the hair stays black; the red stays a T-shirt, never a hood or red at the collar, and the jacket never closes to the throat; small plain buttons in the jacket's colour; no emblem or lettering anywhere; the jacket stays a muted slate; the irises stay grey; no scar, no headband, one sleeve rolled, wraps never gloves; plaster and wraps clean with straight edges; Haymaker's hit shows light, sparks or dust, never black sparks with red lightning, and never flame on the fists without a new check. The upload batch gets its own check.
- **`Rook_Piston` and `Rook_Tape`: both go** (my call, as the redo leaves it). Nothing is built on his body, and a part kept only to hold keys nobody sees is dead weight. A clip's pose for a joint that isn't there is skipped, so today's published clips still play. It costs Faber three lines at the install: the self-test's two joint checks and its "eight pieces" count (`SelfTestController/Cases.luau`, about line 1802), and `Rook_Piston` out of Haymaker's `Glow.Pieces` (`Config/Effects.luau`). The glow's mask moves to the right fist's wraps.
- **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes, at the fight camera and the card's size. Delivered, no: the model's files for Faber come with the finish. In the game, no.
- **Next:** research's second look at the five fixes; the director's look (sent at 01:37). Meanwhile the finish's build: the kit writes one picture and the pieces as `ModelRecipe` reads them, then his clips are run on the new body.

### Research's second look: AR59's pick passes at competent (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 01:38)

From `rook_pick.png` and `compare_rook_pick.png`. This look judges the five fixes and anything they broke.
- **All five are met:**
  1. From the fight camera he reads as hair, a slate jacket, a red line, dark trousers and a pale fist. He's never a slab, and he stands apart from the Shade.
  2. The wraps close over the hands' ends, so they read as fists.
  3. The plaster is a small mark on the bridge.
  4. The folds pull from the shoulder, the arm's pit, the waist and the elbow, with nothing on the chest or the stomach.
  5. The hair is smooth with its band of light, and the cloth has its grain. Beside the reference, the cloth now holds.
- **Nothing broke.** The hair's geometry is unchanged (3,360 triangles).
- **An older note, not a must-fix:** beside the two reference units, his hair is the quietest of the three. It stands lower over the crown, and at 64 px its points don't break the outline, which the content check also noted. The lever is the reference's own measure, 0.45 to 0.6 of a head over the crown, with the crown's points lifted. It goes to polish-later, and it's the first thing to try if the director asks for the hair again.
- **The cloth's fold shade, one step deeper** to match the reference's harder paint, also goes to polish-later. It's one number in the kit.
- **The sheet's label** says "no element". He's Fire (TR49's decision 6; `docs/roster.md` now agrees). That's only the sheet, not the game.

## AR60. Kirra, redesigned in the make (Modulo, for research's pick and the director's look, then its finish; Faber's install after)

Research, 2026-11-24, 01:50. On the director's order of 2026-11-23, by 22:52 (Rook, Kirra, Pip, Lyric, then Watt's finish), and the words since: 23:52 (the make) and 01:47 (attachments and exaggeration) (`docs/decisions.md`). Tier 2. It goes into the game only on the director's acceptance. It starts when Rook's portraits are done.
- **Feel line:** in a fight, the player sees a quick, quiet assassin from an anime's cast. Her hair's silhouette reads first, then a sharp face, then a long scarf trailing her moves, then two short blades.
- **What holds:**
  - her row in `docs/roster.md`: Common, Blue, Assassin, Void, Scarf Step's function (she vanishes, crosses the target and cuts on the way out), and First Strike;
  - two short blades;
  - the R6 body;
  - violet only on what's alive (AR40).
- **The make:** AR59's "The redo", drawn with the kit (`tools/blender/anime_make.py`). From the director's word of 01:47 she may carry some built attachments and some exaggeration: the scarf, the blades. The clothes stay drawn on the body.
- **The design:** a side-cast type of the genre's famous shows, made our own ("basics or our own"). "Courier" is her story, not a costume: nothing on her reads as a job, a satchel included.
- **What AR59 taught, asked of every take from the first sheet:**
  - three values from behind at the fight camera;
  - the weapon reads from the fight camera;
  - no mark across the face's centre line;
  - folds follow the cloth;
  - the hair stands 0.45 to 0.6 of a head over the crown, so its points break the outline at 64 px.
  - She reads apart from the new Rook: a different hair silhouette and a different colour family.
- **The takes:** two or three, each drawn in the make, never rough. Each is shown:
  - beside Anime Vanguards units at the same height (internal);
  - at the card's portrait;
  - from the fight camera at the smallest phone's size;
  - at 64 px;
  - beside the new Rook.
- **Checks:** the content check, with the Fighters test; research's pick, with fresh eyes given the reference's sheet; the director's look; the finish; the director's acceptance before Faber installs.
- **What waits:** her basic attack's effect (AR26); Scarf Step's look, if the new body asks for it; her portraits after the finish; Faber's install.
- **Time:** the takes in 2 to 3 hours, as a guide.

### Research's pick: A, the night runner, at competent, with three must-fixes (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 11:01)

Fresh eyes, blind, given the sheet's parts and the Anime Vanguards sheet, picked A at competent with the same crown fault; B and C at works.
- **Felt:** from behind her shoulder a silver plume rises over the head and a teal scarf streams back, then an X of steel: the quiet assassin in the feel line's order. B is a schoolkid in a loud cream coat; C's horns of hair and soft smile read cheerful, not quiet.
- **Tier:** competent.
- **The one bold thing:** the high silver tail with the teal scarf trailing under it, the two shapes that carry her from the fight camera.
- **Must-fixes for the finish:**
  1. **The hair is one smooth lit mass of a few large locks, the tail the crown's own silver** (serves "her hair reads first"). Today the crown is a ring of small rope-like curls that reads as a knit cap at the game's camera, and the tail is a duller grey with flat facets, so it looks stuck on. Class: the kit's hair, every Voidkin after her.
  2. **The hair is the brightest value on her** (serves the feel line's order and "quiet"). The pale trousers are her brightest mass in the fight frame and at 64 px and pull the eye down; they come a step darker, keeping three values from behind.
  3. **Folds read on dark cloth as they do on light** (serves the bar beside Anime Vanguards' painted units). Her navy top reads flat beside them. This is polish-later's older "the kit's fold shade one step deeper" (AR59), pulled forward now because a dark top is where it shows.
- **To polish-later:** the scarf's turn round the neck reads as a smooth tube at the card's size; a fold or a turned edge would make it cloth.
- The content check's hold points stand. Next: the director's look, which Modulo sent at 10:59; Modulo starts the finish now. Implemented: the three takes. Visually reviewed: this pick. Director-accepted: not yet.

### Modulo's fix: the hair rebuilt whole, the scarf's follow-through (2026-11-24, 11:40; started 11:20)

The director's look at the finish (about 11:20): "the hair are messy to many artifact and lack". Research's second look asked for the two must-fixes above. This is the hair's second fix round. If the director still finds it wanting, the next step is research's options, not a third patch.
- **What made the mess:** about forty thin leaves laid over each other. Their tips fanned out jagged, their edges and dark undersides showed between them as grey stripes on the crown, and each strand before an ear flared out as a grey sliver. The tail was eight leaves crossing, which crumpled into a grey bag at the knot.
- **The hair now:**
  - **The mass is one piece,** combed from its edge up to the tie (the kit's new `Hair.mass`). Its locks are parted lines painted on it, with a lit side, and they converge at the tie (`Hair.grooves`). The band of light runs round it.
  - **The edge** has a rim turned under, and an inner face where it hangs clear of the head, so a point seen from inside never breaks into splinters. Its points are part of the mass: short ones at the nape, and a broad lobe before each ear.
  - **The fringe** is four broad locks, no longer split.
  - **The tail** is one solid tapered lock (`Hair.tail`), the crown's own silver, with a band of light where it turns over and two locks parting at its end. The knot is the teal tie round its root.
  - The leaves' undersides are lighter (0.56 of their colour, where they were 0.36), so a turned leaf never shows black.
  - All of this is the kit's, for every Voidkin after her. The hair has 3,346 triangles, and she has 5,214 in all.
- **The scarf's tail in every clip** (`tools/blender/kirra_clips.py`):
  - **A follow-through:** each frame's posed tail is now only where the cloth is pulled to. The cloth chases it under the game's gravity and a drag, held to its links' lengths: its root follows closely and its tip late, so it lags the body and settles after it (`follow`, `PULL_ROOT` 0.2 and `PULL_TIP` 0.006 a step, four steps a frame).
  - **No flips:** only the first link's roll comes from the keys. Each later link carries on from the one before with the least twist, so no link turns its underside up between two that show their top.
  - **The Reveal:** its weight takes it over once she has landed (frame 30). From 60 it rises behind her left shoulder and arcs over and down, with a ripple running along it, where it stood as a plank.
  - **The links' joints:** the part of each link that runs on into the next is now a little narrower and thinner, and tucked inside it. The inner ends are open, where each had a dark cap that drew a line across the band.
  - The body, the blades and the markers are the same as at the finish. Every clip's audit passes with the same numbers.
- **Delivered over the finish** in `build/handoff/Kirra/make/`; the finish's files are commit 3b328a9c.
  - The changed files: the recipe, the hair, the scarf links, both maps (`Kirra_Color.png` sha1 `fe504f82`), the eight clips and their poses, the sheet (`22bb9bdc`), and the portraits (`aa110936`, `3e60417e`).
  - What Faber changes at the install is as in "Modulo's finish".
- **Content check (11:42): PASS.** The hold points of 11:21 stand, and it added two for the bare-rig strip of each clip, stepped with the scarf shown:
  - The tail never wraps across her lower face (it would read as a mask). Look hardest at Combo 9 to 21 and 56 to 61.
  - In the Reveal's arc (60 to 100), the tail's tip ends as cloth, never a point that tapers to a drop, from every heading at 64 px.
- **Four facts:** made, yes. Self-reviewed, yes: the head from six sides, the bust, the game camera, the sheet, and the Reveal's, Idle's and Combo's poses. Delivered, yes. In the game, no; nothing uploaded; not director-accepted.

### Research's third look: passed at competent (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 11:47)

From the new sheet, the Reveal's poses and the portrait. It judges the fix and what it changed.
- **Felt:** the hair now reads first and clean, a silver crown drawn to one smooth tail. The scarf's teal trails her moves as cloth.
- **Tier:** competent. **The one bold thing:** the silver tail and the teal scarf against the navy, from the game's camera.
- **Must-fix 1, met:** the tail is the crown's silver, one smooth lock, and the knot is the teal tie. On the portrait it reads as a clean tie, not a bag. The crown has no stripes or splinters from any of the six sides, which answers the director's "messy, too many artifacts" too.
- **Must-fix 2, met:** in the Reveal the tail is a curve at every frame. From 14 it trails above her from the drop, from 36 it comes over, and from 60 to 100 it arcs behind her shoulder with a sag. No pole, no plank, and no joint seen at the sheet's size.
- **Broken by the fix:** nothing seen. The body, the blades and the values are as at the finish.
- **Where it's first seen in the game:** the hair riding the head through every clip, and the tail at rest in the Idle at the install. Faber's install checks in "Modulo's finish" cover both.
- Visually reviewed: yes, research's pass. Director-accepted: not yet. Rook and Kirra wait on the director's acceptance together before their install.

## AR61. The hit sparks: the shared kit's landed hit, by rung (Scintilla, then Faber's hook)

Scintilla's first piece, on the director's order of 2026-11-23 ("Shared kit first", `docs/decisions.md`; `docs/briefs/effects.md`, "Your queue", 1). Research hadn't written this request when the seat opened (2026-11-24, 10:44), so Scintilla wrote it from the brief, AR8 A and the study's rules; research confirms or corrects it at the blockout's verdict.

- **Feel line:** your fighter's blow lands and a crisp star of light snaps open on the struck side, its needles flung on along the blow and slowing, gone before the next blow lands; a combo's last opens wider with a ring, and a critical bursts a nine-armed star that lights the two bodies.
- **Job and rank:** say "landed, and which way" on every hit, at rungs 2 (an ordinary blow), 3 (a combo's last, the Shade's swing) and 4 (a critical) (`docs/research/vfx-reference.md`, "DESIGN", rule 1). A premium basic hit is small and crisp, never a small explosion. Rung 7 and up is each signature's own.
- **Hero shape:** the kit's spark sheet (the starburst at rung 4), facing the camera so it reads from any camera, snapping open in its first frames and breaking outward. **Direction:** the needles, thrown along the blow, fast then slow (Drag 9), stretched along their travel.
- **Levers:** punctuation (the star's first two frames) on the hit's frame, a backlight under it, the needles one frame after, the ring a breath after at rungs 3 and 4, and a light at rung 4. The hitstop, the struck body's flash and the trauma stay AR8 A's, unchanged.
- **The block contains:** three rows effects in grey, `HitSpark2`, `HitSpark3` and `HitSpark4` (`src/shared/Config/EffectRows/`), written by `tools/fx/hit_sparks.py` and taken by `tools/fx/rows_take.py`. Only the kit's uploaded images: nothing new is drawn or uploaded.
- **What waits:** the look's colours (after the blockout's verdict); the element states' own hit cues (AR48, queue item 3); each class's strike (queue item 4); a Shade's hit on your lead, which keeps today's spark until the verdict says otherwise.
- **The hook asked of Faber** (code, the builder's):
  1. In `FighterController.show`, where `Effects.Spark(surface, from, heavy)` is called: play the highest `HitSpark<n>` at or under the hit's rung, anchored at `CFrame.lookAt(surface, surface + blow)`, the blow being `at - from` flattened on the ground, so the effect's Front (-Z) is the way the blow travels. Play it on the effects' free clock (`Effects.run`, which a capture's `TimeScale` holds), so the hold never stills it. Keep `Effects.Spark` as the fallback when no rows effect is named.
  2. In `raise`, when the server lifts a shown hit to rung 4 (a critical): play `HitSpark4` at that hit's surface, once, since the client showed it at its own rung first.
  3. A Studio switch to show the old spark beside the new for the before and after, if it's cheap.
- **Tuned live:** `NL_Debug:Invoke("Tune", "Effects.Rows.HitSpark2.Rows.Needles.Emit.1.2", 6)` and the like, once the hook is in.
- **Checks:** the content check on the spec (before the build); offline logic after the take (2106 passed, 0 failed, 2026-11-24, 10:47); the blockout in motion from the player's camera at the smallest phone, on the pale deck and the dark yard, beside the old spark; research's verdict.
- **The content check, 2026-11-24, on the spec: PASS**, with four conditions. (1) The needles stay thin, straight, tapered light lines, never a round or drop head, never dark or red-to-maroon, never longer-lived or less dragged so they arc or land; their fall is now 0. (2) At the capture, each rung read at 64 px from the player's camera, side and three-quarter headings: the needles read as light leaving the flash, not something out of the target's back; if not, shorten their throw or fan them round the contact. (3) The flash guard measured on the capture (the near-white share, bright floor, closest camera, each rung, rung 4's light above all); over about 3% with hits faster than 3 a second, the backlight and light hold rather than re-flash. (4) The finish colours inside the kit's conditions. The capture gets its own content check before the piece closes.
- **Blocked:** Scintilla's Studio playtests wait on the director's item 10 (the hooks); the rows show in the game only once Faber's hook is in.
### Scintilla's targets: the star, the crescent and the pop on Rook's ram (2026-11-25, 23:14)

- **Made by `tools/fx/spark_targets.py`** over a frozen frame of the game, `build/handoff/Hits/stage/stage_deck.json`:

  - The crescent reads as a slash: a blade's, weaker on a ram.
  - The pop's ring reads partly as a reticle.
- **Next:** the dark yard's frame in my next Studio turn, for the flash guard's read in the dark (the content check's condition 3).
- **Research's pick, 2026-11-25, 23:18: the star.** Read in pairs on `phone_deck/`'s peaks and squint, and each technique's rungs.

  - **Star over pop:** the pop's ring reads as a reticle at every rung, and its round core has no direction, when the job is "landed, and which way". The star's needles carry the way, and rung 4's gold nine arms read as a critical at once.
  - **Watch at the quarter checkpoint:** rungs 2 and 3 are small and white, and hold here only against the Brawler's dark body. Judge a hit with the pale deck behind it, and the dark yard's frame. Rung 3 must read as a step up from 2 (its ring), and on this sheet it barely does.

The changed rows aren't seen in Studio yet: the take waits on my next turn, and it's the bloom's last try before the options go to the director. Visually reviewed by me only. Not director-accepted. **For Faber's review:** the three rows files and `tools/fx/hit_sparks.py`.

Also the Festival street at night at the player's camera (`last_star_street_night_*`: a Brawler 26 studs up the street with Rook held at Hit3, the same crop size).

**Research's verdict on the last take (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 18:53; `checkpoint/last_star_day_rungs_vs_target.png` and `last_star_street_night_rungs_vs_target.png`, in both orders):**

The build covers 7,217 and 4,730 (a glowing floor line takes part of the box), as one soft mass with no arms. At the squint the mass is the focal point, but the shape is gone.

## AR62. The screens' redo: their colours, their light and their art (research's audit, then Modulo's directions, then Faber, a screen at a time)

**Why:** the director, 2026-11-24, 13:35, on a still of the Arc panel: "that lantern drawing is so bad and the rest is just a pain in the eye many hud design will need a big polish and change of colors"; and 13:37: "let not add those shiny bright effect that goes outside the ui buttons" (the register; `docs/ui.md`, rule 5, "Light stays in its piece"). The sixth time the colour has been called too much. It's a redo under the 11:11 word: blockouts beside the old, research's verdict in the game, the old kept.

### The style frame first (the director, 2026-11-24, 20:50)

The director said yes to research's restatement. AR62 becomes one style frame before any more UI or Voidkin work, and every later screen and Voidkin derives from it (`docs/decisions.md`, the top row; `AGENTS.md`, "target first, verdicts in pairs"). The audit below is its input.
- **Feel line** (research's reading of the accepted examples; the director's pick decides): on a phone, Home with one Voidkin on it reads as one game, mature and composed. It's dark and quiet with one strong accent, and the Voidkin is the brightest, most finished thing on the screen.
- **What the frame shows:**
  - Home at a phone's size, from a real capture (`build/captures/tr54/home.png` is the latest);
  - one Voidkin in the game's light on Home's stand: Rook, the director's accepted direction (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-11-24, about 01:47), with his hair made AR64's new way;
  - the tokens beside it: three hues and neutrals, two fonts, one type scale, two or three stroke widths and corner radii, and one material family for the Voidkin and the world;
  - the same frame in greyscale.
- **The takes:** two or three frames at final quality, made in about a day (Modulo, with research's audit below and `docs/research/examples.md`). **The director picks one.**
- **Checkpoints:** at a quarter and at half of the day, the takes sit beside the accepted examples (the graphite UI, the seam's rail, the galaxy Breaker; Watt's take A beside the genre's hits) and the rejected ones (the lantern drawing, the Mask Ledger, the slot marks).
- **What waits** (research's reading, on the Now board for the director to correct):
  - AR63 (Pip) and AR64 beyond Rook wait for the pick.
  - Each screen's redo below follows the picked frame, a screen at a time, as before.
- **After the pick:**
  - Faber writes the tokens into `src/client/UI/Theme.luau`, and the lint checks them (TR63).
  - `docs/ui.md` gets the tokens' page.
  - Research adds the frame to `docs/research/examples.md` as the accepted example for screens and Voidkin.

### Research's audit: the look, worst first (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 13:38)

From the latest phone captures: `build/captures/tr50/arc_panel_hard_open.png`, `tr54/card_win.png`, `tr54/home.png`, `ar49/phone_open.png`, `ar57/` (the HUD over the street). The screens with no capture since 11-19 are read from Faber's capture pass (below).
1. **The Arc panel**, the worst.
   - The drawn lantern stands for a place and is rejected.
   - Its body is lavender glass, and its badges glow lavender past their edges.
   - Hard's tile repeats its chevron on each of its four badges and its title, five in one row.
   - The Fight press's coral glow and an orange haze spill below the panel.
   - "Recommended" is grey on lavender and hard to read.
   - Everything is lit, so nothing reads as the one lit thing.
2. **The run's card:** the same lavender glass and glowing badges, a gloss streak across the Hard press, and the dare chip in a third material (dark with a gold edge). Three materials on one card. The band is already gone (TR54, 11:31).
3. **The HUD over the world:** three plate colours across the top (the lead card's violet, Credits' teal, the kW bar's yellow), each with an edge glow that spills. The dock's dark plates with a coloured underline read cleanly.
4. **The full screens' top bar** (Voidkin and Equipment), from the director by 13:50: "this left ui is so ugly", then "ugly heart lock". It holds Back, a framed group of a heart, an open padlock and a pencil, the title and two pills. The heart and the padlock are the favourite and lock marks, drawn as stock outline glyphs in a box. They're redrawn in the kit's own make, and the bar is laid out again with the screens.
5. **The Menu** (Equipment, Voidkin, Today) is the best of what's seen: graphite cards with our own art and one gold edge. It still has one fault: the Today card's gold glow spreads past the card.
- **The classes, for every screen:**
  1. One material family across all screens. Today there are at least three: Seam's lavender glass, Graphite Dark, and the HUD's coloured plates.
  2. Light stays in its piece (13:37), and only the one thing to press is lit.
  3. Violet only on what's alive or for sale, never a panel's body.
  4. A screen's picture of a place, a foe or a Voidkin is our own render of our own world or model, never a flat drawn icon standing for it.
- **Out of this piece, for the world's own colour:** the Festival square's paving reads lavender by day and night (AR57's stills). The screens are judged over it as it is.

### The plan

1. **Faber, at his next Studio turn:** one capture of every screen in its main state at phone size (852 x 480), by day, saved to `build/captures/ar62/before/`. That covers the HUD, the dock, the Menu and its three cards, the Voidkin screen, Equipment, the Breaker, the Void Shop, the reveal, the Arc panel, the run's card (win and fall), Today, Settings, and any toast.
2. **Modulo, after Pip: the design first, in today's colours** (the director, by 13:51: "the whole hud design will need a polished or change, color change later"). A rough blockout of two takes on the same three pieces, beside today's, at phone size over the street by day:
   - the Arc panel, with the Lantern Row tile's picture as our own render of the street and its keeper;
   - the run's card;
   - the full screens' top bar, with the favourite and lock marks redrawn in the kit's own make.
   - Each take names its material, its shapes and where light lives (13:37: inside the piece). One material family across the three.
   - The Menu's graphite cards with our own art are the best we have today, and a take may grow from them.
   - The kit's look is Modulo's to draw (D, 2026-11-16).
3. **Research's verdict** picks the take. The director sees it on return and can overrule.
4. **Then each screen,** worst first, as Modulo's kit and Faber's install, each beside the old.
5. **The colour change comes later,** on the picked design, as its own blockout of two or three palettes, at least one lighter than dark glass.
- **The style frame** (the director, 20:52, item 11b; `docs/decisions.md`): steps 2 to 5 become one piece.
  - Modulo brings two or three style frames. Each is the Home screen at phone size over the street by day, and Rook in the game's light with AR64's hair, and its tokens on one page: hues and neutrals, two fonts and one type scale, two or three strokes and radii, one material family.
  - The palettes differ between frames, at least one lighter than dark glass.
  - The director picks. Then each screen is redone from the frame, worst first.
  - UI art is drawn as SVG and rendered by headless Chrome (item 11c).
- **The director, 21:26:** "only modulo will keep working and redesign the voidkin + ui it unreadable for players and ugly we need to study" (`docs/decisions.md`). The frame becomes the Voidkin's and the UI's redesign, Modulo's alone tonight.
  1. **The study first** (`/ga-reference-study`): why shipped anime games' fighters and screens read on a phone and ours don't, measured on our captures beside theirs.
  2. **Then the targets,** beside the old, at the smallest phone's size, for research's pick in pairs.
- **Scope:** the whole HUD's and screens' design: materials, shapes, marks, light and screen art; the colours after. Layouts change where a screen's redo needs it, named in its own step. Words: research's sweep (below).

### Research's sweep: the words (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 13:49)

Every line players read, about 1,100 collected from `src/` by a read-only search, judged against `docs/design.md`, "The words". Most are plain and dry already: the refusals, the base, the Shop, the bag, the reveal. These change, for Faber to build:
- `Rules/Modes.luau:73`, the Festival's line: "Four stretches, then its keeper", replacing "Fight through the grounds beside your lead".
- `Rules/Modes.luau:93`, the Tower's line: "Your best floor this week sets your rank", replacing "One climb, floor by floor, until your lead falls".
- `Definitions/Goals.luau:43`: "Drum Court opens in an update", replacing "Drum Court's gate stays shut, for now."
- `UI/EquipmentScreen/Jewels.luau:24`: "Jewels open in an update", replacing "Jewels are coming".
- `UI/VoidkinScreen/init.luau:837`: "A string of {hits} hits", replacing "{hits} hits in a string, again and again".
- `UI/VoidkinScreen/init.luau:886`: "All stars: a copy now sells for Credits", replacing "Every star: a copy sells for Credits" (it read as if every star sold a copy).
- `Controllers/Main/BaseController.luau:121`: "The shares add up to over 100%", replacing "The shares come to more than the whole".
- `Controllers/Main/BaseController.luau:122`: "That shard isn't there any more", replacing "That shard has moved on".
- `UI/EquipmentScreen/Reinforce.luau:320` and `Config/Hud.luau:852`: "Next try succeeds", replacing "Next try is sure".
- **The classes, for every new line:** no tagline in a rhythm under a name; no "for now" or "coming" teasers, but "in an update"; no object given a person's moods ("moved on"). The machines' "wakes" and "awake" stay: that's the game's own state.
- **Not lines, for the roster:** the Commons' trade names (Brick's Hard Hat, Watt's Ground Wire and Insulated, Patch's Quick Fix, Gantry's Piston Drive, Mallow's Hot Meal) change with each one's redesign to the anime bar (`docs/roster.md`), never as a patch here.
- **Content check (13:52): PASS** on the nine. "Next try succeeds" is true only while a full heat bar makes the next try certain (`Rules/Reinforce.luau`): it changes with that rule.

- **From the Gemini blueprint (research, 2026-11-26, 12:30; TR66), for the screens after the pick:** the Voidkin's line reads "Common · Red · Warrior · Fire", where two words name a color. The type shows as its gem, and the element stays a word. Each type's gem is also read by its shape, for color-blind players. The type names stay the director's (2026-11-17).
- **The director, 2026-11-26, about 13:00:** "The whole game UI looks simply horrible, it totally fine to see an old idea and to agree it was an bad thing to use it edit/add/remove it if it better to do so". This covers the screens' redo: an old UI idea may be edited, added to or removed where that is better. Research still picks the frame.
- **Three style frames, for research's pick** (Modulo, 2026-11-26, 14:16): A Graphite, B Pearl and C Cobalt. Each comes as Home at phone size, the HUD over the street by day, grey, a small phone, and its tokens page, in `build/handoff/UI/style_frames/`. `tools/ui/style_frames.py` draws them. Today's HUD is cloned out of the street capture. Rook wears round 43's hair; AR64's new hair goes into the picked frame. Nothing uploaded.

  - **Felt:** A reads as a quiet, grown-up game where the cards carry the colour. B reads as a casual app, its pearl frame brighter than Rook. C reads as a loud, young simulator, nearest the rejected "childish" rail.
  - **Gates:** greyscale groups into three masses, with Rook's card the lightest. The small phone reads. Text: ivory on panel about 14:1.
  - **Beside the examples:** A is nearest the accepted graphite sheet ("i love the graphite UI wayyy more"), and plainer than it.
  - **Must-fixes, each a class, then the frame is final:**
    1. Violet only on what's alive or for sale: Today's card gets a graphite ground, and its shard stays the picture.
    2. The Voidkin is the brightest, most finished thing: Equipment's gold ground comes down in saturation and value, so the gold lives in the sword.

### The HUD overhaul (the director, 2026-11-26, between 22:37 and 22:57; `docs/decisions.md`)

- **Covers:** every HUD piece, as one system:
  - the lead's card, the skill and Auto presses, and spectate;
  - the top right's pills, the enemy's card with its poise, and the dock;
  - the damage numbers and the notices;
  - their palette, type, strokes and radii, motion, UI effects where they're earned, and sounds.
  - It replaces the frame's graphite and Oswald (21:04).
- **The study comes first** (the director, 21:04): `docs/research/anime-crusaders-ui.md` and `hud-reference.md`. A live session on a shipped action game's fight HUD follows, if the director sits for it.
- **Targets (Modulo):** three palette directions, each painted at final quality over the same two phone captures: a fight at the player's camera with the enemy's card, and the base at the gate.
  - The plates are bright or mid-value, never near-black or washed in violet ("too purple, black, somber", `docs/research/examples.md`).
  - Gemini's three daylight sketches are a start, not a brief (`build/scratch/quaestor/gemini/transcripts/2026-11-26_redteam-structure-tap-hud.md`, part 3).
  - Research picks in pairs.
- **To solve in the targets** (cold reads of `build/captures/lighting/base_light_a_installed_c8b49e.jpg` and the fight pad, research's and Gemini's):
  - "91 / 10 kW" reads as an overload.
  - The dock sits over the avatar's legs and stays up in a fight. A fight shows only what the fight needs.
  - The damage numbers pile over both fighters (AR48's finding, TR66).
  - The "While you were away" card is a cream box from another game.
- **With the pick:** one page of motion and sound.
  - Effects and sounds only on a ready signature, a poise break, a level up, a reward banked and the lead's low health. The rest stays silent.
  - Faber builds, Sonus shortlists the sounds, and the director judges in play.

**Research, 2026-11-26, 23:20, after Gemini's production read (`build/scratch/quaestor/gemini/transcripts/2026-11-26_production-speed-quality.md`):**
- **AR62's final frame (A with the cut, beside B) is withdrawn.** The HUD overhaul above replaces it.
- **Home's two cards** join the overhaul, after its first screen. They no longer wait on examples from the director.
- **One specimen first:** the overhaul's picked style goes into one screen, the fight's HUD, and is judged in play before any other screen takes it. It isn't copied across screens before then, as graphite was.

### Research's pick: Rail for the base, Tally for the return (Quaestor, 2026-11-27, 00:07; alone, with Gemini busy on the audits)

- **The base:** Rail over Tags, in both orders.
  - Rail's one strip reads as one unit at phone size. Its names, with no plates, let the base come first. That answers the "six floating labels" the director read as a prototype (`docs/research/examples.md`).
  - Tags' two stacked boxes compete with the lead's card.
  - In play, check the plateless names over the brightest sky and the clouds. If one blurs, its dark edge thickens. It never gets a plate back.
- **The return:** Tally over Grid, in both orders. Tally has one big number to lead, and its list scans easily. Grid's eight equal tiles give nothing the lead.
- **The press:** a tap anywhere closes. When shards are ready, "Reveal" stays as the panel's one press, and a tap anywhere else still closes. Modulo's three lines of text are taken.

### Modulo: the press's finish, B with the three must-fixes (2026-11-27, 01:29; started 01:25)

The files are in `build/handoff/UI/hud_overhaul/press/finish/`. The script is `tools/ui/hud_press.py`.
- `press_states.png`: HAYMAKER ready, cooling at 5 s, in the last second, ready again, firing, and on a keyboard, at 3x.
- `press_names.png`: the five launch names and HANDS OF THE VOID, ready, cooling and firing.
- `press_names_phone_x4.png`: the same names ready, at the smallest phone, enlarged.
- `close.png`, `fight.png`, `fight_small.png` and `phone_x4.png`.
- `images/`, for `Slate.Press.Art.Images`:
  - one name per launch signature, `Press<Name>.png`;
  - the cooldown's two faces, `PressFace.png` and `PressFaceCold.png`;
  - each 256 px, covering the 80 points round the press's middle, on a clear ground.

The must-fixes:
1. **The count and the word never cut each other.** While cooling, the word steps out whole and the count takes the disc. Ready again, the word steps back in with the pop.
2. **Long names hold.** A name goes on one line when it fits at size 17 or more. Otherwise it goes on two, split where the longer line is shortest. The widths are Bangers' own, measured in Chrome.
   - LANTERN / BURST, GROUND / WIRE and HANDS OF / THE VOID go on two lines at 18: caps 13.5 points, 9 px on the smallest phone.
   - HAYMAKER (18.8), SCARF STEP (17.6) and HELD NOTE (19.0) stay on one line.
3. **A gap to Auto.** A line is at most 62 points wide, so the slash ends at the ring's edge. That leaves 11.4 points to Auto at today's size, and the gap scales with the press.

For the install, the cooldown is one layer over the press:
- `PressFaceCold` underneath, with `PressFace` shown in the sector that has come back, clockwise from the top;
- a gold edge line (`#FFD04F`) along the sweep;
- the seconds left in the HUD's display face (Fredoka One), white with a dark stroke.
- The last second turns the count gold and lights the face's rim with the same gold.
- Firing uses the same name image: the press shrinks to 0.94 inside a white ring.

**Content check:** PASS. **Next:** research's verdict, then Faber's install.

2. **+1,240 is the one big number:** 52 points beside a 60-point coin, with WELCOME BACK at 24.
3. **The return's cards sit on tinted slate:** gold under the Credits card, azure under the list.
4. **The dock's tiles take their icons' hues:** blue for the base, gold for Home, coral for Play. The tile you're on is lit from inside, with its rim brightest.

### Research's call after the director's 02:04 word: the finish made as painted images (Quaestor, 2026-11-27, 02:05)

Ring stacks with painted light, gradient fills, an inner vignette, texture at a whisper, heavy stroked type and painted icons are what make the named games' panels read as art.

### Modulo: the fight HUD, painted as images (2026-11-27, 02:12; for research's look, then the director)

- **What it is:** F2's layout, every plate a painted image at 2x to the surface study's recipe. Script `tools/ui/hud_paint.py`, pictures in `build/handoff/UI/hud_overhaul/painted_fight/`.

- **My read beside the shipped frame:** at phone size it holds and reads as one family, with the shipped frame's weight of rim and type. Still short of it: the shipped tiles' saturated fills and the big painted icons spilling over them. Our card grounds stay dark slate in their hue.
- **Content check:** PASS (02:14). Before any upload, the plates get a sheet at full size and at 64 px on a dark ground, for the check before upload; the press's art gets its own check.

## AR63. Pip, redesigned in the make (Modulo, for research's pick and the director's look, then its finish; Faber's install after)

On the director's order of 2026-11-23, by 22:52 (Rook, Kirra, Pip, Lyric). The frame is AR60's: what holds, the checks, and the pictures. What holds is his row in `docs/roster.md` (Common, Purple, Mage, Fire, Lantern Burst, Kindle). Research's steer: his lantern is a young mage's, dark metal and glass with a living flame inside, never a paper festival lantern.
- **Feel line:** in a fight, the player sees a small apprentice mage from an anime's cast. His outline reads first (a hat too big, or hair swept up), then the lantern floating at his right shoulder with its flame, then sleeves past his hands.

### Modulo: three takes in the make (2026-11-24, 14:18; started 14:05)

Sheet: `build/handoff/Pip/takes/pip_takes.png` (sha1 `0e3bfd08`), with its parts and the new Kirra in the first place. Script: `tools/blender/pip_make_takes.py`.
- **A, the first-year:** an oversized slouched pointed hat, copper hair, and a cream tunic under a green mantle.
- **B, the prodigy:** no hat, black hair in solid spikes, and an ivory jacket with a charcoal cape.
- **C, the hearth kid:** a huge soft ochre cap, sandy hair, and a brick-brown coat.
- **The lantern is the same in each:** an iron cage, glass you can see through, and a flame that lights itself. The kit's hats are turned right way out by their volume.
- **Kit fix:** a hair of whole pieces alone (no leaves) now builds (`anime_make.py`).
- **Waits:** the lantern's size at 64 px (it reads small), research's pick, the finish, the clips, the portraits.
- **Content check (14:21): PASS.** Its hold points for the finish:
  - A keeps its brim and ochre band: no all-green outfit, no feather, no stars or moons.
  - B keeps its jacket closed: no headband, no crest on the back, no fingerless gloves. Lantern Burst stays a lantern, never a hand-thrown fireball.
  - C keeps its cap soft and peaked, with no brim all round and no straw. If it's picked, its band goes brown or ochre, not red.

### Research's pick: A, the first-year (Quaestor, 2026-11-24, 14:20)

- **Felt:** at the lead's distance, A reads as a mage before anything else: the oversized green hat over copper hair, the lantern riding at his shoulder.
- **Tier:** competent as a take. The bold thing is the slouched hat, the most distinct outline at 64 px of the three, and his row's "a hat too big".
- **Not B:** the sharpest anime face, but at 64 px a white jacket with no class read, and it drops the hat. **Not C:** reads as a delivery boy, a job costume.
- **Must-fix at the finish:** the lantern and its flame read at 64 px, beside the hat (class: what the row names reads at the game's size).
- The director's look comes next. They can overrule.

## AR64. The Voidkin's hair, a new way (Modulo, for research's pick and the director's look; then Rook, Kirra and Pip)

Research, 2026-11-24, 16:53, on the director's word, "the hair still need a rework" (`docs/decisions.md`, the top row). Kirra's hair had its two fix rounds (AR60), so this tries new ways, not a third patch.
- **Feel line:** in the lead card and at the lead's distance, each Voidkin's hair reads as a few bold, clean locks with sharp tips, flat colour, one hard shadow and one shine, a shape you know in silhouette. It's the hair of the anime games players know: never a pile of leaves, never a soft helmet.
- **What's wrong, from the make sheets:**
  - **Rook:** about forty thin leaves. Their tips fan out jagged, their grey undersides streak the crown, and at 64 px it's a dark scribble.
  - **Kirra:** after the fix, one smooth mass. It reads soft, a white helmet with grooves painted on, the locks lost, and the sides still splinter.
  - **Both:** soft modelled shading, where anime hair in our make is flat colour, one hard shadow and one shine band.
- **The block:** three takes, each a different way of making hair, on Rook's and Kirra's heads, beside today's. Show the lead card's bust, the game camera at the lead's distance, and the 64 px silhouette, beside Anime Vanguards' units as at Watt's comparison. The way is Modulo's.
- **Waits:** Pip's hair, made the picked way at AR63's finish. Rook's and Kirra's install waits on the pick. The clips are untouched, since the hair rides the head.
- **Checks:** the content check on the takes; research's pick; the director's look. **Cap:** 2 hours for the takes. It's a fighter's look, so it closes at the finish against the anime bar.
- **Order:** Modulo's next, after the step it's on.
- **Folded into AR62's style frame** (the director, 20:52, item 11b): the frame's Voidkin is Rook with the new hair. Kirra's and Pip's hair follow the picked way.

Asked what, the director named all of it: the shape (no designed silhouette, too wide, droopy), the locks (stiff flat shards, too many, too alike, faceted), the colour and shading (flat, no shine, no depth). ## AR65. The base, simpler: a redo (research's direction on the director's freedom; Faber's cut pass, then Modulo's targets, then the build)

**Why:** the director, 2026-11-26: "the base really look like a mess it should be way more simpler, easy to the eye, to much blooms and hard to notice different machine i will give you creative freedom to cut/add major stuff" (the register). It's a redo under the 11:11 word: targets beside the old, research's pick, then the build. Research's calls below are made inside that freedom.
- **Feel line:** from the gate on a phone, the yard reads in one look: a calm floor, the Void Mill turning as the one landmark, every machine told apart by its shape and its one colour, and nothing glowing but what calls you.
- **What fails today** (`build/captures/ar54/`, the base as installed on 2026-11-23):
  - Everything glows: the Mill's three neon rings, the floor's lit lines, a crystal on every top, the lit pillar, the Door's gold rim. At the kit's glow of 2.4, under dusk and night bloom, nothing leads.
  - Every machine wears the same teal, bone and gold, and the Breaker and the bays share their bone horns.
  - Noise behind and underfoot:
    - pylons and cables, a real power grid, against the void-machines word (2026-11-15 and 16);
    - the teal spikes on the skyline;
    - hazard stripes, which belong to a building site;
    - the floor's seams.
- **Cut:**
  - the pylons and cables, the skyline's spikes, and the hazard stripes;
  - the floor's lit lines, and its seams at the camera's distance;
  - the Mill's neon rings;
  - every crystal but the Breaker's;
  - the Lab's sleeping place (the Lab comes after launch).
- **Folded:** the battery into the Mill's foot. Its charge stays on the HUD and the Power screen.
- **Out of the yard at launch:** the Forge machine. Reinforce lives in Equipment (2026-11-17), and the Forge's own use comes after launch, when the machine comes back with it. If the director says no, it stays.
- **Added:**
  - each machine gets one accent colour that no other machine uses, and the rest of it takes the shared neutrals;
  - each machine gets a silhouette that reads in greyscale;
  - a small name plate stands over each machine, in its accent.
  - each machine shows its work in motion where it can: the Training machine's Voidkin training, a shard in the Breaker, the bays' workers at work (from the Gemini addendum, 2026-11-26).
  With the arrows gone after the tutorial (TR65), the shapes and the plates are how a player finds a machine.
- **Stays:**
  - AR54's spots and clears, and the 128-stud plot;
  - the gate and its plate, and the showcase plinth;
  - the bays' rarity light, the Door, and the toon outline.
- **The light:** at most one glow per machine, with the Mill's as the only large one, and nothing on the floor glows. The world's bloom stays, since the effects need it (AR61). The base's glow comes down through its own parts and `Config.Lighting.Glow`.
- **Order:**
  1. **Faber, in a Studio turn, about an hour, nothing committed:** the cut pass as the first target. In a playtest, hide what's cut and turn the base's glow down. Capture from the gate at the smallest phone, by day and at dusk, beside the same frames today. Research picks whether the cuts go as listed.
  2. **Modulo, after the hair goes to the director and AR62's frames:** two or three targets drawn on step 1's frame, showing each machine's accent and silhouette, the plates, and the floor in one calm tone. Research picks them in pairs.
  3. **The build:** Modulo's kit, then Faber's install.
- **Gates:**
  - a blind reader names every machine from a still at the smallest phone;
  - the gate's view in greyscale tells every machine apart;
  - no two machines share an accent;
  - the gate's view shows one glow per machine, plus the Mill's.
- **Waits:** the stages' looks (I to III) follow the picked target. Nothing else joins this piece.
- **Research's pick on the cut pass (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 13:16; `build/captures/ar65/compare.png` and the four frames at 568×320, each pair read in both orders):** the cut, by day and at dusk. It's clearly calmer, and the Mill now reads as the one landmark. Implemented in a playtest only; not yet built.
  - **Make the cut real now (Faber),** with its rollback, everything hidden in the pass plus two more items. It's the picked direction, and it doesn't wait for the targets:
    - the two Transformers, which are real grid kit like the pylons;
    - the two FloodMasts, which stand at both edges of the Mill and break its outline (`cut_day.png`), and are real floodlights too.
  - **LevelFin and LevelFinOuter** stay for now. They're behind this camera, so Modulo's targets judge them from the walk in.
  - **Must-fix for the targets (the main read; class: every piece's glow against the Mill's):** at dusk the Door's gold rim is now the brightest thing in the frame and pulls the eye from the Mill. No piece outglows the Mill.
  - **Joins Modulo's step 2:** the Mill's two lilac crystals become one glow; the bays' lilac lenses; the floor's seams; the gate's hazard stripes. Faber checks whether the dark lines across the floor toward the bays (`cut_day.png`, right of the Mill) are parts, which he hides, or the texture, which is Modulo's.
  - **Gates:** read by eye at 568×320, not measured (Pillow isn't installed on this PC). The Mill holds the frame's centre and its strongest mass. The measured gates come at the build.
- **Modulo's targets (step 2, 2026-11-26, 14:59; `build/handoff/Base/ar65_targets/`):** two, painted over step A's built frame (`build/captures/ar65/built_stepA_day.png`), each beside it at the smallest phone, in colour and in grey (`ar65_targets_small.png`).
  - **A, graphite:** the machines in AR62's graphite, each with one accent no other uses (the Mill cyan, the Breaker violet, the bays coral, Training green, the Stone Condenser blue; the Door's gold kept, a step down), on a pale warm stone floor in one tone. **B, ivory on slate:** the bone kept as a clean ivory, on a mid slate floor. The plates in both use style frame A's cut, each in its machine's accent, with the names the game uses.
  - **In both:** the floor's seams, its lines and the battery's blocks are gone. The Breaker's crystal is the only crystal. The Mill's two lilac crystals take its cyan, so they read as one glow with its lights. The bays' lenses are plain white.
  - **Measured in grey** (the spread of luminance in each machine's box, today → A → B): the Breaker 0.24 → 0.28 → 0.26, the Mill 0.22 → 0.24 → 0.24, the bays 0.20 → 0.27 → 0.22, Training 0.21 → 0.28 → 0.21. A gains on every machine. B loses the far bays and Training against the pale sky. My read: A.
  - **Not covered:** dusk, because step A has no dusk capture. The Door's rim at dusk stays the build's must-fix. The gate's hazard stripes and the walk-in fins are behind this camera. Made and self-reviewed; nothing uploaded.
- **Research's pick, in pairs (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 15:01; `ar65_targets_small.png`, `target_A.png`, `target_B.png`, each pair read in both orders): A, graphite on pale stone.**
  - **A against B:** A both ways. Every machine stands as a dark mass on the light floor, and its one accent shows clearly against the graphite. In B the far bays and Training go pale against the pale sky, and the slate floor makes the frame the heavier one, which is the director's "too black, somber" in a new place. Modulo's grey measure agrees: A gains on every machine, and B loses two.
  - **A against today:** A both ways. Today every machine wears the same bone, teal and gold. In A each is told apart by its shape and its colour, and the Mill holds the centre as the one landmark. Gates by eye at 852×480, OBSERVED: no two accents are shared; the grey row parts every machine.
  - **Nearer** the accepted "anime yet mature" base (AR14, `Base/base_1_across.png`) than the rejected ones, with one risk below.
  - **Must-fixes for the build:**
    1. **The floor reads as stone, never a bare baseplate** (class: every large flat surface; the director's "the base feel empty now", 11-16). A low-contrast slab pattern shows up close and fades by the gate's distance, as the cut said, "its seams at the camera's distance".
