## AR66. Clips follow the model (Modulo's audit and targets, research's feel line and pick, then Faber's install)

The rule: `docs/design.md`, "Clips follow the model, rules follow the class" (the director, 2026-11-26, about 13:28). Each new clip keeps its action's Beats, Range and Reach (`Definitions/Actions.luau`), and a reaction keeps its set's length and Land.

### Modulo's audit and Watt's targets (2026-11-26, 17:07; started 16:44)

- **The audit, the five made Voidkin:**
  - Rook and Pip play their own sets: they fit.
  - Watt fits in everything but his Combo: "three blows, all of the shaft, none of the blade", the spear held across like a bar. That's the director's own example.
  - Kirra plays Rook's Hit, Down and Up (research's option b, 2026-11-14), which lean on his ram ("hangs on the ram like a post"). She has no ram.
  - Lyric plays Pip's (the robe set), built round his hat and lantern, and her stand and speakers stay at rest through them.
- **The block, in order:** Watt's Combo, then Kirra's own Hit, Down and Up (her tail inside them), then Lyric's (the stand and the speakers keyed).
- **A finding before the pick: the review camera.**
  - The clip scripts' "game" view (`rook_clips.GAME`, 152°, behind his right shoulder, marked "assumed") isn't the player's.
  - A lead fights beyond its target, turned `Combat.Lead.Frame` (45°). So the camera sees it 43° off its front, on its left (AR1, the replay, 2026-11-12), and the Shade, nearer the camera, hides the contact.
  - From there the contact sits behind the Shade in every take, today's included. What reads is the spear above his head, and its back end when it swings out past his left.
  - From the side (the `Beside` spot, 90°), both targets read at once.
- **Watt's targets,** each beside today's (`Combo`), in `build/scratch/modulo/ar66/out/`. `poses_*.png` holds the keys from the player's view and from the side, with their frames. `strip_*.png` holds every fourth frame from the player's view. Today's Beats are kept: Hit1 21, Hit2 36, Hit3 60, cancel 88, 110 frames.
  - **A, the wall:** the spear taken up overhand, two stabs (the second with a step), then the butt stamped in behind his heel and the point set on the Shade: the brace.
  - **B, the drive:** a thrust from the low guard, the blade swept across, then the spear raised overhand and driven down with a lunge.
  - **My read:** from the side, A is the clearest spear fighter and the most the Tank's: a wall that plants. B travels more. From the player's view, both read only above his head (A's stabs, B's drive); A's brace and B's thrust and sweep sit behind the Shade.
  - **The feel line, proposed for research to set:** "Watt holds his ground and lets the point do the work: short, heavy stabs from behind the shaft, then the spear planted and set, and the Shade stopped on it."
  - **The build's, not the target's:** the shaft through an arm at 57 and 61, and the butt 0.2 under the street at 79. The content check comes before the build.
- **Status:** made and self-reviewed; research's feel line and pick in pairs, or its call on the staging first. Nothing delivered.

### Research's feel line and pick (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 17:10; `poses_*.png`, `strip_*.png`, `play_*.png`, each pair in both orders)

- **Feel line, set:** from the player's camera, Watt keeps the Shade at the spear's length and works it with the point, never the shaft: a thrust, a sweep, then the spear raised overhand and driven down into it, a heavy blow you see land over its head, and he ends set behind the shaft like a wall.
- **Picked: B, the drive.** The judge is the player's camera, and there B wins in both orders. Its finisher, the heaviest beat, lands above the Shade's head, while A's brace sits behind the Shade from 60 to 72. From the side A is a little clearer, but B reads there too, so B holds whichever way TR69 F settles the staging. Both answer the director's "none of the blade".
- **Must-fixes for the build:**
  1. **Every hit shows outside the Shade at the player's camera** (class: every hit of a melee set). B's thrust at 21 is hidden behind it. Start it from a higher guard, or with a step to the open side, so the point shows past the Shade.
  2. **The end is the quiet wall's** (`docs/roster.md`, Watt; class: Watt's set). The drive plants, and at 68 his feet are set and the point is held in, as A's brace stands. It doesn't travel on.
  3. **No part passes through the body or the street** (class: every key): the shaft through an arm, and the butt under the street, as Modulo listed. The content check comes before the build.
- **The camera finding** goes to TR69 F, an experiment of Faber's. The clip tools' "game" view gets corrected to the player's, `play` (Modulo's).
- **Next:** Watt's B build, then Kirra's and Lyric's targets, in Modulo's order.

### Modulo's targets for Kirra's and Lyric's own Hit, Down and Up (2026-11-26, 17:30; started 17:12)

- **Where:** `build/scratch/modulo/ar66/kirra/` and `build/scratch/modulo/ar66/lyric/` (gitignored). `poses_*.png` holds the keys from the player's view (`rook_clips.GAME`, corrected in 4403df2f) and from the side, with their frames. `strip_*.png` holds every third frame of a Hit and every fourth of a Down or an Up, from the player's view.
- **Each keeps its set's length and marker:** Kirra's (R6's open set) Hit 19 frames, Down 67 with Land at 34, Up 55. Lyric's (the robe set) Hit 21, Down 67 with Land at 29, Up 59, her legs inside the jacket's tail (the widest swing is 27 of 30 degrees). A Down and its Up come as a pair: the Up starts where the Down lies.
- **Kirra,** beside a Shade stand-in, her tail falling in the air and lying still on the settles:
  - **Hit A, snapped back** (3 struck, 12 back on it): head and arms thrown back, the tail whipped up. **Hit B, folded** (3 folded, 12 eyes up): she folds round it, arms in.
  - **Down A, the breakfall** (16 off her feet, 26 turning over, 34 lands, 52 lying), **Up A, the kick-up** (10 rocked back, 16 the kick, 22 on her feet).
  - **Down B, the slide** (18 thrown, 34 lands, 42 the slide, 54 propped on her right hand, eyes up), **Up B, the spring** (12 the push, 20 legs under).
- **Lyric,** the stand and the speakers keyed in every clip:
  - **Hit A, rocked** (3 struck, 13 on the nod): back on her heels, hanging on to the stand as it leans in after her, the speakers knocked off their line. **Hit B, the shield** (3 shielded, 14 out again): her shoulder turned, the stand pulled in, the speakers swung in front of her.
  - **Down A, with it** (14 and over, 29 down, 41 lying): over backwards all of a piece, the robe set's fall. She keeps hold, the stand lies along her right side with her fist on it, and the speakers drop dead on the street. **Up A, up it** (9 plants it, 20 hauls up, 32 up, 42 on the nod): she swings it upright, plants it and hauls herself up it, the other hand off the street.
  - **Down B, let go** (6 the blow, 29 down, 41 lying): the stand stays standing where she let go of it, her hand after it, the speakers sagging beside her. **Up B, caught** (12 comes up, 24 caught, 30 on the beat): she closes on it as it tips to her and turns it once round its line on the beat, and the speakers snap home.
- **My read:** Kirra's A pair (the breakfall and the kick-up) is the most hers, light and back up at once, and her Hit A reads best for its tail. Lyric's B pair gives the stand a life of its own and puts her rise on the beat. Her Hit A reads clearer than B, whose speakers cross her face.
- **The build's, not the targets' (the audit):**
  - Kirra: the legs cross 0.12 at Hit A 7; an arm into the torso up to 0.17 (Down A 1, Up A 13, Down B 38, Up B 6); a lying arm up to 0.1 under the street; Up A's head under the street at 6 to 8 before the last lift (not re-audited); a leg sinks at Up B 20.
  - Lyric: Hit B's left arm 0.35 into the torso at 3, its speakers through her head and arm at 2 to 14; an arm under the street, 0.03 at Down A 29 and 0.15 at Down B 32; Up A's left arm 0.09 into the torso at 20; Up B's speakers 0.1 into her arms at 8 and 25.
  - The content check comes before the build.
- **For research, a conflict:** `docs/design.md` line 131 still says reactions are one shared set per rig, which Lyric wears too, and `lyric_clips.py`'s header says the same. The rule at line 54 overrides both.
- **Status:** made and self-reviewed. Research's pick in pairs is next. Nothing delivered. Meanwhile: Watt's B build, with research's three must-fixes.

### Research's pick, Kirra's and Lyric's reactions (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 17:32; `kirra/poses_*.png`, `lyric/poses_*.png`, each pair in both orders, from the player's view and the side)

- **Kirra: Hit A, and the A pair.** Hit A both ways: thrown back, her face and the tail open, where B's fold hides her face. In the A pair, the breakfall and the kick-up are light and back up at once, and the kick reads in the air from the player's view. B's slide and its spring sit low by the Shade.
- **Lyric: Hit A, and the B pair.** Hit A both ways: back on her heels, the stand leaning after her, while B's speakers cross her face. In the B pair, the stand left standing is a mark you see while she's down, and her rise lands on the beat with the speakers home. That's the singer's own, where A's haul up the stand is only a way up.
- **Must-fix for the build (class: every key):** no part passes through the body or the street, as the audit lists for the picked four. Kirra's Up A has its head under the street at 6 to 8, not yet re-audited. Lyric's Up B has its speakers in her arms at 8 and 25.
- **design.md, reconciled:** the shared sets are where a reaction starts, and a model they don't fit gets its own. Kirra and Lyric left the robe list. `lyric_clips.py`'s header is Modulo's to match.

## AR67. The Generator's new model, and any machine that needs one (Modulo's targets, research's pick, then Faber's install)

Research, 2026-11-26, 17:47, on the director's word of about 17:46 (the register): "we can change the whole model of any machine that need it and dont need to stick with what i said abt the generator turning ... just make it the best it can be".
- **Feel line:** from the gate on a phone, the Generator is the base's one landmark. It's a void machine you read in one look as the thing that makes your power, with one cyan glow and its work shown in motion where its make wants it, not a wheel forced to turn.
- **Holds from AR65 (picked A):** graphite with cyan as its only accent, the one large glow, the battery in its foot, the plate "GENERATOR", AR54's spot and clears, and its three stages (I to III) following the picked make. A void machine, never real electrical kit.
- **Targets first:** two or three, each a turnaround in the game's light beside the base's other machines and a Voidkin, plus the gate's view at the smallest phone. Judged against shipped anime games' landmarks, not against our own sheet. Research picks in pairs.
- **The other machines:** research reads AR65's installed build from the gate. Any machine that fails "told apart by its shape" or the bar gets its own target here, one at a time, after the Generator's.
- **Code (Faber's):** the wheel's turn (`Config/World.luau`, the Generator's Least and PerKw) and BaseCases' AR19 wheel check come out or change with the picked make.
- **Order:** Modulo, after Watt's Combo B build, before AR56's drifts and AR66's builds, since the director is looking at the base now. The content check comes before the build.

### Modulo's targets: three makes (2026-11-26, 19:04; started 18:23)

`build/handoff/Base/ar67_targets/`: `ar67_sheet.png` (all three beside today's Mill), and per make `<make>_gate.png` at installed_day's camera (852×480, fitted to Faber's capture, the gate's truss hidden as the game's camera sees through it), `_gate_grey.png`, `_home.png` (the phone's default behind the spawn), and `_turn.png` (front, three-quarter, side and back). In the base's own scene and light, with a Voidkin's size, by `build/scratch/modulo/ar67/gen_targets.py`. Stages I to III and the motion are DESIGN, written on the sheet.
- **A, the held core** (23.9 studs, 3,176 triangles): a cyan core held up in an opened graphite pod over the lit column, its gauge. The core turns and breathes, and the pod stands still.
- **B, the helix** (25.8 studs, 8,096 triangles): two graphite flights wind up a column of light to a flame at the crown. The flights turn, so the power reads as climbing.
- **C, the split stone** (28.5 studs, 1,072 triangles): a graphite monolith split by a seam of light that fills in eight steps, with stones floating over it. The halves breathe apart.
- **Measured at the gate** (the Generator's region, x 330 to 560, y 40 to 300): cyan pixels A 2,642, B 1,603, C 1,170, today 853; pixels under L 60, A 7,351, B 9,539, C 13,815, today 5,777.
- **My read:** A reads best. In grey its core is the lightest mass mid-frame, the one large glow. B shows its work best in motion, but its light is cut into strips and it's the busiest. C has the boldest silhouette, but it's the darkest mass in the frame, which is the director's "too black, somber" risk.
- **Content check (19:03): PASS**, for the targets only. The picked make gets its own check on its files before upload, the stand-ins don't ship, and its source moves into the kit's tool.
- **Next:** research's pick in pairs. Meanwhile, AR66's Kirra builds.

**Research's pick (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 19:06; `ar67_sheet.png` with each make's gate and grey frames, in both orders): A, the held core.**

- **A over B, both orders:** at the gate, A's core is the one large glow and the lightest mass in grey, so it reads first, and it reads as the power. B's light breaks into slits between its flights, and its crown is small (cyan 1,603 px against A's 2,642).
- **C is out:** it's the darkest mass in the frame (13,815 px under L 60), the "too black, somber" the director has named many times.
- **Beside today's Mill:** A carries three times the cyan (2,642 against 853) and has no wheel to turn, which the director's word of about 17:46 freed.
- **For the build, one check:** at the gate, beside the Breaker, A reads first at the squint, and in each of its three stages the core stays the lightest mass. The breath and the motes are judged in play.
- **Next:** Modulo builds A. Then Faber's install: the wheel's turn in `Config/World.luau` and BaseCases' AR19 check.

### Modulo's build of A, the held core (2026-11-26, 19:45; started 19:25)

- **Where:** `tools/blender/base_generator.py`, in place of the Mill. `base_kit.py` takes its pieces and `base_plan.py` its stages. Exported to `build/handoff/Base/` (the manifest, `plan.json`'s `generatorStages`, and the meshes). The Mill's 22 stale meshes are gone from the handoff, and every map came out byte-identical. `base_mill.py` is retired from the kit, but its shape helpers stay shared.
- **The stages:**
  - **I:** `Generator` (the foot with the battery's cells, the column's seat, bed and cap, and two blades side on) and `GeneratorCore` at 0.8 size.
  - **II:** `Generator2` (the target's three blades) and `Generator2Core` at full size.
  - **III:** `Generator3` (the blades a tenth taller, their tips meeting in a steel clasp over the core) and `Generator2Core`.
  - **Every stage:** `GeneratorMotes`, `GeneratorArc1` to `8` (the column's steps) and `GeneratorDemand` (the collar).
  - **Size:** 23.9 studs tall at I and II, 29.0 at III; 2,888, 3,176 and 3,696 triangles as laid.
- **Changed from the sheet:** Stage III has no ring. Levels 2 and 3 already float their teal rings on the generator's upright, and "level 3 stays one halo" (research, 2026-11-23, 03:22). Tried round the core's waist, the ring cut the core in two at the squint (206 fell to 186). The clasp keeps steel over the core.
- **Research's check, measured:** the frame blurred to a squint (a box a twentieth of its height, three passes), in the day's light, each stage beside the Breaker at the same stage.
  - **From the capture's view** (the target's gate frame): the core reads 213, 207 and 206 at I, II and III. The Breaker reads 165, 125 and 120, the column's head 128, 109 and 109, and the cells 95. In every stage the core is the machine's lightest mass and reads first.
  - **From the far gate on the Strip:** the core reads 204, 182 and 182, the Breaker 190, 178 and 180. That's near a tie at II and III, as the picked target was there (178 against 190).
- **Fit:** the least clear is 12.31, now between bays 1 and 6 rather than the generator. AR50's rings PASS, with A's body 18.26 studs from level 3's outer ring. That took a fix to `base_levels_clear.py`, broken since AR65 folded the battery into the generator's foot.
- **For Faber's install (Faber's code):** the contract is in `plan.json`'s `generatorStages` and each piece's note.
  - **The core** (`GeneratorCore`, `Generator2Core`): `turns` about its upright (Roblox's Y) at rate 1, at the old wheel's speed (Generator.Wheel's Least, PerKw and Most). It `breathes` 0.04 of its size and back over 2.4 seconds, about its middle. On a round core the turn is barely seen; the motes show it.
  - **The motes:** turned about the upright by the core's angle times -0.5.
  - **The steps:** a gauge, shown or hidden, as before.
  - **`GeneratorDemand`:** `rises` up the column by the demand's share times 8 studs, from 3 up. This is new; it was a turn about the axle.
  - **Gone:** `GeneratorWheel`, the pinions, and Stages II and III's own steps and marks. BaseCases' AR19 wheel check changes with them.
  - **The solid:** the well stays shut to walking (radius 9, 2.6 high).
  - **The plates:** the machine plate stands over the laid pieces, so it follows A. `Config.Hud`'s Condenser plate still sits "over [the Mill's] far horn", so its Aside may want a retune.
- **Sheet:** `build/handoff/Base/generator_stages.png`. Top: the target beside Stage II. Middle: Stages I and III. Bottom: the three stages from three-quarters.
- **Status:** made and self-reviewed (the squint measures, the fit and the ring audit). Content check before upload: PASS by 19:44, on the sheet, the 64 px read and the module. It found no real electrical kit, no body shapes and no text. Delivered to the handoff for research's verdict, then Faber's install. Not accepted.

**Research's verdict on the build (Quaestor, 2026-11-26, 19:46; `build/handoff/Base/generator_stages.png`, beside the picked target A): passes, and goes to Faber's install.**

- **Felt:** a held cyan core in a graphite pod that closes as it grows, read first beside the Breaker.
- **Gates (MEASURED by Modulo at the squint, from the target's gate view):** the core is the lightest mass in every stage (213, 207 and 206), against the Breaker's 165, 125 and 120 and the column's head 128 and 109, so the pick's one check is met. ### Modulo's fix: the core smooth up close (2026-11-26, 21:16; started 21:08)

- **Research's must-fix (20:57), met in the mesh:** the core is rebuilt as a smooth round (`tools/blender/base_generator.py`, `core`). Its profile runs through a curve, turned on 24 sides, with smooth normals, so the game shades it as one round of light rather than flats lit one by one. It's 816 triangles where it was 112, at the same size and middle, with the same contract (it turns and breathes).
- **A fault found on the way:** the kit dropped a lathe's quad wherever two of its corners met on the axis (`tools/blender/lantern_row_kit.py`, `Mesh.to_object`), which left a hole at each pole. Today's core was open at both poles (1.55 studs across at its head, 1.6 at its foot), and so was Stage III's clasp (48 open edges). The kit now keeps such a face as a triangle.
  - **The audit, every kit's pieces:** only GeneratorCore, Generator2Core, Generator3 and the Strip's VeinWide and VeinThin lost faces. Every other mesh comes out unchanged.
  - **Generator3 is now closed** (0 open edges, as Generator2's). Its triangles fall from 1,408 to 1,360: the hull pass had wrapped the open clasp a second time.
- **Sheet:** `build/handoff/Base/generator_core_smooth.png`, today's core left and the smooth one right, from a player's distance (top) and near (bottom), in the base's day. Its render draws the core unlit, so it shows the outline (14 sides to round). The shading's change is first seen in the game.
- **For Faber's install:** upload `GeneratorCore`, `Generator2Core` and `Generator3` again (`build/handoff/Base/meshes/`), and the Strip's `VeinWide` and `VeinThin` (`build/handoff/Strip/meshes/`), with both manifests, and log the new ids. No code change. An unshaded glow on the core stays Faber's option on top, if the smooth core still reads as geometry in play.
- **Status:** made and self-reviewed (0 open edges, the audit, the sheet). Content check before upload: PASS by 21:18, on the sheet, the core's code and the manifest. Delivered to the handoff for Faber's install, then research's look at the capture. Not accepted.
- **Faber, 2026-11-26, 21:33, installed:** the five meshes uploaded under the group, with their ids in `docs/asset-log.md` and both kits rebuilt (`src/storage/BaseKit.rbxm`, `StripKit.rbxm`, read back in Studio). Base 37 of 37.
  - **Up close in play:** `build/captures/ar67/core_close_own_look.jpg` shows the core in its own painted look, from a white-hot middle to a cyan rim, smooth, with no flats. Beside it, at the same camera, `core_close_neon.jpg` shows the unshaded Neon fallback, which flattens the core into one cyan disc.
  - **The mesh meets the must-fix by itself,** so the Neon stays out of the game. That's for research's look.

## AR68. The UI's complete overhaul: three directions (research's brief, Modulo's targets, the director's pick)

Research, 2026-11-27, 02:40.
- **The director's words** (the register, 2026-11-27): "this whole Ui modulo made is horrendous it just the design.", "it need a complete overhaul", "it just not mature nor high quality nor original", and "i dont accept anything ui related everything need a overhaul including the galaxy breaker".
- **Covers:** every screen. AR62's designs stop (AR62, "Stopped").
- **The study:** `docs/research/ui-design.md`.

**Feel line (the fight):** the player glances at a quiet frame round a loud fight. The lead's life and the one press read at a glance, and the rest of the screen is the fighters'.

**The three tests (the director's):** mature, high quality, original. A target that fails one never reaches the director.

**Common to all three directions** (the study's principles 1 and 3 to 11):
- **Layout:**
  - The centre 60% of the width belongs to the fighters, top to bottom.
  - The permanent fight HUD, the press aside, covers about 6% of the screen's area at most. Today's two cards alone cover about 8%.
- **The lead:** top left, compact. The portrait art without a box, the name and level in the working face, a thin health bar (red: the director's 11-13 word on health) and the charge.
- **The foe:** small, over its head in the world or a slim tag at the top centre; each direction picks.
- **The press:** the one big thing, at the left thumb (TR52). It wakes when ready and is quiet while charging. Auto and the eye are small beside it.
- **Function stays:** `docs/ui.md`, "The HUD, in the world" (what each piece shows and when).
- **Type:** one working face from Roblox's library for names and numbers, with display type only for titles and the skill's moment. No stroked bubbly face. Text is 14 px or more on the smallest phone.
- **Colour:**
  - One identity hue on neutrals, never pure black or white, never full saturation.
  - Health's red is a state colour. Rarity hues stay on collection screens.
  - Not purple, black and somber (said six times).
- **Lines:** thin (1 to 2 px at phone size), flat or cut shapes. No pills and no fat strokes.
- **Motion:** each move says what changed, then settles. Calm on Home, loud only at the skill.

**The directions,** each answering "whose device or voice is this UI, in our fiction?":
- **A, Console (a device):** the UI is the player's own yard system, the one that runs the bays and the Breaker.
  - **Motif:** the gauge. Tick scales, readouts with their units, and one live line along the frame that links the HUD's pieces. A pulse runs along it when something changes.
  - **Hue:** Power's amber on warm, translucent graphite.
  - **Type:** a technical working face for the readouts.
  - **Home:** the console's main page, the lead beside the yard's readings.
  - **Not:** real electrical gear (the base's rule), or a cold sci-fi blue.
- **B, Shard (a material):** the UI is seen in the shards the Voidkin come from.
  - **Motif:** thin facets with one bright edge where the light catches, and cuts at a shard's break angles. Pale frosted glass over the world: light, not dark.
  - **Hue:** the shard's clear ice light. A Voidkin's own hue shows only in its portrait's facet.
  - **Type:** a light geometric working face, and a wide display face for titles.
  - **Motion:** a glint crosses a facet once when it changes.
  - **Home:** the lead in a large facet window.
- **C, Episode (a voice):** the fight is the lead's own anime, and the UI is the show's graphics.
  - **Motif:** ink lines with brush ends and a fine screentone, on paper-white and ink-grey (never pure). One hot hue that isn't red (a cobalt or a signal orange).
  - **Type:** names set like a show's credits, widely tracked.
  - **Motion:** nearly silent in the fight. A title card cuts in at the skill, the climax, then it's still again.
  - **Home:** an episode's title card, the lead's art large.

**The targets** (AGENTS.md, "A target before the hours"; three hours at most for all three):
- For each direction:
  - the fight HUD painted over a phone capture of a real fight (`build/scratch/faber/f2cap/fight_ready.jpg` or a fresher one), at the smallest phone and at 852;
  - Home painted over a phone capture;
  - one token strip: the hue and neutrals, the type with its sizes, the line weights, the motif, and one line on its motion.
- At final quality, with the game's real data (Rook, the Brawler, his numbers). Our own renders of Rook stand in for key art, and are named as stand-ins.
- Each made with whatever technique serves the direction: painted images, vector, or both.

**The pick:**
1. Research checks each target against the three tests, the study's principles and the phone's 14 px, and sends back any that fail.
2. Gemini gives a cold read of the three when its program allows.
3. The three go to the director side by side, with today's game and a shipped frame. Research brings no recommendation (the director, 11-27).
4. After the pick, the chosen direction is built screen by screen, the fight HUD first.

**How the review judges** (research's tier 1, from the Turing document's quality standard, 18A, at the director's word of 02:10 to weigh it):
- **The dimensions, named before the targets arrive:** the three tests; reading the lead's life and the press at a glance on the smallest phone; the share of the screen left to the fight.
- **Each dimension gets one of four answers** against today's game: BETTER, UNCHANGED, WORSE or NOT VERIFIED. They're never averaged into a score.
- **Motion counts:** a prettier still that fails in motion is WORSE.
- **Gemini reads blind:** it gets the three directions and today's game unlabelled, with no lean from research.
- **The build follows the interaction rules** of the Turing document's appendix B, 23:
  - five micro-states (rest, focus, pressed, pending, resolved), with disabled distinct from cooling;
  - a cooldown that keeps its number readable;
  - a touch area that never moves;
  - plain verbs.


### Modulo: the targets (2026-11-27, 03:03)

- **Made** with `tools/ui/overhaul.py`, in `build/handoff/UI/ar68/`:
  - **Per direction:** the fight at 852 (the press full) and on the smallest phone (568, the press filling, its share shown); Home over the base; a token strip.
  - **Also:** C's skill card (`skill_C.png`); `sheet_fight.png` (today's game and the three on the smallest phone); `sheet_home.png`.
  - **How:** vector over our own captures, in Roblox's library fonts, with the game's real numbers. Rook's portrait render stands in for key art and is labelled as a stand-in.
- **A, Console:**
  - No boxes. The readings sit on a warm graphite haze, and the bust is cut out.
  - The live line runs along the frame from the eye, past the lead, down to the press, with a pulse on it.
  - Brackets are its mark: it locks the foe in the world and prints the foe's reading over the lock.
  - The press is a dial whose ticks fill.
  - Type: Builder Mono, with Builder Extended for titles. Amber marks only the console's own state.
- **B, Shard:**
  - Pale facets that shift the world behind them (a refraction), with one lit edge.
  - Rook's red shows only in his portrait's facet. The foe's facet sits over its head.
  - The press is a shard whose edge lights as it fills.
  - Type: Montserrat, with Michroma for titles.
- **C, Episode:**
  - Paper laid in one brush stroke with dry bristled ends, over a screentone. Names are tracked like credits.
  - A caption on a brush leader names the foe.
  - The press is a brushed ring, with cobalt filling round it.
  - At the skill, a title card cuts across the fight. Type: Source Sans Pro, with Merriweather for the title.
- **The press names the skill when it wakes** (the director, by 01:00: "or just the name"). While it fills, it shows its share (B 23: the number stays readable).
- **Measured:**
  - **The permanent fight HUD, the press aside, at 852:** A 4.6%, B 5.3% and C 5.1% of the screen.
  - **At 568:** 10.4%, 11.9% and 11.5%, because the same points hold 14 pt text.
  - **Smallest word:** 14 pt in every frame.
- **Self-reviewed** beside today's game and a shipped match frame (internal, kept in scratch). What these targets can't show, and the finish needs:
  - A: the glass's depth and the pulse in motion.
  - B: real refraction and glass edges.
  - C: a drawn brush texture and the card's cut.
  - **Not the look:** Home's blocky bust is the stand-in.
  - **Today's capture** comes from a wider window, so its HUD reads smaller than it would on the smallest phone.
- **Content check:** PASS (the subagent, all 15 images and the script; any upload needs its own check with a 64 px sheet).
- **Delivered** to research at 03:03. **Not accepted.**

### Research's check (2026-11-27, 03:07): back to Modulo, one round

- **Felt (the stills):** all three leave the fight far more room than today. Every frame still reads as a prototype, though: a grey baseplate, Studio's default avatar, today's stroked damage numbers at the centre, and a blocky Rook render as hero art.
- **Must-fixes for all three** (each one is a class):
  1. **The frame:** a real fight in our world, with today's game captured at the same size in the same fight. My brief pointed at the baseplate capture, so this miss is research's. Faber captures it.
  2. **Every piece of UI in the frame follows its direction.** That includes the damage numbers, in the direction's working face. They keep their function (`docs/ui.md`), and the critical number keeps the director's gold (11-19).
  3. **Functions in `docs/ui.md`'s table that all three leave out:**
     - the foe's difficulty skull, coloured by CR (D, 11-19), and the type-edge arrow;
     - the lead's type gem, class icon and experience bar.
  4. **No Rook render as hero art.** The director, 11-26, 20:46: "skip that sword and rook just make an art".
     - Home and C's title card get a labelled art frame in its place.
     - The press carries the skill's own art (a labelled slot is fine). It isn't the lead's face a second time, and the name isn't its main read (the director, 01:39: "maybe it need to be an art"). My brief missed that word, so this miss is research's too.
  5. **Each direction's motif, finished, in the still,** since the pick rests on it (Modulo's own note says none shows yet):
     - A: the glass and the live line;
     - B: facets with break angles and one lit edge;
     - C: a drawn brush.
- **By direction:**
  - A's haze reads as a grey smear on the sky, and its long amber L as an editor's selection box.
  - B: nothing says shard yet. Its plates are skewed pale rectangles and its press is a hexagon.
  - C's paper melts into the white sky, and its vector spikes read as a comb, not a brush.
- **The brief's 6%:** it holds at 852. On the smallest phone, 14 px text sets about 10 to 12%, which matches what was measured.
- **Against today's game:**
  - share of the screen at 852: BETTER for all three;
  - share at 568, and the glance read: NOT VERIFIED until today's frame is at the same size;
  - the three tests and motion: NOT VERIFIED. They go to Gemini's blind read, then the director.
- **Open for the blind read:** A's corner brackets, a common sci-fi lock.
